**Gran Hazel**: Should I have gone after Corvin? That night, long ago?  

**Choices**

- **You were right to wait. He had to come back himself.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Maybe. You can’t carry anyone home.
    - **Gran Hazel**: They have to walk back on their own.
    - **Gran Hazel**: That was the one I wanted. Thank you, love.
- **Somebody should have gone after him.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Yes. Some nights I think so too.
    - **Gran Hazel**: I’ll never know. That’s all right.
    - **Gran Hazel**: That was the one I wanted. Thank you, love.

#### `s10-night`

_You walk Gran home. She stops at the door._  
**Gran Hazel**: Tomorrow, everyone comes. You’ll tell them about the Grove.  
**Gran Hazel**: Then I’ll go up the hill. No fuss.  
_Going up the hill is Gran’s way of saying she will die._  
**Gran Hazel**: Being ordinary has been lovely.  
_Inside, Gran sits on her bed for a long time._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): telling a grown-up when we feel sad  
> _If you feel sad, you can tell a grown-up._  
> _A hug and a cuddle can help._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that when they feel sad they can tell a grown-up and get a hug._


#### `s10-foot-msc`

_The bottom of the hill path. The grass is long here._  
_Gran looks back at the Grove. The pond, the gate, the posts._  
**Gran Hazel**: It looks like somebody lives here now.  
**Gran Hazel**: I can walk up on my own. But I’d rather not.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): asking for company when something is hard  
> _Gran could walk alone. But she asks for a friend._  
> _When something is hard, it’s good to ask for help._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that it is good to ask for company and help when something is hard._

**Gran Hazel**: Who’s coming?  

**Choices**

- **Just me, Gran.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Good. The quiet way.
- **Moss. He’s already decided.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Of course he has! He decided about me long ago.
- **Sable.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: The grey one? Yes. I’d like that very much.
- **Corvin. Let him carry the lantern.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Corvin. Yes. He used to carry it for me.

#### `s10-letter`

_Morning. Gran Hazel has died._  
_That means her body stopped working. It won’t start again._  
_Under the signpost stone is a letter from Gran._  
**Gran Hazel**: “Don’t make a big fuss. Well, maybe a little fuss.”  
**Gran Hazel**: “You kept my three rules. Here is one more.”  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): add 1: three and one more make four  
> _Three rules, and one more. How many rules now?_  
> - ✅ Four → Yes. Three and one more make four. (Count them on your fingers: one, two, three… four!)
> - Three → Three, and then one more. Let’s count up: one, two, three… four!
> - Five → Let’s count: one, two, three rules. One more makes four.
> _For grown-ups: Added one more to three to make four._

**Gran Hazel**: “Show up. Every morning. Same sticky gate.”  
**Gran Hazel**: “I love you. I always will. Love, Gran.”  
_Behind you, the gate creaks. Fern. Sparks. Everyone._  
_They hug you. Nobody needs to say anything._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): feeling sad when someone dies, and remembering together  
> _When someone dies, we feel sad. Crying is all right._  
> _We remember them together. That helps._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that sadness and crying are natural when someone dies, and that remembering together helps._


## S11. The Blight

- `s11-step1`: Go next door. Look at Fern’s poorly plum trees. · _Go through the gap in the hedge. The poorly trees are at the bottom of the hill._
- `s11-step2`: Look at every tree with Byte’s magnifying glass. · _Look at a leaf, the bark and the fruit. Then pick a tag. You can always look again._
- `s11-step3`: Catch one of the tiny bugs on the leaves. · _Keep the sun in front of you, so your shadow stays behind you. Don’t look right at the sun._
- `s11-step4`: Help Clover let the ladybirds go in the hedge. · _Open the jar by the old dead tree. Then step back and keep still._

#### `s11-fern`

_Fern is at your gate. It is very early._  
_She holds out a plum. It has black spots._  
**Fern**: Don’t touch it. Just look.  
_She tips her hand. Squish! The plum goes soft and dusty._  
**Fern**: Three of my trees look like this. They were fine yesterday.  
**Fern**: Hazel helped me plant these trees. Years ago.  
**Fern**: I haven’t told anyone. I’m too worried.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): telling someone when you are worried  
> _Fern is worried. What could help her?_  
> - ✅ Tell a friend about it → You’re right. Telling someone helps. And I just told you!
> - Keep it a secret → Secrets make worries feel bigger. Telling you helps already.
> - Pretend it isn’t there → I tried that. The plums are still spotty! Telling helps more.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about what helps a worried friend: telling someone they trust._


**Choices**

- **Go and look now.**
    - You go through the hedge. Fern follows, slowly.
- **Give her a hug first.**
    - **Fern**: Oh! Thank you. That helps a bit.
    - Then she marches off through the hedge.
- **Say you’ll fetch Byte.**
    - **Fern**: Byte! Yes. Byte loves a puzzle.

#### `s11-ember`

_Three trees at the bottom are poorly._  
_Their leaves are curled up and black._  
_Ember is at the wall. Her arms are folded tight._  
**Ember**: Burn them all! Tonight! Before it spreads!  
**Fern**: All of them? There are forty trees!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> **Clover**: Lots of trees are green and well. Only three are poorly.  
> **Clover**: Are there more well trees, or more poorly trees?  
> - ✅ More well trees → Yes! Lots and lots of well trees. Only three poorly ones.
> - More poorly trees → Let’s look again. Only three are poorly. Lots more are well!
> _For grown-ups: Compared two groups and saw that the big group of healthy trees had more._

**Clover**: Wait. We don’t know what it is yet.  
**Ember**: I waited once, and I lost my Ash.  
_Fern sits down on a box. She is very quiet._  

#### `s11-lens`

_Byte runs up. He has a magnifying glass on a string._  
**Byte**: Nobody burns anything till we look properly.  
**Ember**: You have till sunset. Then I’m doing it.  
**Byte**: This glass makes little things look big.  
_Byte ties a tag on each tree. NOT FINE. FINE. PROBABLY FINE._  
**Byte**: Look at the leaf, the bark and the fruit.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): parts of a tree: leaf, bark, fruit  
> _(Point to a leaf. Now the bark. Now a plum.)_  
> **Byte**: Leaf, bark, fruit. A plum tree has all three.  
> Check: `edu:point:tree-parts:leaf-bark-fruit`
> _For grown-ups: Named three parts of a fruit tree: leaf, bark and fruit._

**Byte**: And don’t lick anything! I have to say that.  
**Clover**: This branch looks lonely. Can I give it a bandage?  
**Byte**: It isn’t hurt. It’s a branch.  

#### `s11-map`

_By the afternoon, every tree has a tag._  
_Three trees say NOT FINE. They are all in a row._  
**Byte**: Look! The poorly trees are all where the wind blows.  
**Byte**: Something came on the wind. Something tiny.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): wind can carry light things  
> _(Hold up a tissue or a leaf. Blow gently: whoosh!)_  
> **Byte**: See? Moving air pushes light things. Wind can carry tiny bugs too.  
> Check: `edu:blow:wind:tissue`
> _For grown-ups: Blew on something light to see how wind can carry small, light things._

**You**: What is in the wind?  
**Byte**: Good question. I have a jar!  

#### `s11-jar`

_Byte gives you a jam jar with a cloth lid._  
_Clover gives you another one. Just in case._  
_You crouch in the long grass. The sun is warm._  
_A tiny grey bug lands on a leaf._  
_Your shadow touches the leaf. Plop! The bug drops away._  
**Clover**: Shy things hide from shadows. Keep the sun in front of you.  
**Clover**: Then your shadow stays behind you. Never look right at the sun!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, do): keeping still and calm; patience  
> **Clover**: Let’s be still as a stone. Count to five, slowly.  
> _(Count slowly together: one… two… three… four… five.)_  
> **Clover**: Still and slow. That’s how you catch shy things.  
> Check: `edu:keep:still:count-5`
> _For grown-ups: Practised keeping their body still and calm while counting slowly to five._

**Ember**: Or we could just burn it all.  
_But Ember stays. She watches every try._  

#### `s11-beetle`

_Got it! A tiny grey beetle in the jar._  
_It is no bigger than a lentil._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): insects have six legs  
> _(Look closely. Count the beetle’s legs together.)_  
> **Byte**: One, two, three, four, five, six. Six legs!  
> **Byte**: Beetles are insects. Every insect has six legs.  
> Check: `edu:count:legs:6`
> _For grown-ups: Counted a beetle’s legs and learned that all insects have six legs._

**Byte**: It’s covered in black dust. That’s the poorly stuff!  
**Byte**: The beetle isn’t the sickness. It’s the postman. It carries it.  
_Byte follows the wind. He finds an old dead tree, full of beetles._  
**Byte**: Only the trees near this one get poorly. The rest are safe!  
**Clover**: I have ladybirds at home. Ladybirds eat beetle eggs!  
**Clover**: Look! One tree in the poorly row is fine.  
**Fern**: That’s Grudge. Hazel’s old tree. It has the sourest plums.  
_Byte puts a new tag on the jar. It says: GUILTY._  

#### `s11-dusk`

_The sun goes down. Bats fly over the stream._  
_A light comes through the hedge. It’s Ember, with a burning torch._  
**Ember**: I’m sorry, Fern. I can’t wait.  
**Ember**: I waited once, for Ash. I waited too long.  
_Ember’s face looks cross. But inside, she is scared._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): naming feelings: scared can look like cross  
> _Ember looks cross. What is she really feeling?_  
> - ✅ Scared → Yes. Ember is scared of losing the trees. Scared can look cross.
> - Happy → Look at her face. Not happy. She’s scared. Scared can look cross.
> - Silly → Not silly. She’s scared. Sometimes scared looks cross.
> _For grown-ups: Noticed that a person who looks cross may really be feeling scared._

_Byte holds the map. Clover holds Fern’s hand._  
_Everybody looks at you._  

**Choices**

- **Show her Byte’s map, kindly.**
    - You hold out the map. Ember reads it by torchlight.
    - **Ember**: Three trees. Not forty. Just three.
    - She dips the torch in the water butt. Hiss! It goes out.
    - **Ember**: Show me the old tree. I’ll help.
- **Ask the grown-ups to burn only the poorly trees, safely.**
    - Ember nods. Fern helps her. They go very carefully.
    - They burn only the three poorly trees and the old dead tree.
    - You stay far back with Clover. Fire is for grown-ups.
    - **Ember**: Not on my own this time. Not in a rush.
    - Fern brings out tea. The fire gets small, then goes out.
- _(left out in this band; plays as `map`)_

#### `s11-ladybirds`

_Clover opens her jar._  
_Out come ladybirds! Hundreds of them, red as berries._  
**Clover**: Ladybirds eat tiny bugs that hurt plants. Gardeners love them.  
**Clover**: Off you go, ladybirds! Gobble up the beetle eggs!  
_One lands on your thumb. Then three more._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 7  
> _(Look at the ladybird on your thumb. Count its spots together.)_  
> **Clover**: One, two, three, four, five, six, seven. Seven spots!  
> **Clover**: This kind is called a seven-spot ladybird.  
> Check: `edu:count:spots:7`
> _For grown-ups: Counted seven spots and met the seven-spot ladybird, a gardener’s friend._

**Byte**: I tried to tag them all. They’re all called LADYBIRD now.  

#### `s11-complete`

_A week later, the orchard is quiet and green._  
_Three old stumps. Lots and lots of happy trees._  
_In the middle stands Grudge, full of sour purple plums._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.1.4, do): taste words: sour and sweet  
> _(Make a sour face together! Now a sweet, happy face.)_  
> **Fern**: Sour like a lemon. Sweet like a strawberry. Grudge is sour!  
> Check: `edu:make:face:sour`
> _For grown-ups: Acted out the taste words “sour” and “sweet”._

**Fern**: The beetles don’t like Grudge. Too bitter for them!  
**Fern**: This was Hazel’s. A little knife for joining trees.  
**Fern**: It’s sharp. We’ll use it together, you and me.  

> **Lesson** (health, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): staying safe with sharp things  
> **Fern**: Who should help when we use something sharp?  
> - ✅ A grown-up → Yes. Sharp things need a grown-up. Then everyone stays safe.
> - Nobody → Ouch, no! Sharp things can cut. A grown-up helps every time.
> - A Nubbin → Ha! Nubbins have no hands for knives. A grown-up helps.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that a grown-up always helps when something sharp is used._

**Fern**: You saved my orchard. You’re the Orchard Warden now!  
_Byte had put a tag on Ember. It said CROSS._  
_He crosses it out. He writes: SCARED. And then: FAIR._  
**Ember**: …Fair.  

## S15. Lucky Eggs

- `s15-step1`: Help Byte count what comes out of the Mystery Eggs. · _Stand by the wagon. Each time an egg opens, tap PLAIN or SPARKLY._
- `s15-step2`: Tiptoe to the wagon and talk to Jackpot the parrot. · _Dashwood is having a nap. Go along the stream stones. Don’t touch the wagon, or the bells ring!_
- `s15-step3`: Shine a light through the special egg to see inside. · _Go somewhere dim, like under the bridge. Hold the egg in front of the lantern._
- `s15-step4`: Make an honest egg stall with Byte. · _Put the stall by the bridge or by your gate. Byte writes the true numbers on the board._

#### `s15-wagon`

_A wagon rolls down the lane. Ting-a-ling! Bells on the roof!_  
_A man in a yellow waistcoat sits on top. That’s Dashwood Prance._  
_A brass parrot sits on his shoulder. Tick, tick, tick._  
**Dashwood Prance**: Mystery Eggs! Every egg a winner!  
**Jackpot**: MOST EGGS ARE PLAIN.  
**Dashwood Prance**: Thank you, Jackpot.  
_A big sign says: ONE IN THREE IS SPARKLY!_  
**The children**: Sparkly! Sparkly! Sparkly!  
**Pip**: I saved my Shells! I can buy four eggs!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): hearing rhymes  
> **Pip**: Egg, egg, I love an egg!  
> **Pip**: What rhymes with egg?  
> - ✅ leg → Yes! Egg, leg. They rhyme!
> - bell → Bell rhymes with shell! Egg rhymes with leg.
> - wagon → Egg… wagon. No! Egg rhymes with leg.
> _For grown-ups: Listened for a rhyme: egg and leg._

**Byte**: Is that true? Let’s count every egg and see.  

**Choices**

- **Buy an egg yourself. Let’s see!**
    - **Dashwood Prance**: A Keeper! Choose with your heart!
    - You choose one. It’s plain. Jackpot says nothing, very loudly.
    - **Byte**: That’s one egg. Let’s keep counting.
- **Ask Pip to wait before she spends it all.**
    - **Pip**: Wait? But the sparkly ones might run out!
    - **Jackpot**: THEY WILL NOT RUN OUT.
- **Ask Dashwood how he knows.**
    - **Dashwood Prance**: I know it in my heart, friend!
    - **Byte**: Numbers don’t live in hearts. They live in counting.

#### `s15-tally`

_By lunchtime, Byte’s card is full._  
**Byte**: Lots and lots of plain eggs. Only three sparkly ones.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> **Byte**: Plain eggs or sparkly eggs: which are there more of?  
> - ✅ Plain eggs → Yes! Many more plain eggs than sparkly ones.
> - Sparkly eggs → Look again. Only three sparkly. Lots and lots of plain. More plain!
> _For grown-ups: Compared two groups and saw there were more plain eggs than sparkly ones._

**Byte**: That’s not one in three. The sign is wrong.  
_Pip sits on the bridge with four plain eggs._  
_She is trying hard to be brave._  
**Pip**: The plain ones are nice too. They’re warm.  

#### `s15-sneak`

**Byte**: Jackpot always tells the truth. Let’s ask him.  
_After lunch, Dashwood has a nap. Snore… snore…_  
_Jackpot sits in the window. Tick, tick, tick._  
**Byte**: Don’t touch the wagon. All the bells will ring!  
_There are stepping stones by the stream._  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): moving slowly and quietly  
> _(Let’s tiptoe! Five tiny, quiet steps together.)_  
> _(Count them: one, two, three, four, five.)_  
> **Byte**: Quiet feet! Like a cat.  
> Check: `edu:tiptoe:steps:5`
> _For grown-ups: Controlled their body to take five slow, quiet tiptoe steps._

_One stone wobbles. One stone is a frog! Ribbit!_  

#### `s15-jackpot`

**Jackpot**: HELLO. I CANNOT LIE. IT IS MY SPRINGS.  
**You**: How many eggs are sparkly, Jackpot?  
**Jackpot**: ONE IN TEN. HE KNOWS. HE WROTE IT DOWN.  
**Byte**: And the big sign?  
**Jackpot**: HE SAYS THE SIGN IS A POEM. A POEM IS NOT A NUMBER.  
**Jackpot**: ALSO THE SPECIAL EGG IS NOT SPECIAL. OOPS.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): honesty: telling the truth is fair  
> **Byte**: The sign says something that isn’t true. Is that fair?  
> - ✅ No, it isn’t fair → Right. The truth is fair. Then people can choose.
> - Yes, it’s fine → Hmm. Pip spent her Shells because of that sign. Not fair to Pip.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about why telling the truth is fair to other people._

_The snoring stops. You freeze! Then it starts again._  

#### `s15-legendary`

_Dashwood wakes up. He shines the special egg._  
**Dashwood Prance**: Lovely, isn’t she? Hold her for one Shell a minute!  
**Byte**: Here are three Shells. Three minutes, please.  
_Under the bridge, it is dim and green._  
_Byte lights a little lantern._  
**Byte**: Light behind an egg shows what’s inside. Hazel taught us.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): light can shine through thin things  
> _(With a grown-up, shine a torch through your fingers.)_  
> _(Look! Your fingers glow pink. Light shines through thin things.)_  
> **Byte**: Eggshells are thin too. Let’s look!  
> Check: `edu:shine:light:fingers`
> _For grown-ups: Shone a torch through their fingers to see that light passes through thin things._


#### `s15-painted`

_The light shines through the egg. Glitter sparkles everywhere._  
_But the egg inside is plain pink. Something little is moving!_  
**Byte**: It’s a plain egg with paint on! There’s a big thumbprint.  
**Byte**: It’s a healthy egg, though. It will hatch in a week.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): a week is seven days; show 7  
> _(A week is seven days. Hold up seven fingers together.)_  
> **Byte**: Seven days. Then, crack! A baby Nubbin.  
> Check: `edu:show:fingers:7`
> _For grown-ups: Learned that a week is seven days and showed seven on their fingers._

_Glitter gets on your fingers. It stays for days!_  

#### `s15-verdict`

_Dashwood sees the glitter on your hands._  
_He sits down on the wagon step._  
**Dashwood Prance**: When I was little, I bought a golden egg.  
**Dashwood Prance**: I carried it home in my hat. I loved it.  
**Dashwood Prance**: It hatched brown. It had been painted.  
**Dashwood Prance**: But I loved that brown baby anyway. So much.  
**Jackpot**: HE MEANS IT. I CANNOT LIE.  
_The children wait. Pip holds her four plain eggs._  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, ask): fairness: putting things right, kindly  
> **Byte**: The sign wasn’t true. How can we make it fair?  
> - ✅ Tell the truth, kindly → Yes. Kind and true. That fixes things best.
> - Shout at Dashwood → Shouting makes people sad and cross. Kind and true works better.
> - Do nothing → Then Pip loses her Shells. Let’s be kind and true.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about how to make an unfair thing fair by being truthful and kind._


**Choices**

- **Show everyone the count and the painted egg.**
    - You hold up the painted egg. Byte reads the count.
    - **The children**: One in TEN? That’s not fair!
    - **Dashwood Prance**: It was only a poem. I’m sorry.
    - Dashwood drives away before dark.
    - He leaves a table, a box of eggs, and Jackpot.
    - **Jackpot**: I DID NOT WANT TO GO. I CANNOT LIE.
- **Ask him to give the Shells back and fix the sign.**
    - **Dashwood Prance**: Give them back? Oh… all right.
    - He counts the Shells back into small hands. One by one.
    - He changes the sign. Now it says ONE IN TEN.
    - **Pip**: I’m keeping one egg. I don’t mind if it’s plain!
    - Jackpot stays with you. Everybody agrees.
- **Buy his wagon. Run an honest stall yourself.**
    - **Dashwood Prance**: Buy Marigold? My wagon? Hmm. How much?
    - Byte does the sums. Byte loves doing sums.
    - **Dashwood Prance**: Polish her once a year. And keep her away from goats!
    - He walks off down the lane, whistling.

#### `s15-odds-board`

_Byte puts up a board. He writes in big chalk letters._  
_PLAIN: 9 IN 10. SPARKLY: 1 IN 10._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, do): 9 and 1 make 10  
> _(Hold up ten fingers. Fold one down for the sparkly egg.)_  
> **Byte**: How many are still up? Nine! Nine plain, one sparkly. Ten eggs.  
> Check: `edu:show:fingers:9-and-1`
> _For grown-ups: Used ten fingers to see that 9 and 1 make 10._

_Underneath, smaller: ALL OF THEM ARE WARM._  
**Pip**: Why do I like this board better?  
**Byte**: Because it’s true. True feels good.  
_Some children buy eggs. Some just come to read the board._  

#### `s15-complete`

_The last egg is wrapped in a red spotty hanky._  
_You unwrap it. It glows all over, like a pearl!_  
**Jackpot**: THAT ONE IS REAL. I CHECKED. TWICE.  
_A card says: For the Keeper. This one really is a winner._  
_Jackpot hops onto your shoulder. Tick, tick._  
**Jackpot**: I WOULD LIKE TO LIVE WITH YOU. I CANNOT LIE.  
_Dashwood gave Pip the painted egg as he left._  
_Pip takes the painted egg home in her hat._  
_A week later, it hatches. It is brown as a boot._  

> **Lesson** (art, do): naming and finding a colour: brown  
> _(Can you find something brown near you? Point to it!)_  
> _Brown like a boot. Brown like Pip’s new Nubbin._  
> Check: `edu:find:colour:brown`
> _For grown-ups: Named the colour brown and found something brown nearby._

_Pip loves it more than anything._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): loving friends just as they are  
> _Pip’s Nubbin isn’t sparkly. Pip doesn’t mind one bit._  
> _We love our friends just as they are._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that friends are loved for who they are, not for being fancy._


## S16. The Workyard

- `s16-step1`: Walk up the river with Sparks and Clover to the Workyard. · _Follow the river path. Listen for the saws: bzzz!_
- `s16-step2`: Watch the Workyard Nubbins quietly. What do they like to do? · _Hide behind the log piles. If a Nubbin sees you, it works to be polite. Just try again!_
- `s16-step3`: Make a Fair Roster. Each Nubbin picks the job it likes. · _Put each Nubbin’s card under the job it chose. Then hold Sparks’s ladder._
- `s16-step4`: Go back to the Workyard in a week. How is it going? · _Wait seven days. Then follow the river path. Brisk will have the kettle on._

#### `s16-sparks`

_Sparks arrives with wire looped over both shoulders._  
**Sparks**: Job up the river! A sawmill needs lights!  
**Sparks**: I need someone to hold the ladder and say “wow”.  
**Clover**: I’m not good with ladders. I’m good at not falling off.  
_You can hear the saws far away. Bzzz! Like wasps._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.1.4, do): sound words  
> _(Make the saw sound together: bzzzzz!)_  
> **Sparks**: Bzzz! That’s a sound word. Say it loud!  
> Check: `edu:say:sound-word:bzzz`
> _For grown-ups: Said a sound word (“bzzz”) that sounds like what it names._

**Sparks**: Brisk says his Nubbins love it there. He wrote it twice!  
**Clover**: Twice is a lot.  

#### `s16-yard`

_The Workyard! Sheds, logs and lots of busy Nubbins._  
_Each one wears a tiny yellow hard hat._  
_A sign says: WORK IS PLAY!_  
_Three Nubbins run inside a big wooden wheel. Round and round._  
**Brisk Hollins**: Welcome! I’m Brisk Hollins! My Nubbins love working!  
_A big Nubbin carries a log on his back. He hums. That’s Lug._  
**Brisk Hollins**: Lug’s my best worker. I can’t stop him!  
_Behind the logs sit five Nubbins. Their colours look faded._  
**Clover**: Those ones are tired. Not lazy. Tired.  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, ask): tired bodies need rest  
> **Clover**: What does a tired body need?  
> - ✅ Rest → Yes. Rest helps a tired body feel better.
> - More work → More work makes tired bodies more tired. Rest helps.
> - Run faster → Running makes us puff! A tired body needs rest.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that when our bodies are tired, they need rest._


#### `s16-shadow`

**Brisk Hollins**: Tired? Nonsense! Look at my chart!  
_His chart line goes up so high it runs off the paper!_  
**Clover**: That chart is about logs. I’m asking about Nubbins.  
**Sparks**: Brisk, what do they do when nobody’s watching?  
_Brisk opens his mouth. Then he shuts it. He doesn’t know!_  
**Brisk Hollins**: Good question! Go and find out!  
**Clover**: Hide where they can’t see you. Or they’ll work to be polite.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, do): position words: behind, beside, under  
> _(Where can you hide? Behind the log pile! Point behind you.)_  
> **Clover**: Behind the logs. Beside the shed. Under the bench. Good hiding places.  
> Check: `edu:point:position:behind`
> _For grown-ups: Used the position words behind, beside and under._


**Choices**

- **Watch Lug first.**
    - Lug sees you. He picks up a bigger log, to show off!
    - You’ll need to be sneakier.
- **Watch the tired ones first.**
    - **Clover**: Good. Start with the ones nobody’s counting.
- **Watch the big wheel first.**
    - **Sparks**: That wheel! It’s wired backwards, for a start.

#### `s16-notes`

_By sunset, your clipboard is full._  
_The choppers chop even on their breaks. They like the thunk!_  
_The wheel runners sneak out and hide under the bench._  
_The five tired ones just lie in the sun._  
_And Lug carries little planks out to the reeds._  
_You follow him. In the reeds is a tiny house!_  
_The five tired Nubbins are asleep inside._  
**Clover**: He built them a house!  
**Sparks**: Nobody asked him to. He just did.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): noticing kindness and how others feel  
> **Clover**: How do the tired Nubbins feel in their little house?  
> - ✅ Safe and cosy → Yes. Lug made them feel safe. That’s real kindness.
> - Cross → I don’t think so. They’re snoozing! They feel safe and cosy.
> - Scared → Look how they snuggle. They feel safe. Lug was kind.
> _For grown-ups: Noticed a kind act and thought about how it made others feel._


#### `s16-roster`

_You show Brisk the clipboard. And Lug’s little house._  
**Brisk Hollins**: I thought they loved it. They were always so busy.  
**Brisk Hollins**: Busy isn’t the same as happy, is it?  
_He takes down WORK IS PLAY. He puts up a new sign._  
_It says: WORK IS… OFTEN PLAY!_  
**Sparks**: That’s worse.  
**Brisk Hollins**: I know. I’m working on it.  
**Clover**: Each Nubbin gets a card. They pick the job they like.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.B.3, do): sorting into groups  
> _(Help sort the cards! Choppers here. Stackers there. Resters over here.)_  
> **Clover**: Every Nubbin in its own group. Now let’s count each group!  
> Check: `edu:sort:cards:jobs`
> _For grown-ups: Sorted job cards into groups and counted each group._

**Sparks**: And the big wheel comes off! The water wheel can work the lights.  

#### `s16-bell`

_The Fair Roster hangs by the gate. A card for every Nubbin._  
_Next to it hangs a little bell. Low down, so everyone can reach._  
_Clover paints on it: NO THANK YOU._  
_The big wheel stops. A Nubbin is asleep inside it._  
_A wheel runner walks to the bell. It rings it, softly. Ding._  

> **Lesson** (music, do): soft and loud sounds  
> _(Ring a pretend bell softly: ding. Now loudly: DING!)_  
> **Clover**: Soft and loud. A soft ding says “No, thank you” nicely.  
> Check: `edu:ring:bell:soft-loud`
> _For grown-ups: Made a pretend bell sound softly and then loudly._

_Then it sits in the sun. Nobody stops it!_  
**Brisk Hollins**: Well I never! Fewer logs! Happier Nubbins!  

#### `s16-question`

_Brisk walks you to the river path._  
**Brisk Hollins**: Can I ask you something, Keeper?  
**Brisk Hollins**: Your Nubbins fetch you apples. Do you ask them first?  
_You think about your Nubbins. They bring you apples. They look happy._  
**You**: I don’t know.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): asking first; letting others say yes or no  
> **Brisk Hollins**: Before a friend helps you, what can you say?  
> - ✅ “Would you like to help?” → Yes! Ask first. Then they can say yes, or no thank you.
> - “Do it now!” → That’s a bit bossy, isn’t it? Asking first is kinder.
> - Say nothing → Then they never get to choose. Ask first!
> _For grown-ups: Practised asking before someone helps, so they can choose yes or no._

**Brisk Hollins**: Me neither. Now, the five tired ones. What shall I do?  

**Choices**

- **Bring them to the Grove to rest.**
    - **Brisk Hollins**: Yes. That’s kind. Lug will want to visit.
    - Lug picks up their whole little house, with them inside!
- **Keep them here as the cheer-up team.**
    - **Brisk Hollins**: Cheer-up team! Their job is sitting in the sun, being lovely!
    - **Sparks**: Is that just a job with a nicer hat?
- **Let them go and live by the river.**
    - **Brisk Hollins**: By the river? Who’ll look after them?
    - **Clover**: They can look after themselves. Some Nubbins like being free.
    - Brisk looks up the river. He doesn’t say no.

#### `s16-week`

_A week later, the Workyard is louder. And slower._  
_Everybody stops for fruit, morning and afternoon._  
_Someone has hung up a hammock!_  
_The new sign says: ASK FIRST._  
**Brisk Hollins**: Fewer logs. But the lights stay on! The water wheel does it.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): moving water can push and turn things  
> **Sparks**: The river pushes the water wheel round and round.  
> **Sparks**: The turning wheel makes the lights work. Water power!  
> _For grown-ups: Heard how moving river water pushes a wheel round, and the wheel can power lights._

**Sparks**: I knew it. I said so. Loudly.  
_Lug walks past with a log, humming. He just likes it._  

#### `s16-complete`

**Brisk Hollins**: Here! Every Keeper needs a hard hat.  
_A yellow hard hat, just your size._  
_On the brim, in Lug’s pencil: KEEPER._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: k  
> **Sparks**: KEEPER! What sound does KEEPER start with?  
> - ✅ “k”, like kite → Yes! K-k-keeper. Like kite and kitten!
> - “s”, like sun → Listen: k-k-keeper. It starts with k, like kite!
> - “m”, like Moss → M is for Moss! Keeper starts with k-k-k.
> _For grown-ups: Heard the first sound “k” in the word “Keeper”._

**Brisk Hollins**: Try the Fair Roster at the Grove. Let them pick.  
_Back home, you hang the roster by the door._  
_By morning, three Nubbins have picked jobs._  
_Watering. Fetching apples. And sitting with Moss._  
**Sparks**: That last one isn’t a job!  
**Clover**: It is to Moss.  

## S12. The Hum

- `s12-step1`: Carry the little beetle home to the old wall. · _The old wall is on the north side of the Grove. Carry the teacup flat and steady._
- `s12-step2`: Put your ear to the old wall at dusk. Listen! · _Lie down by the bottom stone, near the lantern post. Keep very still and quiet._
- `s12-step3`: Sit with Solo in Luna’s garden until morning. · _Sit on the bench by the moon flowers after dark. Don’t get up._
- `s12-step4`: Follow the buzzing swarm with Pixel’s tuning fork. · _Tap the fork and turn slowly. It buzzes most when you face the swarm._

#### `s12-solo`

_Evening in Luna’s night garden. The moon flowers open._  
**Luna**: Keeper! Over here. I’ve got something in a teacup.  
_In the cup is a round bronze beetle. It’s fuzzy, like a peach._  
_It’s a Nubbin! Its wings buzz when it talks._  
**Luna**: It’s a Thrum. Lots of them live in the old wall.  
**Luna**: They hum together. They never come out alone.  
**Solo**: We are— no.  
**Solo**: I. I am. Hello. I think I am sorry.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, ask): the words “I” and “we”: one and lots  
> **Luna**: It’s just one beetle. Should it say “I” or “we”?  
> - ✅ “I” → Yes! One on your own says “I”. Lots together say “we”.
> - “We” → “We” is for lots together. One on its own says “I”.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that we say “I” about one person and “we” about a group._

**Luna**: It needs a name, I think.  

**Choices**

- **Call it Solo.**
    - **Solo**: Solo. That means just one. Yes. That’s me.
    - **Luna**: Good name.
- **Give it a name of your own.**
    - You say a name. The beetle says it three times, quietly.
    - **Solo**: Nobody ever said a thing just to me before!
- **Ask what it would like to be called.**
    - **Solo**: Like? I never liked anything on my own before.
    - **Solo**: I would like you to choose. That’s my first like!
    - You call it Solo. It fits.

#### `s12-wall`

_The old wall hums. Put your hand on it. Buzzzz!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-1, do): sounds are made by shaking (vibration)  
> _(Hum a long hmmm. Touch your throat. Can you feel the buzz?)_  
> _Sounds are made by things shaking very fast. That’s the buzz!_  
> Check: `edu:hum:feel:throat`
> _For grown-ups: Felt their throat buzz while humming: sounds are made by vibrations._

_It smells a little of honey._  
_Every crack has a tiny round door. Every door has a face._  
**The Thrums**: Hello, Keeper. Thank you for bringing the one home.  
**The Thrums**: The one is welcome. Wait. We are checking we all agree.  
_A long, buzzy pause._  
**The Thrums**: We agree! Welcome! Sorry. We are very many.  
_But Solo climbs up your sleeve instead. And stays there._  
**Solo**: In a minute. I’ll go in a minute.  

#### `s12-flat`

_Next morning, Pixel is at the wall. She has a tuning fork._  
**Pixel**: I tune my lantern clock to this wall. It always hums one note.  
_She taps the fork. It sings. The wall sings back, a bit lower._  

> **Lesson** (music, do): high and low notes  
> _(Sing a high note together: laaa! Now a low note: looo.)_  
> **Pixel**: High and low! The wall went a little bit lower.  
> Check: `edu:sing:notes:high-low`
> _For grown-ups: Sang a high note and a low note and heard the difference._

**Pixel**: It’s gone flat! Walls don’t do that!  
**The Thrums**: Sorry. It’s our Queen-Mother. She is getting quieter.  
**The Thrums**: Soon, half of us will fly away to a new home.  
**Pixel**: That’s the saddest thing a wall ever said to me.  
_In your sleeve, Solo has gone very still._  

#### `s12-queen`

_At dusk, you lie down with your ear on the bottom stone._  
_Deep inside, one slow note. Hmmmm. Like a big, sleepy bee._  
**The Queen-Mother**: Ah. The Keeper. And the little one in your sleeve.  
**The Queen-Mother**: I am the middle of the hum. It’s a bit lonely here.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): helping someone who feels lonely  
> _The Queen-Mother feels lonely. What helps when someone is lonely?_  
> - ✅ A friend to talk to → Yes. You’re here, talking to me. I feel less lonely already.
> - Being told to cheer up → That doesn’t help much. A friend who listens helps most.
> - Going away → Then I’d feel lonelier! A friend nearby helps.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about what helps a lonely friend: company and listening._

**The Queen-Mother**: Soon, half will go. The little one must choose.  
**The Queen-Mother**: Go, or stay. Don’t choose for it. Let it choose.  
_Solo makes a tiny sound. Like a question._  

#### `s12-dark`

_Solo won’t go into the wall. So you go to Luna’s garden._  
_It is night. The paths shine a little in the dark._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): day and night animals  
> **Luna**: Some animals wake up at night. Which one?  
> - ✅ An owl → Yes! Lots of owls wake up at night. So do moths and bats.
> - A butterfly → Butterflies rest at night. Owls, moths and bats wake up!
> - A hen → Hens go to bed at night! Owls, moths and bats wake up.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that some animals, like owls, moths and bats, are awake at night._

**Solo**: In the hum, I’m warm all over. Nothing is scary.  
**Solo**: Out here, the dark is big. I feel scared.  
**Solo**: But I want to see what happens next. Is that greedy?  
**Luna**: I garden at night. Some nights I’m a bit scared too.  
**Luna**: I come out anyway. Wanting to see isn’t greedy. It’s brave.  

#### `s12-swarm-goes`

_You wake up on the bench. The morning is warm and still._  
_Across the Grove, the old wall ROARS!_  
_Bronze Thrums pour out of every crack, sparkling in the sun._  
**The Thrums**: Goodbye! Some of us are leaving. We are deciding which!  
_When a bee family gets too big, some fly off. That’s a swarm._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): taking away leaves fewer  
> **Pixel**: Lots fly out. Now, are there more or fewer in the wall?  
> - ✅ Fewer → Yes! When some go away, there are fewer left.
> - More → They flew away, so there are fewer. Fewer left behind.
> _For grown-ups: Understood that when some of a group leave, fewer are left._

**The Thrums**: Sorry it’s slow. We’re trying to be quick and polite.  
**Solo**: I want to see where they go. Just see.  
**Pixel**: Come on. I’ve got my tuning fork!  

#### `s12-follow`

_Pixel gives you her tuning fork. PROPERTY OF PIXEL, it says._  
**Pixel**: Tap it on your knee. Hold it up. Turn slowly.  
**Pixel**: When it buzzes in your fingers, that’s them. Walk that way.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): our ears can tell where a sound comes from  
> _(Close your eyes. Your grown-up says “buzz” from somewhere.)_  
> _(Point to where the sound came from!)_  
> **Pixel**: Good ears! Ears can tell where a sound comes from.  
> Check: `edu:point:sound:where-from`
> _For grown-ups: Listened with eyes closed and pointed to where a sound came from._

**Pixel**: And don’t walk into the stream. People do.  
**Pixel**: Not me. Other people.  

#### `s12-settled`

_The fork leads you over the stream to Hazel’s crooked tree._  
_The swarm hangs from a branch, like a big buzzing pear._  
**Pixel**: It was never one note! It’s hundreds of tiny hums together!  
**The Thrums**: We are a new we. Solo, will you come in?  
**Solo**: Keeper. I don’t know what to do.  
**Solo**: Sit with me. No. Tell me. No. Both?  
_You sit down beside Solo. You wait for Solo to choose._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): making a hard choice slowly, with a friend  
> _Solo can’t decide. What helps when you can’t decide?_  
> - ✅ Take your time, with a friend → Slowly. With you here. Yes. That helps.
> - Hurry up and pick! → Hurrying makes my wings wobble. Slowly, with a friend, helps.
> - Let someone else pick → The Queen-Mother said I must choose. With a friend beside me.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about making a hard choice: slowly, with a friend beside you._


**Choices**

- **Solo picks the hum. You nod.**
    - You carry Solo back to the old wall.
    - **Solo**: Will you remember this for me?
    - Solo walks in through a little round door.
    - **Pixel**: Listen. The hum sounds just right again.
- **Solo picks a home of its own. You nod.**
    - You find a little hole in the gatepost. It looks at the pond.
    - **Solo**: Mine! I chose it!
    - Every evening, one tiny hum comes from the gatepost.
- **Solo asks the hum for a little room. You nod.**
    - **Solo**: Could Thrums go out, and come back in?
    - **The Thrums**: We will talk about it.
    - They talk until lunch. Pixel eats two sandwiches. Then yours.
    - **The Thrums**: We agree! Ones may go out, and come back in.
    - **Solo**: In for supper. Out for the stars. Yes!

#### `s12-complete`

_A week later, the Queen-Mother dies, in the night._  
_She was very, very old. Her body stopped working._  
_Her whole family hummed around her. She wasn’t alone._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): feeling sad when someone dies; remembering together  
> _It’s all right to feel sad when someone dies._  
> _Remembering them together helps._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that sadness after a death is normal, and that remembering together helps._

_In the morning, there’s a present on your doorstep._  
_A little clay hive. It hums a tune you know!_  
**The Thrums**: For the Keeper. We taught it to hum. We hope that’s not rude.  

> **Lesson** (music, do): speaking in chorus, at the same time  
> _(Say “hello” at the very same time as your grown-up. Ready?)_  
> _Together, like the Thrums! That’s called a chorus._  
> Check: `edu:say:chorus:hello`
> _For grown-ups: Said a word in chorus, at the same time as their grown-up._

_Beside it is a thimble of golden nectar. It smells of summer._  
**Pixel**: I’ve changed my lantern clock. It listens to the whole crowd now.  

## S17. Nightjar

- `s17-step1`: Look for clues around the nursery. · _Look at the straw, the roof and the top of the fence. Show Luna what you find._
- `s17-step2`: Creep quietly through Luna’s garden to the hiding place. · _Walk on the soft moss, not the crunchy gravel. Move when the wind blows the leaves._
- `s17-step3`: Climb up to Nightjar’s nest, slowly. · _Hold on where the ferns grow. Stop when she turns her head._
- `s17-step4`: Carry the eggs home, one at a time. · _Hold each egg in both hands. Walk, don’t run._
- `s17-step5`: Visit Nightjar at night, when the little egg hatches. · _Go up the cliff path after dark. Sit and wait._

#### `s17-missing`

_Autumn. Shiny conkers lie on the ground._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): signs of autumn: conkers and falling leaves  
> **Luna**: Conkers fall in autumn. What else falls off trees in autumn?  
> - ✅ Leaves → Yes! Leaves fall in autumn. Crunch, crunch!
> - Snowflakes → Snow comes later, in winter. In autumn, leaves fall. Crunch!
> _For grown-ups: Named signs of autumn: conkers and falling leaves._

_In the straw is an empty, warm dip. An egg is gone!_  
**Luna**: One egg a night. Always the warmest one.  
**Luna**: Never a crack. Whoever it is, they’re careful.  
**Nova**: Morning! I’m not stopping. I heard there’s a mystery.  
**Nova**: Badger.  
**Luna**: It’s not a badger.  
**Nova**: A tall badger.  
**Luna**: Let’s look for clues first. Gently.  

#### `s17-clues`

_On the fence: a big grey feather. As long as your arm!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, do): comparing lengths: longer, shorter, the same  
> _(Stretch out your arm. Is the feather longer, shorter, or the same?)_  
> **Luna**: The same! This feather is as long as your arm.  
> Check: `edu:compare:length:feather-arm`
> _For grown-ups: Compared two lengths using the words longer, shorter and the same._

_On the roof: tiny scratches. Something landed very gently._  
_The dip in the straw is still warm._  
**Luna**: That’s an owl feather. A really big owl.  
**Nova**: Two badgers in a coat, with an owl feather!  
**Luna**: Nova.  
**Nova**: Fine. Tonight, then. I’m not stopping. I’m just staying up.  

#### `s17-night`

_At night, Luna’s garden glows. The white flowers are open._  
**Luna**: We hide in here and watch the nursery.  
**Luna**: Stay on the soft moss. The gravel goes crunch!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.1.4, do): loud and quiet sound words  
> _(Say it with Luna: gravel goes CRUNCH. Moss goes… shhh.)_  
> **Luna**: Crunch is a loud word. Shhh is a quiet word.  
> Check: `edu:say:sound-words:crunch-shh`
> _For grown-ups: Said loud and quiet sound words: “crunch” and “shhh”._

**Luna**: If something lands, don’t stare. Owls don’t like it. Nor do I.  
**Nova**: Can I bring my list?  
**Luna**: In your head. Quietly.  

**Choices**

- **Follow Luna’s footsteps exactly.**
    - **Luna**: Good. Step on the thyme. It smells nice.
- **Find your own way.**
    - Crack! You step on an empty snail shell. Everyone freezes.
    - **Luna**: …Your own way is very loud.

#### `s17-reveal`

_Midnight. Something flies across the moon. Not a sound._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): owls fly quietly on soft feathers  
> **Luna**: Owls have soft, fluffy feathers.  
> **Luna**: That makes their flying very, very quiet.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that owls’ soft feathers let them fly almost silently._

_A huge grey owl lands on the nursery roof._  
_She lifts an egg very gently in her feet._  
_She turns her head nearly all the way round. She looks at you._  
**Nightjar**: Hoo-oo.  
_Then she flies off to the cliffs, holding the egg._  
**Nova**: …I’m crossing off the badgers.  
**Luna**: That’s Nightjar. Hazel told me about her.  
**Luna**: The biggest owl in the valley. And the saddest.  

#### `s17-cliffs`

_Next night, you follow her to the high cliffs._  
_Halfway up is a nest. It’s as big as a cart wheel!_  
**Nova**: I slept up here once. An owl called all night. Two notes.  
**Luna**: She’ll go hunting soon. We could climb up then.  
**Luna**: Or we could wait, and let her see us coming.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): being gentle with a shy animal  
> **Luna**: How do we meet a shy animal?  
> - ✅ Slowly and quietly → Yes. Slow and quiet, so she isn’t scared.
> - Run up fast → That might scare her. Slow and quiet is kinder.
> - Shout hello → Loud sounds scare shy animals. Slow and quiet is better.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about how to be gentle and calm around a shy animal._


**Choices**

- **Climb while she’s out hunting.**
    - You climb up, hand over hand.
    - Whoosh! A quiet shadow. She’s home already.
    - **Nova**: She was always going to be home, wasn’t she.
- **Wait where she can see you.**
    - You sit on a rock below the nest. She comes home.
    - She looks at you a long time. Then she moves over, a little.
    - **Luna**: I think that means come up. Slowly.

#### `s17-nest`

_The nest is made of heather, wool and soft grey feathers._  
_Under Nightjar are eggs. All whole. All warm._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, do): count to 5; 4 and 1 make 5  
> _(Count the eggs together.)_  
> _One, two, three, four, five. Five eggs!_  
> _Four glow, like Grove eggs. One doesn’t. Four and one make five._  
> Check: `edu:count:eggs:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five eggs and saw that four and one more make five._

_Deep in the nest are two old, cracked eggshells._  
**Luna**: Hazel told me. Long ago, a cold night came in spring.  
**Luna**: Nightjar’s own two eggs got too cold. The babies inside died.  
**Luna**: They never hatched. She has been sad ever since.  
_Now she keeps other eggs warm. It helps her feel a little better._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): understanding grief; being gentle with someone sad  
> **Luna**: Someone can stay sad for a long time when someone dies.  
> **Luna**: Being kind and gentle helps. Let’s be gentle with her.  
> _For grown-ups: Heard, gently, that grief can last a long time, and that kindness helps._

**Nightjar**: Hoo-oo.  
_She settles over the eggs, like a blanket._  

#### `s17-return`

_Nightjar won’t let you take them all at once._  
_You lift two. Clack! goes her beak._  
**Luna**: One at a time, then. Let her watch each one go.  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): careful, gentle movement  
> _(Pretend to carry an egg. Two hands. Slow, gentle steps.)_  
> **Luna**: Two hands, slow feet. That’s how to carry an egg.  
> Check: `edu:carry:gently:two-hands`
> _For grown-ups: Practised moving slowly and carefully with both hands, as if carrying an egg._

**Nova**: That’s four climbs. In the dark!  
**Luna**: That’s four climbs. In the dark.  
_Nightjar watches the first egg all the way down._  

#### `s17-wild`

_Morning. Four eggs are back in the nursery._  
_One egg is still under Nightjar. It’s small and speckled._  
**Nova**: That’s a wild egg. Its mummy never came back.  
**Luna**: Let’s leave it with her. She can keep it warm.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): thinking about how someone else feels  
> **Luna**: How might Nightjar feel, with a baby egg to keep warm?  
> - ✅ Happier → Yes. Caring for someone can help a sad heart.
> - Cross → Look how gently she sits. I think she feels happier.
> _For grown-ups: Imagined how caring for someone small can help a sad heart feel better._

_On the way down, Luna stops._  
**Luna**: Next spring, she might come back to the nursery. What shall we do?  

**Choices**

- **Leave the nursery open, with a lantern for her.**
    - **Nova**: A night nurse with a beak!
    - **Luna**: She can sit on our eggs, where we can see. Like Hazel would.
- **Move the nursery inside, by the warm stove.**
    - **Luna**: That’s fair. The eggs come first.
    - **Nova**: She’ll sit on the roof and look at the chimney.
    - **Luna**: I’ll leave her a snack on the step sometimes.

#### `s17-feathers`

_Some weeks later, a grey feather is on your windowsill._  
_The next night, there’s another one._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): adding one more  
> _One feather, then one more. How many feathers now?_  
> - ✅ 2 → Yes! One and one more make two.
> - 3 → Let’s count: one… two. Two feathers!
> - 1 → One, and one more… two feathers!
> _For grown-ups: Added one more to one to make two._

**Luna**: She’s calling you! The little egg must be hatching.  
**Luna**: Go after dark. Just go and sit.  
**Nova**: I’m not coming. I’ll just be on the path. By accident.  

#### `s17-hatching`

_The egg is rocking. Nightjar moves over a little._  
_Crack! A tiny beak pokes out._  
_Out comes a fluffy grey baby. All eyes. Very cross!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): animal babies: an owlet  
> **Luna**: A baby owl has a special name. What is it?  
> - ✅ An owlet → Yes! A baby owl is an owlet.
> - A kitten → A kitten is a baby cat. A baby owl is an owlet!
> - A puppy → A puppy is a baby dog. A baby owl is an owlet!
> _For grown-ups: Learned the name for a baby owl: an owlet._

**Nightjar**: Hoo-oo. Hoo-oo.  
_The same two notes. But now they sound happy._  
_The baby owl tumbles into your lap. Nightjar lets it._  
**Luna**: She’s sharing. Look at that.  

#### `s17-complete`

_At the end of autumn, Nightjar drops her old feathers._  
_The wind blows them down into the Grove._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): birds moult: old feathers fall out, new ones grow  
> **Luna**: Birds drop their old feathers. New ones grow in their place.  
> **Luna**: It’s called moulting. Lots of birds do it every year.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that birds moult: old feathers fall out and new ones grow._

**Luna**: I sewed them into a cloak for you!  
_It’s light as a breath. Warm as a bird._  
_On the cliff top, two soft feet hold your collar._  
_Whoosh! You glide down over the valley to the Grove._  
**Nova**: Show-off.  
**Nova**: …Take me next time.  

## S18. The Grove Museum

- `s18-step1`: Dig up three fossils with Dusty. Put them in order, oldest first. · _The deepest layer is the oldest, like the bottom blanket on a bed._
- `s18-step2`: Wait quietly by Luna’s moth sheet for three night finds. · _Keep very still. The stiller you are, the faster the finds come._
- `s18-step3`: Find three creatures in Marina’s rock pools. · _Look under the weed and the stones. Always put each stone back the same way up._
- `s18-step4`: Find the Pole Star with Nova. Then wait for the Moon. · _Find the seven stars of the Plough. The two end stars point to the Pole Star._
- `s18-step5`: Fill the middle case. Then talk to Professor Delve at the door. · _Put something special in the big case in the middle of the barn._

#### `s18-barn`

_Hazel’s old barn smells of hay. Swallows’ mud nests line the roof._  
_A mole in a tweed waistcoat stands by a barrow of glass boxes._  
**Professor Delve**: Hello! I’m Professor Delve. I want to make a museum!  
**Professor Delve**: Four rooms. Twelve treasures. And every sign must be true!  
_A little brown moth lands on his nose._  
**Professor Delve**: Oh! Oh dear! A moth!  
_Professor Delve faints, very neatly, into the barrow._  
**Professor Delve**: I’m all right. I’m just lying down.  
_Dusty is at the door, holding a trowel._  
**Dusty**: I heard “fossils”. My hands want a job. Can I help?  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: d  
> _Delve and Dusty start with the same sound. Which one?_  
> - ✅ “d”, like dig → D-d-Dusty. D-d-Delve. And d for dig!
> - “m”, like mole → Mole starts with m. Delve and Dusty start with d-d-d!
> - “s”, like sun → Listen: d-d-Dusty, d-d-Delve. They start with d!
> _For grown-ups: Heard the same first sound, “d”, in two names._


#### `s18-fossils`

_The cliff has stripes, like a cake. Lots of layers._  
**Dusty**: Deeper is older. Like blankets on a bed.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): first and last: the deepest rock layer is the oldest  
> **Dusty**: When you make a bed, which blanket goes on first?  
> - ✅ The bottom one → Yes! Bottom first. So the bottom rock is the oldest.
> - The top one → The top one goes on last! Bottom first. The bottom rock is oldest.
> _For grown-ups: Used first and last to see why the deepest layer of rock is the oldest._

_Fossils are signs of plants and animals from long ago, kept in stone._  
_You find a curly shell, a flat fern and a little stone bug._  
**Dusty**: Fossil was my Nubbin. She always fell asleep while I dug.  
**Dusty**: Fossil died, not long ago. It’s quiet without her snoring.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): missing someone who died  
> **Dusty**: I miss her. Missing someone means you loved them.  
> _Dusty feels sad. It’s all right to feel sad._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that missing someone who died is a sign of love, and that sadness is all right._

**Professor Delve**: A trilobite! A sea animal from long, long ago.  
**Professor Delve**: A fern! Ferns have no flowers.  
**Professor Delve**: An ammonite! A curly-shelled sea animal. A cousin of the octopus.  
**Professor Delve**: True, true and true! Hooray!  

#### `s18-night`

_Luna hangs a white sheet between two trees._  
_A lantern shines on it. It glows like a sail._  
**Luna**: A moth trap. Nobody gets hurt. We look, we draw, they go home.  
**Professor Delve**: I shall watch from far away. Very far away.  
_He sits at the end of the garden, under a blanket._  
**Luna**: Now, the hard part. We wait. And we don’t talk.  
**Luna**: …That was me talking. Starting now.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, do): waiting quietly  
> _(Be as quiet as a moth. Your grown-up counts to ten.)_  
> _(One… two… three… all the way to ten. Shhh.)_  
> **Luna**: Lovely waiting! You stayed so still.  
> Check: `edu:wait:quietly:count-10`
> _For grown-ups: Practised waiting quietly and still while a grown-up counted to ten._


#### `s18-night-finds`

_A moth lands on the sheet. It’s green and black._  
_It’s hard to see! It looks just like old tree bark._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): camouflage: colours that help animals hide  
> **Luna**: Why is the moth so hard to see on the bark?  
> - ✅ Its colours match the bark → Yes! Its colours match. It hides in plain sight.
> - It’s invisible → Not invisible! Its colours just match the bark. That’s how it hides.
> - It’s asleep → Maybe! But mostly its colours match the bark. That’s how it hides.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that some animals hide because their colours match where they rest._

**Luna**: Its name means “Marvel of the Day”. But it flies at night!  
_Far away: thump. Professor Delve has fainted again._  
_By morning: a moth drawing, a spider web and an owl pellet._  
**Professor Delve**: Owls swallow little meals whole. They cough up the fur and bones.  
**Professor Delve**: Spiders make silk inside their bodies. They spin it into webs.  

#### `s18-shore`

_Low tide. Marina’s rock pools shine like little mirrors._  
**Marina**: Rule one. Turn a stone, then turn it back the same way.  
**Marina**: That stone is somebody’s roof.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-3, ask): looking after animals’ homes  
> **Marina**: Why do we put the stone back the same way?  
> - ✅ It’s a little animal’s home → Yes. Little crabs live under there. We keep their homes safe.
> - So it looks nice → It does look nice! But mostly, it’s somebody’s home.
> - It doesn’t matter → It does! Little animals live under it. It’s their home.
> _For grown-ups: Learned to put stones back in rock pools to look after the animals that live there._

**Marina**: And the tide always comes back. Even if you’re not ready!  
_A little crab walks past Professor Delve’s boot._  
**Professor Delve**: Not a moth! What a good day.  

#### `s18-shore-finds`

_You find an orange sea star. One arm is short. It’s growing back!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Count the sea star’s arms together.)_  
> _One, two, three, four, five. Five arms. One is little!_  
> Check: `edu:count:arms:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted the five arms of a sea star._

_You find a limpet, stuck tight to its rock._  
_And a hermit crab, moving into a bigger shell!_  
**Professor Delve**: A sea star can grow back an arm it lost.  
**Professor Delve**: A hermit crab moves to a bigger shell when it grows.  
**Marina**: Look, it left its old shell. A smaller crab might want it.  
**Professor Delve**: Or a museum might want it. I’m only saying.  

**Choices**

- **Take the empty shell for the museum.**
    - **Marina**: Fair. There are lots of shells.
    - **Professor Delve**: It shall have a little cushion!
- **Leave the shell for the next crab.**
    - **Marina**: Good. Somebody’s old house is somebody’s new one.
    - **Professor Delve**: Then we’ll have a drawing instead.

#### `s18-stars`

_Night on the hill. Nova lies in the cold grass._  
**Nova**: Clear night. Best sky in the valley.  
**Nova**: I brought you something. Don’t make a fuss. It’s a rock.  
_A black stone, heavy and bumpy. Like burnt toast._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): saying thank you for a gift  
> _Nova gives you a present. What can you say?_  
> - ✅ “Thank you!” → …You’re welcome. Don’t make it a moment.
> - “Is that all?” → That might hurt Nova’s feelings. “Thank you” is kinder.
> - Say nothing → A “thank you” lets Nova know you love it.
> _For grown-ups: Practised saying thank you for a present._

**Nova**: It fell from the sky. I walked two days to find it.  
**Professor Delve**: A rock from space! And it isn’t a moth!  
**Nova**: Now. Find me north.  

#### `s18-star-finds`

_Look for seven stars in the shape of a pan. That’s the Plough._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 7  
> _(Count the stars of the Plough together.)_  
> _One, two, three, four, five, six, seven!_  
> Check: `edu:count:stars:7`
> _For grown-ups: Counted the seven stars of the Plough._

_The two end stars point to the Pole Star._  
_The Pole Star stays still. The other stars turn slowly round it._  
**Nova**: That star helped me find my way home. Lots of times.  
_Late at night, the Moon comes up. Orange, then white._  
**Professor Delve**: The Moon always shows us the same face.  
**Professor Delve**: A shooting star isn’t a star. It’s space rock or dust, burning up.  
**Nova**: You read signs to the sky, Professor.  

#### `s18-heart`

_Twelve cases are full. The big middle case is empty._  
**Professor Delve**: Every museum has a middle case. It holds the most special thing.  
_Dusty comes in, holding a bent card._  
**Dusty**: Fossil’s card. From the hill, by her flower.  
**Dusty**: I don’t want it in a drawer. I want people to see it.  
**Professor Delve**: A museum is where a valley remembers.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): remembering someone we love  
> _How can we remember someone we love?_  
> - ✅ Tell stories about them → Yes. Telling stories keeps her close.
> - Hide all their things away → Then I might forget. I want to see her card and remember.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about remembering someone who died by sharing stories about them._

**Professor Delve**: Or there’s Hazel’s egg book. Forty years of eggs are in it.  
**Dusty**: That’s a good thing. It just isn’t Fossil.  

**Choices**

- **Put Fossil’s card in the middle case.**
    - **Dusty**: Thank you. She’d hate the glass. She’d sit on top of it!
    - **Professor Delve**: And Hazel’s book stays at the cottage. It’s still being written.
- **Put Hazel’s egg book in the middle case.**
    - **Dusty**: Can Fossil’s card go inside it? Like a bookmark?
    - **Professor Delve**: A bookmark is allowed. A bookmark is very welcome!
    - **Professor Delve**: The book is only borrowed. When you want it back, I’ll carry it home.
- **Leave the case empty, for whoever comes next.**
    - You put a card inside: For whoever we remember next.
    - **Professor Delve**: An empty case! People will stand and think.
    - **Dusty**: Good. Fossil’s card can go beside it.

#### `s18-door`

_The barn is swept. Twelve signs shine._  
**Professor Delve**: One last thing. The door.  
**Professor Delve**: Two Shells a visit would keep the barn mended.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): adding 2 and 2  
> **Professor Delve**: Two Shells for you. Two Shells for a friend. How many?  
> - ✅ 4 → Yes! Two and two make four.
> - 3 → Let’s count on fingers: one, two… three, four. Four!
> - 2 → That’s just one visit. Two and two more make four!
> _For grown-ups: Added two and two to make four._

**Professor Delve**: Or it could be free. We’d manage. Probably.  

**Choices**

- **Two Shells a visit, to keep it mended.**
    - **Professor Delve**: Sensible. Places need mending.
    - **Professor Delve**: Anyone shorter than the door handle goes free!
- **Keep it free for everyone.**
    - **Professor Delve**: Free. Yes. It belongs to the whole valley.
    - **Professor Delve**: I’ll put out a jar. Just a hopeful one.

#### `s18-complete`

_Opening day! Half the valley comes._  
_Children press their noses on the trilobite case._  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.His.2.K-2, notice): museums keep things from long ago  
> **Professor Delve**: A museum keeps old things safe.  
> **Professor Delve**: Then children can learn about long ago.  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that museums keep old things safe so we can learn about the past._

_Sparks reads every sign out loud. Very loud._  
**Professor Delve**: You did this, Curator. Well done.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, do): clapping syllables  
> _(Clap it together: Cu-ra-tor. How many claps?)_  
> _Three claps! Cu-ra-tor._  
> Check: `edu:clap:syllables:curator`
> _For grown-ups: Clapped the three syllables in “Curator”._

_He gives you his old magnifying glass, on a ribbon._  
**Professor Delve**: It makes little things look big. I’d like it to keep looking.  
_Then a moth flies in. The Curator catches the professor!_  

## S13. The Mill Cellar

- `s13-step1`: Get past the rats in the grain store. · _Share some fruit with them, or tiptoe along the wall. Then find the hatch under the sacks._
- `s13-step2`: Let the water out of the flooded room. · _Open the bottom gate first, then the middle one, then the top. Water runs downhill!_
- `s13-step3`: Find what is stopping the big wheel. · _Look in the biggest gear. Keep your hands far away from the wheel._
- `s13-step4`: Open the iron door with Tobin’s key. · _Take a lantern. It is very dark down there._
- `s13-step5`: Do what you chose with the letter. · _Keep it safe in your bag, take it to the stove with Tobin, or take it to the grave by the river._

#### `s13-tobin`

_Autumn. The river runs brown and loud. Whoosh!_  
_By the river leans an old mill. Moss sits on its millstone._  
**Old Tobin**: Hazel’s grandchild! You have her chin.  
**Old Tobin**: I’m Tobin. I lit the lamps in this valley for fifty-one years.  
**Old Tobin**: This was Aldous’s mill. He was Corvin’s dad. Yes, grumpy Corvin.  
**Old Tobin**: Under it is a cellar. Four floors, going down, down, down.  
**Old Tobin**: Once, in the year of the Three Hats, the wheel spun so fast—  
_He stops. He scratches his head._  
**Old Tobin**: Where was I? Cellar. Yes. Dark.  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-2, do): we need light to see  
> **Moss**: Dark means we need a lantern. We can only see when there is light.  
> _(Cover your eyes. Can you see? Now open them. Light!)_  
> Check: `edu:cover:eyes:light-dark`
> _For grown-ups: Found out that we can only see things when there is light, by covering and uncovering their eyes._

**Moss**: He never gets to the end of that story. Never once.  

#### `s13-level1`

_Floor one: the grain store. Big sacks, all the way up._  
_It smells of old flour. And mouse._  
_Scritch, scratch. Scritch, scratch. Something is in the dark._  
_Little noses. Little whiskers. Rats!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Count the little noses together: one, two, three, four, five.)_  
> **Old Tobin**: Five rats. And a lot of whiskers.  
> Check: `edu:count:rats:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five rats in the grain store._

**Old Tobin**: Mill rats. Very tidy. They never take a day off.  

**Choices**

- **Roll them a plum, and walk past while they eat.**
    - You roll a plum. Five little faces turn. Munch, munch.
    - You walk right past. Nobody looks up.
    - **Old Tobin**: Sharing food! Your grandmother’s way.
- **Cover your lantern and tiptoe along the wall.**
    - Tiptoe, tiptoe, along the wall.
    - A rat sits on your boot. It thinks you are a chair!
    - Behind you, Tobin tiptoes too. He whispers very loudly.

#### `s13-sluice`

_Creak! The old hoist lifts the sacks. Underneath is a hatch._  
_And three old things, dusty and forgotten._  
**Old Tobin**: Treasure! Everything in a cellar gets a grand name.  
**Old Tobin**: A ladle. The Ladle of Second Thoughts.  
**Old Tobin**: A sock. The Sock of Nobody Knows Where.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): hear words that rhyme  
> **Old Tobin**: Sock! What rhymes with sock?  
> - ✅ Rock → Sock, rock! They sound the same at the end.
> - Cheese → Cheese is lovely. But listen: sock, rock. Rock rhymes with sock!
> - Ladle → Sock, ladle… no. Sock, rock! Rock rhymes.
> _For grown-ups: Found a word that rhymes with “sock”._

**Old Tobin**: A cheese rind. The Cheese Rind of Being Brave!  
_Below the hatch, water lies still in the dark._  
**Old Tobin**: Floor two is flooded. Three gates. Open them in the right order!  
**Old Tobin**: Or we’ll be swimming. Marina could swim in soup.  

#### `s13-level2`

_Bottom gate first. Then the middle. Then the top._  
_Glug, glug, glug! The water runs out of the room._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): water runs downhill  
> **Old Tobin**: Water always runs downhill. Down, down, to the lowest place.  
> _(Run your finger down, down, like the water. Whoosh!)_  
> _For grown-ups: Saw that water runs downhill, which is why the lowest gate opens first._

_Two eels flop on the floor. They look very cross._  
_Up one wall are chalk lines. Each one a little higher._  
_Someone measured a boy here, every year, as he grew._  
_By the top line, a grown-up wrote: BIG ENOUGH TO WORK._  
**Old Tobin**: …Where was I.  
_Tobin goes quiet. He walks on down the steps._  

#### `s13-wheelpit`

_On the steps the flood left boots, a doorknob and a soggy scarf._  
**Old Tobin**: The Squeaky Boots! The Doorknob That Won’t Open!  
**Old Tobin**: And the Soggy Scarf. It still thinks it will dry.  
_Floor three: the wheel pit. The giant wheel drips. Plip, plop._  
_The river pushes it. The whole mill shakes. Rumble!_  
_Moss calls down. He is staying on his millstone, thank you._  
**Old Tobin**: Mind the wheel. It’s stuck, but it can still move.  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, ask): keeping safe near big machines  
> **Old Tobin**: Where should our hands be, near a big wheel?  
> - ✅ Far away, by our sides → Yes. Big machines can hurt fingers. We stay back.
> - On the spokes → Ooh, no. A wheel can move and pinch. Hands far away, by our sides.
> _For grown-ups: Learned to keep hands away from big moving machines._

**Old Tobin**: Find what’s jamming it. Then we step round.  

#### `s13-level3`

_In the biggest gear is a wooden wedge. Somebody put it there._  
_A label hangs from it. It says: STOPPED. LEAVE IT._  
**You**: Somebody stopped the wheel on purpose.  
**Old Tobin**: Aldous did, in his last winter. He sat on that step.  
**Old Tobin**: He was very old. Soon after that, he died.  
**Old Tobin**: His body stopped working, and it won’t start again.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): noticing when someone is sad, and what helps  
> _Tobin is sad. He misses Aldous._  
> _What could help Tobin?_  
> - ✅ Sit next to him for a bit → Thank you. That helps. Sitting together always helps.
> - Run off and play → Tobin might feel lonely. Let’s sit with him a moment instead. Thank you. That helps.
> _For grown-ups: Heard plainly that Aldous died, noticed Tobin was sad, and chose to sit with him._

_Past the wheel, a stair goes down to an iron door. It is locked._  
**Old Tobin**: Not that door. Not yet.  
**Old Tobin**: Read Hazel’s notebook first. Then come back for the key.  

#### `s13-key`

_Later, with Hazel’s notebook in your coat, you go back._  
**Old Tobin**: Corvin was Hazel’s helper once. Before that, he was this mill’s boy.  
**Old Tobin**: Every evening I lit the lamp by the gate.  
**Old Tobin**: And every evening there was a boy at the window. Working.  
**Old Tobin**: That’s the whole story. The only one I ever finished.  
_He gives you a little iron key. It’s warm from his pocket._  
**Old Tobin**: I found more treasure. A cog: the Cog of Very Big Patience.  
**Old Tobin**: And a tiny hat, cut from a round millstone. Heavy!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, do): circles, big and small  
> **Old Tobin**: A cog is round, with little teeth all round the edge.  
> _(Draw a circle in the air. Round and round!)_  
> **Old Tobin**: The hat is a circle too. Big circle, little circle. Both circles!  
> Check: `edu:draw:shape:circle`
> _For grown-ups: Drew a circle and saw that big and small round things are both circles._

**Old Tobin**: Down we go. Together.  

#### `s13-desk`

_The bottom room is small, dry and very cold._  
_A desk. A chair. An apron on a hook, still floury._  
_A pocket watch, stopped just after five o’clock._  
**Old Tobin**: Five in the morning. That’s when the wheel started, every day.  
_Inside the big book are three rules, in chalk._  
_ONE: the wheel never stops. TWO: nobody rests. THREE: keep up!_  
_Under them, in a child’s careful writing, the same three rules._  
_Every page says work, work, work. Never once does it say rest._  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, ask): everybody needs rest and sleep  
> **Old Tobin**: Hazel’s rules were kind ones. These say: never rest.  
> **Old Tobin**: Do people need rest?  
> - ✅ Yes! Everybody needs rest and sleep → Yes. Rest and sleep help our bodies feel better and grow.
> - No, keep working → Aldous thought so. But bodies get tired. Everybody needs rest and sleep. Even millers.
> _For grown-ups: Compared a rule that said “never rest” with Hazel’s kinder rules, and learned that everybody needs rest and sleep._

_At the back is a letter. It was never sent. It says: Corvin._  
**Old Tobin**: I kept it shut away for twenty years. It got heavy, keeping it.  

#### `s13-letter`

_You open the letter. It is short._  
**Aldous Vale**: Dear Corvin.  
**Aldous Vale**: I worked all the time. I never stopped. Not once.  
**Aldous Vale**: I was too busy to say the most important thing.  
**Aldous Vale**: I love you. I should have said it every day.  
**Aldous Vale**: If you ever want to stop, come and sit by the river.  
**Aldous Vale**: Sit as long as you like. Nobody will count.  
**Aldous Vale**: Love, your dad.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): saying “I love you” to the people we love  
> _“I love you.” Three small words. They feel big when we hear them._  
> _(Ask: who could you say “I love you” to today? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard a kind letter from a dad to his son, and thought about who they could say “I love you” to._

**Old Tobin**: “Nobody will count.” He counted everything. And then that.  
**Old Tobin**: It isn’t mine to decide. What shall we do with it?  

**Choices**

- **Keep it for Corvin. Give it to him when his hands are warm.**
    - You fold it up and put it in your coat, by Hazel’s notebook.
    - **Old Tobin**: Yes. Cold hands can’t hold a thing like that.
    - **Old Tobin**: You’ll know when. Your grandmother always knew when.
- **Ask Tobin to burn it in the stove. Some things end with the person.**
    - **Old Tobin**: That’s a real answer. I’ve thought it too.
    - **Old Tobin**: He wrote lots of letters, and burned them. This would be the last.
- **Read it to Moss, and leave it at Aldous’s grave.**
    - **Old Tobin**: Read to old Moss, and left with Aldous. Yes.
    - **Old Tobin**: Nobody ever read Aldous a letter. This is a way to remember him.

#### `s13-settled-kept`

_At home, you put the letter in the tin box under your bed._  
_Hazel’s letter is in there too._  
_Hazel’s writing leans over, like it’s walking uphill._  
_Aldous’s writing stands up straight, stiff as a soldier._  

> **Lesson** (art, ELOF: Literacy, do): straight lines and leaning lines  
> _(Draw a straight line in the air, like Aldous. Up and down!)_  
> _(Now a leaning line, like Hazel. Whee, over it goes!)_  
> Check: `edu:draw:line:straight-leaning`
> _For grown-ups: Drew straight and leaning lines in the air, the first strokes of writing._

_You check on the letter a lot. That’s part of keeping it safe._  

#### `s13-complete`

_A week later, Tobin gives you the apron and the stopped watch._  
**Old Tobin**: Somebody meant to hand these down. He never stopped long enough.  
_Then he knocks the wedge out of the gear. Bonk!_  
_The wheel turns. Slowly. As slowly as the river likes._  
**Old Tobin**: There. Nobody’s counting.  
**Old Tobin**: Bring your seeds down. The mill makes lovely flour.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): how a mill turns seeds into flour  
> **Old Tobin**: The river turns the wheel. The wheel turns the big stone.  
> **Old Tobin**: The stone squashes seeds into soft flour. Flour makes bread!  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that a water mill uses the river to grind seeds into flour for bread._

**Old Tobin**: Now, the year of the Three Hats… Where was I?  
**You**: Mill. Yes. Light.  
_Tobin laughs so hard he has to sit down._  

## S19. Cinder’s Last Light

- `s19-step1`: Ask Marina how to climb up the snowy hill. · _She’s at the rock pools, breaking the ice._
- `s19-step2`: Climb up the snowfield. · _Step in Marina’s footprints. When the wind blows hard, crouch down and wait._
- `s19-step3`: Sit by the little red light, and wait. · _Sit very still and very quiet. It takes a long time._
- `s19-step4`: Stay with Cinder. · _Stay sitting. You don’t have to do anything. Just stay._

#### `s19-warmth`

_Two days after the Long Night. Snow everywhere. Very quiet._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, ask): winter brings snow and cold  
> _Snow on the ground. Cold air. What season is it?_  
> - ✅ Winter → Winter. The coldest time of year.
> - Summer → Summer is warm and sunny. Snow comes in winter.
> _For grown-ups: Named winter as the snowy, cold season._

_Moss sits on the step, looking up at the high snowy hill._  
_He is very thin now. You can almost see through him._  
**Moss**: There is something warm up there.  
**Moss**: There shouldn’t be. Nothing has been warm up there for ages.  
**Moss**: I’m too thin to climb. Will you carry me in your coat?  
**Moss**: I won’t talk much.  
**Moss**: That was a fib. Ask Marina. She knows the path.  

#### `s19-marina`

_Marina is in a rock pool, breaking the ice. She’s very happy._  
**Marina**: The high pass? In winter? Lovely! You’ll hate it.  
**Marina**: All our water comes down from that snow. I know the way.  
**Marina**: Boots. A rope. Two pairs of socks.  
**Marina**: Don’t be brave. Be careful.  
**Marina**: Two pairs. I will check!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): dressing warmly for cold weather  
> _(What keeps us warm in the snow?)_  
> _(Point to your feet, your hands and your head.)_  
> **Marina**: Socks for feet. Mittens for hands. A hat for your head!  
> Check: `edu:point:clothes:warm`
> _For grown-ups: Matched warm clothes to body parts to get ready for cold weather._


#### `s19-climb`

_First light. Marina ties a rope to you. Then Moss to your button._  
_He rides in your coat. Just his little face pokes out._  
**Moss**: Left.  
**Marina**: Right.  
**Moss**: Her right. My left. We agree, in different directions!  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, do): left and right  
> _(Wave your left hand, like Moss. Now your right, like Marina!)_  
> **Moss**: Left and right. Both good. Both up the hill.  
> Check: `edu:wave:hand:left-right`
> _For grown-ups: Practised left and right by waving each hand._

_Crunch, crunch, crunch. The snow sounds like biting toast._  
**Marina**: Lovely wind! It makes your cheeks tingle!  
**Moss**: Your heart is very loud. I don’t mind. It’s warm.  

#### `s19-hollow`

_Near the top, a rock leans over, like a hand over a cup._  
_Under it there is no snow. The rock is dry and a little warm._  
_On a flat stone sits a tiny red light. Smaller than your fingernail._  
**Marina**: I’ll wait up on the edge. Shout if you need me.  
_Moss has gone very still in your coat._  
**Moss**: …Cinder.  
**Moss**: She was a warden, like me. Long, long ago.  
**Moss**: She can’t hear us yet. We have to sit still for a long time.  
**Moss**: Let me sit by her. You sit by me. We’ll wait.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, do): being still and calm, and waiting  
> _(Let’s be very still and quiet too. Breathe in… and out.)_  
> _(Take three slow breaths together: one… two… three.)_  
> Check: `edu:sit:still:quiet`
> _For grown-ups: Practised sitting still and breathing slowly while waiting._


#### `s19-sit`

_You sit on the dry rock. The wind goes over. Whoooo._  
_Far below is the whole valley. The cottage looks tiny._  
_The waterfall is frozen white. It doesn’t move at all._  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS2-3, ask): very cold water turns to ice  
> _Why is the waterfall white and still?_  
> - ✅ It got so cold, the water froze into ice → Yes! When water gets very cold, it turns into ice.
> - It got tired → Waterfalls don’t get tired! It got so cold, the water turned into ice.
> _For grown-ups: Explained a frozen waterfall: very cold water turns into ice._

_You wait. Your feet get cold. You keep waiting._  

#### `s19-question`

_After a long time, the red light grows a tiny bit bigger._  
**Cinder**: Are they safe?  
**Moss**: Not just safe. They’re well.  
**Moss**: They run and play and eat too many plums.  
**Cinder**: Well. Well is better. I didn’t know.  
**Cinder**: Long ago, I held them too tight. To keep them safe.  
**Cinder**: They went still and grey. I am so sorry.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): hear words that rhyme  
> **Cinder**: Do they still say my name, down there?  
> _Children skip to a rhyme about her:_  
> **You**: Cinder held them close and tight, kept them warm one winter night.  
> _Which word rhymes with “tight”?_  
> - ✅ Night → Tight, night. That’s right. They still say my name.
> - Warm → Listen: tight… night. Night rhymes with tight. They still say my name.
