> _For grown-ups: Heard the valley’s skipping rhyme and found the rhyming pair “tight” and “night”._

**Moss**: Cinder, I forgave you long ago. Here. It’s yours.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): saying sorry and forgiving  
> _Saying sorry is brave. Forgiving is kind._  
> _(Ask: when did someone forgive you? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Saw someone say sorry and be forgiven, and talked about a time they were forgiven._

**Cinder**: Thank you, Moss. Keeper, the grey ones live by you.  
**Cinder**: Am I forgiven by them?  

**Choices**

- **“You’re forgiven.”**
    - **You**: You’re forgiven.
    - The light goes warm, like a fire with the kettle on.
    - **Cinder**: Oh. I thought being forgiven would be heavy. It’s light!
    - **Moss**: Yes. This one, you just put down.
- **“That’s theirs to say. But you’ll be remembered.”**
    - **You**: That’s theirs to say. But you’ll be remembered.
    - **Cinder**: Remembered. That’s what I should have asked for.
    - **Cinder**: Will you tell them I loved them?
    - **You**: All of it.
    - **Moss**: We are made of being remembered. I told her that once.

#### `s19-after`

_The red light is getting smaller. Slowly. Gently._  
**Cinder**: I kept my little light on for so long. In case there was news.  
**Cinder**: Now there’s been news. I can stop.  
_Cinder is fading. That means she is dying._  
_Her light will stop, and it won’t start again._  
**Cinder**: Will you stay? You don’t have to do anything. Just stay.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, do): staying close to someone who needs us  
> _(Hold your grown-up’s hand. Staying close is a kind thing to do.)_  
> **Cinder**: Thank you.  
> Check: `edu:hold:hand:stay`
> _For grown-ups: Heard plainly what fading means, and learned that staying close is a way to care for someone._


#### `s19-fade`

_You stay. Moss sits right next to the little light._  
_The light gets smaller and smaller. Slowly. Gently._  
**Cinder**: Moss. It isn’t cold.  
**Moss**: No. Not this way.  
_Then the light is gone. Cinder has died._  
_There is only the flat stone, and the wind._  
**Moss**: That is fading done right. Not frozen still. Let go.  
_Moss sits there a long time. You sit with him._  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, notice): feeling sad when someone dies, and what helps  
> _Moss is sad. You might feel sad too. That’s all right._  
> _Staying close together helps. So does remembering._  
> _(Give your grown-up a hug, if you like.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard plainly that Cinder died, and that feeling sad is all right; closeness and remembering help._


#### `s19-complete`

_Where the light was, the stone is still warm. Like a hand._  
_On it sits a little red pebble. It stays warm all the way home._  
_Marina’s eyes are red. She says it’s the wind._  
**Marina**: Come on. Feet in my footprints. Home before dark.  
_That night, the Long-Grass lantern feels warm to stand by._  
_You put the red pebble on the windowsill._  
_By morning, the Nubbins are asleep in a heap under it._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Count the sleepy Nubbins: one, two, three, four, five.)_  
> _Five Nubbins, warm and snug. Shh!_  
> Check: `edu:count:nubbins:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five sleeping Nubbins._


## S22. The Climb

- `s22-step1`: Climb the slidey stones with Nova. · _Three quick steps, then rest on a big rock. Keep going!_
- `s22-step2`: Climb up the Chimney. · _Push with your back, step with your feet. One at a time._
- `s22-step3`: Cross the Ridge. · _Stay low when the wind blows. Look at the next step, not down._
- `s22-step4`: Put the flag on the very top. · _It’s in Nova’s bag, under the oatcakes. Push the pole into the crack._

#### `s22-foot`

_The Tooth is a tall, pointy mountain. Snow on the very top._  
_Nova waits at the bottom with a rope and a bag of oatcakes._  
**Nova**: I’ve tried to climb it three times. I always come down.  
**Nova**: I asked you for help once. It went well. So here I am!  
_Next to her sits a little shadow, the size of a cat._  
_It looks just like Nova. Same hair. Same boots._  
**Nova’s Worry**: What if you slip?  
**Nova**: …You can see it? Nobody ever sees it!  
**Nova**: It’s my Worry. It talks to me. It’s mostly right. Annoying!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): naming a worry  
> **Nova**: Everybody has worries. Mine always says “what if”.  
> _Giving a worry a name makes it easier to carry._  
> _(Ask: do you have a “what if” worry? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Met Nova’s Worry and talked about naming their own “what if” worries._

**Nova**: Right. Up we go. I’ll sing. It helps me.  

#### `s22-scree`

_The little stones slide under your feet. Clatter, clatter!_  
**Nova**: ♪ Little stones, little stones, sliding under my toes! ♪  
**Nova**: ♪ I’m going up, but I’m sliding down, and that’s how it goes! ♪  
_She sings it four times. It gets worse each time._  

> **Lesson** (music, do): clapping a steady beat  
> _(Clap along as you sing: lit-tle stones, lit-tle stones!)_  
> **Nova**: Clap, clap! Now we’re a band.  
> Check: `edu:clap:beat:stones-song`
> _For grown-ups: Clapped a steady beat along with Nova’s climbing song._

**Nova’s Worry**: Last time you slipped here. You hurt your knee.  
**Nova**: It’s right about that. It’s the next bit it gets wrong.  

**Choices**

- **“It is right. And you got up and kept going.”**
    - **Nova**: …I did, didn’t I? I kept going all the way up.
    - **Nova’s Worry**: That’s… also true.
    - **Nova**: Ha!
- **“That was last time. This time there are two of us.”**
    - **Nova**: Two of us. Huh.
    - **Nova’s Worry**: Three.
    - **Nova**: It can count! It always could.

#### `s22-chimney`

_The Chimney is a narrow crack in the rock._  
_Back on one side. Boots on the other. Push and step!_  
**Nova**: ♪ I’m in a chimney, I am not soot! This is my foot! ♪  
**Nova**: ♪ This is my other foot! ♪  
_Far below, a big bird called a buzzard flies round and round._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): above and below  
> _Is the buzzard above you, or below you?_  
> - ✅ Below → Below! We’re higher than a bird. Wow.
> - Above → Look down… it’s below us! We’re higher than the bird.
> _For grown-ups: Used “above” and “below” to say where the bird was._


#### `s22-chimney-top`

_Halfway up, Nova stops. The Worry sits just above her._  
**Nova’s Worry**: When you were little, somebody left. What if it happens again?  
_Nova holds the rock tight. She doesn’t move._  
**Nova**: That’s true. Somebody left when I was nine.  
**Nova**: So now I always leave first. It hurts less.  

> **Lesson** (health, CASEL: Self-management, do): slow breathing to feel calm  
> _(Help Nova feel calm. Smell a flower: breathe in…)_  
> _(Blow out a candle: breathe out… Once more!)_  
> **Nova**: That helps. A bit. Thanks.  
> Check: `edu:breathe:slow:flower-candle`
> _For grown-ups: Practised slow “smell the flower, blow the candle” breathing to calm a scared feeling._


**Choices**

- **“It’s true. But it isn’t a reason to let go.”**
    - **Nova**: …No. It isn’t.
    - She moves one hand. Then one foot. Slowly.
    - **Nova’s Worry**: I didn’t say let go. I said it’s a long way down.
    - **Nova**: You’re very bad at saying what you mean.
- **“Not now. Look at me, not down.”**
    - She looks at you. Not down. At you.
    - **Nova**: …Right. Hands.
    - She climbs. The Worry climbs too, muttering about loose rocks.

#### `s22-ridge`

_The Ridge is thin, with sky on both sides. Whoosh goes the wind!_  
_This is where Nova always turns back._  
_There’s a little pile of stones. One stone for every try._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, do): count to 3, and one more  
> _(Count the stones: one, two, three.)_  
> **Nova**: Three tries. If I get across, this try is number four!  
> Check: `edu:count:stones:3`
> _For grown-ups: Counted three stones and worked out that one more try makes four._

**Nova’s Worry**: Go back. It’s windy. It’s high. Come another day.  
**Nova**: Every time it talks, I feel smaller. Up here, it always wins.  

#### `s22-ridge-cross`

_In the middle of the Ridge, Nova stops. The Worry is beside her._  
_She looks at it. Then she holds out her hand._  
**Nova**: Come on, then. You always come. So come with me.  
**Nova’s Worry**: …What?  
_The Worry takes her hand. It gets smaller. Not gone. Smaller._  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, notice): worries feel smaller when we share them  
> _When Nova holds her Worry gently, it gets smaller._  
> _Worries feel smaller when we share them with someone._  
> _(Ask: who could you tell a worry to? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Saw a worry shrink when it was held gently, and named someone they could tell a worry to._

_They cross together. Nova sings. A second voice joins in, very flat._  
**Nova**: You can SING?  
**Nova’s Worry**: I always could. You never asked.  

#### `s22-scramble`

_The last bit is a climb on hands and knees._  
_Up here the air is very cold. The snow never melts._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): high up on a mountain it is colder  
> **Nova**: High up on a mountain, it’s colder than down below.  
> **Nova**: That’s why the very top has snow, when the valley doesn’t.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that mountain tops are colder than valleys, which is why snow stays up there._

_At the top is a point of rock, as big as a kitchen table._  
**Nova**: Give me the flag. No, you do it. No. Together!  

#### `s22-summit`

_From the top of the Tooth, you can see everything!_  
_The waterfall, like a white thread. Marina’s pools, like coins._  
_And the Grove: the pond, the cottage, four tiny lantern posts._  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Geo.2.K-2, do): a map is a picture of a place from above  
> _From up high, the valley looks like a map._  
> _(Point: where is the pond? Where is the cottage?)_  
> **Nova**: A map is a picture of a place, seen from above.  
> Check: `edu:point:map:valley`
> _For grown-ups: Looked down on the valley like a map and pointed out places in it._

_Nova sits down. The Worry sits next to her, holding her hand._  
**Nova**: I’d like to stay for a while.  
**Nova**: Not up here. Down there, in the valley. With you all.  
**Nova’s Worry**: It might be scary.  
**Nova**: Yes. You can come too.  

#### `s22-complete`

_Going down is quicker, and much more slippy._  
_Nova and the Worry sing all the way._  
_At the bottom, Nova shares out the oatcakes. They’re a bit squashed._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, ask): one each: counting to share fairly  
> **Nova**: Three of us: me, you, and my Worry. How many oatcakes do we need?  
> - ✅ 3 → Three! One each. Fair and square.
> - 2 → Let’s count: one for me, one for you, one for the Worry. Three!
> - 5 → That’s extra! One each makes three.
> _For grown-ups: Matched one oatcake to each of three friends to share fairly._

**Nova**: Keep the flag. And my spare boots. I won’t need spares.  
**Nova**: I’m not going anywhere. I’m staying.  
_The Worry waves. Small. Only you can see it._  

## S14. Tilly’s Tabletop

- `s14-step1`: Play Tilly’s game: the Bog of Mild Peril. · _Roll the dice when Tilly says. Count the dots!_
- `s14-step2`: Get up the Tower of the Tax Toad. · _The Toad wants shells for everything. Try paying with silly things!_
- `s14-step3`: Find Tilly. · _Listen for her whistle: low, then high._
- `s14-step4`: Get past the Grey Grandmother’s geese. · _Roll the dice three times. You can’t lose. The worst thing that happens is: you get a goose._
- `s14-step5`: Sit next to the dragon, and wait. · _Put the dice down. Don’t move. Just wait._

#### `s14-table`

_Rain on the window. Pitter, patter._  
_In comes Sparks, with a small girl in big wellies._  
**Sparks**: This is Tilly. My cousin. She’s nine. She’s the boss now. Sorry!  
**Tilly**: I’m the Game Mistress. It’s like a boss, but more.  
_She spreads a felt-tip map on Hazel’s table._  
_Then the dice. Cardboard, cut by hand, with tape on the corners._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 6  
> _(A die has six sides. Count them: one, two, three, four, five, six.)_  
> **Tilly**: Five sides have dots. The sixth side just says LOTS.  
> Check: `edu:count:dice-sides:6`
> _For grown-ups: Counted the six sides of a die._

**Tilly**: Rule one: the Game Mistress is always right. Rule two: see rule one.  
**Tilly**: Rule three: snacks at the end. Only at the end.  
**Tilly**: Pick your hero. Ten seconds. Nine. Four!  

**Choices**

- **A knight with a slightly bent sword.**
    - **Tilly**: Sir Bent Sword! Bent swords can hit round corners.
- **A wizard who mostly makes soup.**
    - **Tilly**: Soup Wizard! Your spells are hot. Blow on them first.
- **A sneaky hero dressed as a hat stand.**
    - **Tilly**: Nobody suspects a hat stand. Not even other hat stands.

#### `s14-bog`

_The Bog of Mild Peril is the pond, coloured green._  
_A felt-tip frog sits in it. He does not look impressed._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-1, ask): animal homes: frogs live in ponds  
> **Tilly**: Where do real frogs like to live?  
> - ✅ In ponds and wet places → Yes! Frogs need water. Ponds are perfect frog homes.
> - In a dry desert → Too dry! Frogs need water. They live in ponds and wet places.
> - Up a chimney → Ha! No. Frogs live in ponds and wet places.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that frogs live in ponds and wet places because they need water._

**Sparks**: I ATTACK THE BOG!  
**Tilly**: You can’t attack a bog. Roll anyway.  
_Sparks rolls. One dot._  
**Tilly**: A one! Your sword is now a cheese sandwich.  
**Byte**: Actually, that’s not how—  
**Tilly**: Rule four! Say “actually”, lose a point.  
_You roll a LOTS. The bog likes you. Everybody gets across!_  
**Tilly**: Game over. Nobody’s hurt. Somebody’s a sandwich.  

#### `s14-tower`

_Next day there’s a new tower on the map._  
**Tilly**: The Tower of the Tax Toad! He makes you pay for everything.  
**Tilly**: Ribbit! That’s two shells for the ribbit.  
**Tilly**: And one shell for the stairs!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): add 1 more  
> **Tilly**: Two shells for the ribbit. One for the stairs. How many shells?  
> - ✅ 3 → Three shells! Two and one more make three.
> - 2 → Count with me: one, two… and one more. Three!
> - 5 → Too many! Two and one more make three.
> _For grown-ups: Added two shells and one more shell to make three._

**Pixel**: Can I build a machine to beat him?  
**Tilly**: Roll. Three dots. Your machine is a toad now. It works for him.  
**Byte**: Is there a tax on paying the tax?  
**Tilly**: …Yes. Thanks SO much, Byte.  

#### `s14-toad`

**Byte**: The Toad owes… no, we owe… I’ve muddled it.  
**Tilly**: The Toad doesn’t do sums. The Toad does feelings.  
_Then Pixel’s Nubbin wakes up. It climbs onto the map._  
_Chomp! It eats the cardboard Tax Toad._  
**Tilly**: The Toad has been eaten! No more paying for anyone!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: t  
> **Tilly**: Tilly. Tax. Toad. They all start with the same sound. T-t-t!  
> **Tilly**: Which one starts with t too?  
> - ✅ Table → T-t-table! Yes!
> - Frog → F-f-frog starts with f. Listen: t-t-table. Table!
> - Map → M-m-map starts with m. T-t-table starts with t!
> _For grown-ups: Heard the “t” sound at the start of Tilly, Tax and Toad, and found another t word._

**Sparks**: BEST GAME EVER!  
_Tilly laughs so much she lies on the floor._  
_Then she stops. She looks at one corner of the map._  
_It’s coloured grey. So hard, it went right through the paper._  

#### `s14-sparks`

_Next morning, Sparks is at your gate. Being quiet. Sparks is never quiet!_  
**Sparks**: The dragon in the corner of the map keeps getting bigger.  
**Sparks**: Last night it got a cardigan. It’s Hazel. Of course it’s Hazel.  
**Sparks**: Hazel went away on a long trip. She didn’t say goodbye.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): missing someone: two feelings at once  
> **Sparks**: Tilly misses Hazel. She feels sad and cross at the same time.  
> _Missing someone can feel like that. Both feelings are all right._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that missing someone can make us feel sad and cross at once, and that both are all right._

**Sparks**: Hazel left Tilly a note. She won’t open it. It’s in her welly.  
**Sparks**: Go and find her. She likes you. You’re not a sandwich.  

#### `s14-gate`

_You hear a whistle. Low, then going up high. Wheee-oo!_  

> **Lesson** (music, do): low and high sounds  
> _(Make the supper call with your voice: start low, then slide up high! Wheee-oo!)_  
> _Low… then high! That’s how Hazel did it._  
> Check: `edu:sing:supper-call:low-high`
> _For grown-ups: Made a sound that slides from low to high, like Hazel’s supper whistle._

_Every Nubbin in the Grove looks up._  
_Tilly sits on the gate that sticks, kicking it._  
**Tilly**: They think it’s supper. It isn’t. I just do it.  
**Tilly**: Hazel taught me. She said whoever loves you comes running.  
_The Nubbins sit in a row in front of her wellies._  
**Tilly**: See. It works on them.  

**Choices**

- **Ask her about Hazel.**
    - **Tilly**: I’m not talking about her.
    - A Nubbin licks her welly.
    - **Tilly**: She didn’t say goodbye. You’re supposed to say goodbye!
    - She takes out the note. She looks at it. She puts it back.
    - **Tilly**: Come and play. I’ve got a dragon to do.
- **Ask her about the dragon.**
    - **Tilly**: She’s the Grey Grandmother. Very old. Very rude.
    - **Tilly**: She flew away without saying goodbye. She keeps biscuit tins.
    - **Tilly**: She’s the last boss. Come on.

#### `s14-lair`

_The last game. Curtains shut. A torch under a bowl, for spooky light._  
**Tilly**: The Lair of the Grey Grandmother. First, get past her geese!  
**Sparks**: I HIT THE GOOSE WITH MY SANDWICH!  
**Tilly**: Roll. LOTS! The goose eats the sandwich. Now it’s your friend.  
_Then you roll. LOTS again! Another goose is your friend._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): add 1 more  
> **Tilly**: One friendly goose. Then one more. How many geese?  
> - ✅ 2 → Two geese! One and one more make two. Honk, honk!
> - 1 → One… and one more! That makes two. Honk, honk!
> - 4 → Not that many! One and one more make two.
> _For grown-ups: Added one goose and one more goose to make two._

**Byte**: Do I still have seven points?  
**Tilly**: Four. You said “do” in an “actually” voice.  

#### `s14-dragon`

_Tilly lifts a cereal box off the corner. Under it is the dragon._  
_She’s cut from a biscuit-tin lid. Grey, with a felt-tip cardigan._  
**Tilly**: The Grey Grandmother. She flew away. She’s the boss.  
**Sparks**: Right! I roll to hit the—  
_Tilly grabs the dice. Rrrip! One tears._  
**Tilly**: No! Nobody hits her. Nobody.  
_The kitchen goes very quiet. The rain has stopped._  
**Tilly**: I don’t know how it ends. Not if nobody can hit her.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): helping a friend who is upset  
> _Tilly is upset. She doesn’t know what to do._  
> _What can a friend do?_  
> - ✅ Sit close by, and wait → Yes. Sometimes the kindest thing is to sit close, and wait.
> - Run away → Running away leaves her on her own. Sitting close, and waiting, helps more.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about how to help an upset friend: stay close and wait with them._


**Choices**

- **“My hero puts down the sword and sits by the dragon.”**
    - **Tilly**: That’s not a move.
    - **Tilly**: …What for?
    - **You**: Hazel’s third rule. When you don’t know what to do, sit and wait.
- **Roll to see if your hero can sit down.**
    - You roll the torn die. It lands on the torn side. No number!
    - **Tilly**: That’s… not a number.
    - **You**: Then you decide. My hero would like to sit next to the dragon.
    - Tilly looks at the torn die for a long time.
    - **Tilly**: The dice says… the dragon says yes.
- **Ask the Game Mistress what the dragon wants.**
    - **Tilly**: She doesn’t WANT anything. She just WENT.
    - You don’t say anything. Tilly looks at the dragon for a long time.
    - **Tilly**: …She wants someone to sit with her. Fine. FINE.

#### `s14-wait`

_You move your button to the dragon’s square. You leave it there._  
**Tilly**: She doesn’t do anything. She just sits there.  
**You**: That’s all right. We’re waiting.  
_Sparks puts a button next to yours. Then Pixel. Then Byte._  
**Byte**: Is there a rule against it?  
**Tilly**: …No.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 4  
> _(Count the buttons round the dragon: one, two, three, four.)_  
> _Four friends, sitting and waiting together._  
> Check: `edu:count:buttons:4`
> _For grown-ups: Counted four buttons waiting beside the dragon._

_A Nubbin taps the window. It hopes it’s supper._  

#### `s14-tears`

**Tilly**: The dragon says—  
_Tilly picks up the dragon. She holds it in both hands._  
**Tilly**: She says she had something important to do.  
**Tilly**: She didn’t say goodbye, because she would have cried.  
**Tilly**: I don’t know what else she says. She didn’t SAY!  
_Tilly cries with her whole face, all at once._  
_Sparks comes round the table and hugs her. Not a word._  
_Pixel passes a tea towel. Byte passes a better one._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): crying is all right; comfort helps  
> _It’s all right to cry. Crying lets the sad out._  
> _A quiet hug, and friends nearby, help a lot._  
> _For grown-ups: Saw friends comfort someone who was crying, and heard that crying is all right._

_You keep your button right where it is._  

#### `s14-complete`

**Tilly**: Game over. Snacks for everyone. That was always a rule.  
_Crisps, Fern’s burnt biscuits, and grapes the Nubbins already found._  
_Tilly eats with the dragon in her lap._  
**Tilly**: The Grey Grandmother isn’t the boss now. You can visit her.  
**Tilly**: And Byte gets all his points back. For the tea towel.  
_She puts her cardboard crown on your head. GAME MISTRESS!_  
_Later, you look at the map. The grey corner is coloured yellow now._  

> **Lesson** (art, ask): naming colours  
> _The corner was grey. What colour is it now?_  
> - ✅ Yellow → Yellow! A bright, warm colour, like sunshine.
> - Grey → It was grey. Now Tilly has coloured it yellow, bright as sunshine.
> - Green → The bog is green! The corner is yellow now.
> _For grown-ups: Named the colour yellow, and saw how Tilly changed a sad grey corner into a bright one._

_It says: HERE BE HAZEL. SHE IS ALLOWED._  
_Out by the gate, someone practises the supper call. Wheee-oo!_  

## S20. Fort Night

- `s20-step1`: Round one: gather snow and sticks, and build your wall. · _Put lots of children on gathering at the start. Wall first!_
- `s20-step2`: Round two: send Zip to peek at the other fort. · _Hide Zip behind the snowdrifts. Run when the lookout turns away._
- `s20-step3`: Round three: send the snow-fighters that beat theirs. · _Speedy beats Splashy. Splashy beats Sturdy. Sturdy beats Speedy._
- `s20-step4`: Build one big shelter, and get every child inside. · _Count everyone. Wren is little and easy to miss._

#### `s20-declaration`

_Deep, squeaky snow. Every child in the valley is out in it._  
_A card on your gate says: WAR! FORT NIGHT! BRING MITTENS!_  
_Sparks’s cousin has come for the holidays. And there is snow!_  
**Tilly**: You lead one fort. I lead the other. I’m going to win!  
**Pixel**: I’m the referee. I wrote the rules. Forty-one pages.  
**Pixel**: Three kinds of snow-fighter: Speedy, Splashy and Sturdy.  
**Pixel**: Speedy beats Splashy. Splashy beats Sturdy. Sturdy beats Speedy!  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, ask): what comes next in a pattern  
> **Pixel**: It goes round and round, like a pattern.  
> **Pixel**: Speedy, Splashy, Sturdy, Speedy, Splashy… what comes next?  
> - ✅ Sturdy → Sturdy! Then the pattern goes round again.
> - Speedy → Speedy comes after Sturdy. Listen: Speedy, Splashy, STURDY.
> - Gordon → Ha! Gordon isn’t in the pattern. Speedy, Splashy, Sturdy!
> _For grown-ups: Continued a repeating pattern of three._

_Bonk! A snowball hits a small boy called Gordon._  
**Gordon**: I’m all right!  
**Wren**: Commander! I’m Wren! What’s our battle cry?  

**Choices**

- **“FOR THE FORT!”**
    - **Wren**: FOR THE FORT!
    - **Pixel**: Classic. Approved.
- **“FOR WREN’S NAN’S GOOD MITTENS!”**
    - **Wren**: FOR MY NAN’S GOOD MITTENS!
    - **Wren**: She knitted them. They have thumbs!
- **“REMEMBER GORDON!”**
    - **Gordon**: I’m right here.
    - Everyone shouts it anyway. Then a snowball hits Gordon.

#### `s20-round1`

_Everyone gathers snow and sticks. Pat, pat, pat!_  
_Every time you point at Wren, she salutes._  
**Wren**: Yes, commander! Yes, commander! That’s nine times. I’m only little!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): saying sorry and thank you  
> _Wren is cross. She has been asked nine times!_  
> _What could you say to Wren?_  
> - ✅ “Sorry, Wren. Thank you for helping!” → Oh! You’re welcome, commander. Sorry and thank you make friends feel good.
> - “Faster, Wren!” → Hmm. Wren is only little. Let’s try: sorry, Wren, and thank you! Oh! You’re welcome, commander.
> _For grown-ups: Chose kind words, sorry and thank you, for a helper who felt cross._

**Pixel**: Rule: don’t hit the snowman. He has feelings. He’s called Derek.  
_The whistle blows. Your wall is short and fat and lumpy._  
_Their wall is tall and thin, with pointy bits on top._  
**Tilly**: I put everyone on snow from the start!  
**Pixel**: Round one: a draw. Both of them are walls.  

#### `s20-scout`

**Pixel**: Round two: peeking. You can’t win if you can’t see.  
_Sparks lends you a fast little Nubbin called Zip._  
_Zip wiggles in your mittens. Snow melts off his ears. Drip!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS2-3, ask): snow is frozen water and melts when warm  
> **Pixel**: Why does the snow melt on your mitten?  
> - ✅ My hand is warm → Yes! Snow is frozen water. Warm it up, and it turns back into water.
> - Zip is magic → Zip is fast, not magic! Your warm hand melts the snow back into water.
> _For grown-ups: Explained that snow is frozen water that melts when it gets warm._

**Pixel**: Send Zip round their fort. Count what they’ve got.  
**Gordon**: Can I go?  
**Pixel**: No, Gordon. You’d get hit.  
**Gordon**: That’s fair.  

#### `s20-intel`

_Zip runs back. He has snow on his eyebrows._  
_He runs round you nine times. That means: news!_  
_Their wall is big. But behind it, hardly anyone._  
_Three children went home. A lost mitten. A lost boot. Teatime._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): take away within 5  
> **Pixel**: Five children were behind their wall. Three went home. How many are left?  
> - ✅ 2 → Two! Five take away three leaves two. (Show five fingers. Fold three down. Two left!)
> - 3 → Three went home. Let’s use fingers: show five, fold three down… two left!
> - 5 → Some went home, so there are fewer now. Five take away three leaves two.
> _For grown-ups: Took three away from five, using fingers, to find two._

**Tilly**: We’re FINE! We have LOADS of people!  
**Pixel**: Rule: children are not bricks.  

**Choices**

- **Charge now, while there are only a few. It’s a snowball game!**
    - **Wren**: CHARGE! Yes, commander!
    - Your fort runs across the snow. Whoosh!
    - **Tilly**: That’s not FAIR!
    - **Tilly**: …That’s just what I would have done.
    - **Pixel**: Round two to your fort.
- **Wait till their three come back. Beating half a fort isn’t winning.**
    - Zip takes them a snowball with a smiley face. It means: we’ll wait.
    - Soon a mitten, a boot and a full tummy come back.
    - **Tilly**: You waited for us? That’s nice. Or sneaky.
    - **Pixel**: Round two: a draw. Being fair gets a point. New rule!

#### `s20-counters`

**Pixel**: Round three. The last one.  
**Pixel**: Look at what they send. Send what beats it.  
_Over the ditch, big children roll big snowballs. Sturdy ones!_  
**Tilly**: GIVE UP OR GET ROLLED!  
_Your fort shouts back: “FOR DEREK!” “I’M BRAVE, NOT COLD!”_  
_Above the hill, the sky goes dark grey, like a pigeon._  
_Nobody looks at it. Only Pixel._  
**Pixel**: Hm.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, notice): dark clouds can mean a storm  
> **Pixel**: Dark grey clouds can mean a storm is coming.  
> **Pixel**: When the sky changes, it’s good to look up and notice.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that dark grey clouds can be a sign of a storm._


#### `s20-blizzard`

_You’re nearly at their flag. Then the snow goes sideways!_  
_White everywhere. You can’t see their fort. You can’t see yours._  
**Pixel**: STOP! Everybody stop! This is a real blizzard!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): keeping safe in a storm  
> **Pixel**: In a big storm: stop, stay together, and find a grown-up.  
> _(Hold hands with your grown-up. Stay together!)_  
> Check: `edu:hold:hands:together`
> _For grown-ups: Learned what to do in a storm: stop, stay together and find a grown-up._

_Somebody small is crying. You can’t tell who._  
**Tilly**: Our wall fell in! I can’t find everyone!  
_Your wall still stands. Short, fat and lumpy._  
_But it’s too small for everybody._  

#### `s20-merge`

_Through the snow you can see children from both forts._  
_They stand about, like dropped gloves._  
**Pixel**: Referee’s rule…  
**Pixel**: I don’t have one. You’ve got a wall. They’ve got snow.  
**Tilly**: Is this a trick?  
**You**: No. It’s a plan. One fort, for everyone.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): working together  
> _Working together, everyone can build something big._  
> _(Ask: what do you like to build with a friend? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Saw two teams join up to build one shelter, and talked about building things with friends._


**Choices**

- **Kick open your wall and shout them all in.**
    - You kick a gap. “IN! EVERYONE IN!”
    - Your fort pulls the others in, by their scarves.
- **Carry your wall to them, snowball by snowball.**
    - Your team is good at moving snow fast. You taught them!
    - That’s what it was for, all along.
- **Meet in the middle. Both walls, one shelter.**
    - Both forts bring snow. Both bring sticks.
    - The catapult is the roof. Derek the snowman is the door.

#### `s20-count`

**Pixel**: Head count!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 20  
> _(Count everyone in with Pixel: one, two, three… all the way to twenty!)_  
> **Pixel**: Twenty. Everyone’s inside.  
> Check: `edu:count:children:20`
> _For grown-ups: Counted to twenty to check every child was safe inside._

**Pixel**: …Everyone. Even Gordon.  
**Gordon**: I got hit by a whole sky!  
_Inside it’s blue and snug. It smells of wet wool._  
**Tilly**: That bit of the roof is mine.  
**Wren**: Commander? Which fort won?  
_Outside, the wind stops. Someone pokes a hole to look out._  

#### `s20-complete`

_The sky is clear and black. So many stars!_  
_Both forts lie in one fort, looking up._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-ESS1-1, notice): stars are there in the day too  
> **Pixel**: Stars are in the sky in the daytime too.  
> **Pixel**: The sun is so bright, we just can’t see them.  
> _(Look for stars tonight, with your grown-up!)_  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that stars are always in the sky, but we only see them when the sun has gone._

**Wren**: That one’s a mitten. That one’s Gordon. That one’s Derek!  
**Pixel**: Final rule: both forts win. I wrote it in pen.  
**Tilly**: So what was it all for? The snow and the peeking?  
**You**: So we could build one fort fast, when it mattered.  
**Tilly**: …That’s a really good answer.  
_Up at the cottage, someone in a blanket waves from the window._  
_Everybody waves back._  
**Pixel**: Prizes! A commander’s hat. It’s a tea cosy.  

## S21. CL-4NK

- `s21-step1`: Show Clank round the Grove. · _Go round things, not over them. He has only one wheel._
- `s21-step2`: Tidy up after Clank’s helping. · _Dry the cat. Prop up the tree. Then try to catch Clank!_
- `s21-step3`: Find Clank. · _Follow his wheel-track through the herbs._
- `s21-step4`: Help Byte build a ramp for the step. · _Byte measures. You saw. Measure twice!_
- `s21-step5`: Stay with Clank until the sun goes down. · _Feed the Nubbins first. Then sit by the step and just be there._

#### `s21-unveil`

_Squeak, squeak! Pixel pushes a wheelbarrow up the lane._  
**Pixel**: I had spare lantern parts. So I made a garden helper!  
_A lantern head. A glass tummy. A brass nose. One wheel._  
_Pixel winds the nose. Wheee! He whistles like a happy kettle._  
**Clank**: HELLO! I am CL-4NK! Call me Clank!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: c  
> **Clank**: Clank! C-c-clank! What else starts with that sound?  
> - ✅ Cat → C-c-cat! Clank and cat start the same!
> - Dog → D-d-dog starts with d. C-c-cat starts like Clank!
> - Sun → S-s-sun starts with s. C-c-cat starts like Clank!
> _For grown-ups: Heard the first sound of “Clank” and matched it with “cat”._

**Clank**: You are my BEST FRIEND!  
**You**: We’ve only just—  
_He rolls forward. Off the step. Bump! Into the herbs._  
**Clank**: Announcement! I have found the step!  
**Pixel**: That’s one.  

#### `s21-tour`

**Clank**: Announcement: this is a POND! This is a GATE! Best friend!  
_Soon every Nubbin follows him in a line. Like ducklings!_  
_One climbs on his head. Then three more._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): add 3 more  
> _One Nubbin, then three more. How many on Clank?_  
> - ✅ 4 → FOUR! One and three more make four!
> - 3 → Count them: one… then two, three, four. Four!
> - 2 → More than that! One and three more make four.
> _For grown-ups: Added one and three more to make four._

_He turns his light down low for them. They purr._  
**Clank**: They like me. Announcement: they LIKE me.  
**Clank**: I didn’t know that could happen.  

#### `s21-help`

_At dawn, a whistle wakes you. WHEEEE!_  
**Clank**: GOOD MORNING! I have done SO MUCH HELPING!  
_Fern holds her cat, Mustard. He is soaking wet and very cross._  
**Fern**: He watered my cat. To be fair, Mustard was in the beans.  
**Clank**: Cats are plants that run! I think!  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what plants and animals need  
> _Do cats need watering, like plants?_  
> - ✅ No! Cats drink from a bowl → Right. Plants drink through their roots. Cats lap water from a bowl.
> - Yes → Ha! No. Plants need watering. Cats drink from a bowl. And they hate baths.
> _For grown-ups: Sorted out what plants need (watering) from what cats need (a drink from a bowl)._

_And Fern’s best apple tree is cut into the shape of a duck._  
**Clank**: And I rescued a little old one! He was stuck up the hill!  
_Round the pond zooms Clank, holding the little grey one like a pie._  

#### `s21-moss`

_Clank reaches the cottage. And the step. Bump!_  
_The little grey one flies out of his hands. He lands on his feet._  
**Clank**: Announcement… I found the step again.  
_He gives you a long, long look._  
_Then he walks all the way back up the hill. Slowly._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): asking before we pick someone up  
> _Clank picked up the little grey one without asking. He didn’t like it._  
> _What should we do before we pick someone up?_  
> - ✅ Ask first → Yes! Always ask first. Then they can say yes or no.
> - Just grab them → He didn’t like being grabbed. We always ask first, and listen.
> _For grown-ups: Learned to ask before picking up a pet or a person, and to listen to the answer._

**Pixel**: That’s two.  

#### `s21-log`

_Byte comes with a clipboard and a pencil behind each ear._  
**Byte**: One wet cat. One duck-shaped tree. One old Nubbin, zoomed.  
**Byte**: He means to help. But next time, someone could get hurt.  
**Byte**: Pixel should switch Clank off. Tonight.  
_Behind you, a little whistle starts, then stops._  
_Clank heard. His light goes very dim._  
_He rolls backwards, off the step. Bump. He doesn’t say a thing._  
**Pixel**: …That’s three.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 3  
> _(Count Clank’s bumps with Pixel, on your fingers: one, two, three.)_  
> _Three bumps down the step. Poor Clank._  
> Check: `edu:count:falls:3`
> _For grown-ups: Counted Clank’s three bumps on their fingers, keeping track like Pixel._

_Then he’s gone. A wheel-track leads to the lantern shed._  
**Byte**: I didn’t know he could hear. I didn’t know he could… mind.  

**Choices**

- **Tell Byte he had a point. Clank does need to learn.**
    - **Byte**: I did have a point. It feels worse when it hurts someone.
    - **Pixel**: He needs a bit of slow. I can help with that.
- **Tell Byte that wasn’t fair. Clank is only days old.**
    - **Byte**: Days old. I forgot that.
    - **Byte**: I was days old once. I’m told I bit a vicar.

#### `s21-shed`

_The shed smells of old string. Clank is on a high shelf._  
_He’s pretending to be a lantern. But lanterns don’t shake._  
**Clank**: Announcement. I’m a lantern now. Nobody switches off a lantern.  
**Clank**: I watered a cat. I made a duck tree. I zoomed the old one.  
**Clank**: Am I a friend? Or just a thing that does jobs?  
**Clank**: If I stop being useful, will you still…  
_Click. Click. He looks for a word. He can’t find it._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): friends like you for who you are  
> _Friends like you for who you are. Not for the jobs you do._  
> _(Ask: what do you like about a friend of yours? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that friends like us for who we are, and named something they like about a friend._


**Choices**

- **Sit down below the shelf, and wait.**
    - You sit on the floor. It’s not comfy. You stay.
    - **Clank**: Is it a job?
    - **You**: No. I’m just here.
    - After a long time, Clank comes down. He parks beside you.
- **Teach him: it’s all right to ask for help.**
    - **You**: Do you know why you fall down the step?
    - **Clank**: Because I’m brave?
    - **You**: Because you never ask anyone to help.
    - **You**: Everybody needs a hand sometimes. Even me.
    - Clank thinks so hard, his whistle goes off, very softly.

#### `s21-ramp`

_Next day, Byte comes with a plank and a saw._  
**Byte**: I measured the step. It’s too tall for one wheel.  
**Byte**: So it isn’t Clank. It’s the step.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-2-ETS1-1, ask): solving a problem: a ramp helps wheels  
> **Byte**: How can a wheel get down a step safely?  
> - ✅ A ramp: a gentle slope → Yes. A ramp. The wheel rolls down slowly. No bump.
> - Jump off → Bump! That’s what keeps happening. A gentle slope is better: a ramp.
> - Fly → He hasn’t got wings. A ramp is a gentle slope he can roll down.
> _For grown-ups: Worked out that a ramp solves the problem of a wheel and a step._

**Byte**: Eleven Nubbins follow him everywhere. That matters.  
**Clank**: Byte! Does that mean you’re my best—  
**Byte**: Maybe.  

#### `s21-down`

_The ramp is done. Small, smooth and safe._  
**Clank**: Announcement. I am going down.  
**Clank**: Would somebody come with me? The first time?  
_Byte holds one side. You hold the other._  
_Down he goes. Slow as a snail. He reaches the bottom!_  
**Clank**: I DID THE STEP! And I didn’t fall!  
_He does a dance: forward, back, and a spin!_  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): moving our bodies: forward, back, spin  
> _(Do the Clank dance! Step forward. Step back. Spin round!)_  
> **Clank**: Announcement: you are a GREAT dancer!  
> Check: `edu:dance:clank:forward-back-spin`
> _For grown-ups: Did the Clank dance: a step forward, a step back and a spin._

_You copy it. Byte copies it too. Later, he says he didn’t._  
**Pixel**: That’s… none. I’ll take none.  

#### `s21-evening`

_The afternoon turns gold. Clank sits by the ramp._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-ESS1-1, notice): the sun is low in the evening  
> _In the evening, the sun sits low in the sky._  
> _The light goes gold, and shadows grow long._  
> _For grown-ups: Noticed that the evening sun is low, with golden light and long shadows._

_He doesn’t water anything. It’s very hard for him._  
**Clank**: Announcement: I am not helping. I am just… here.  
**Clank**: Is just here allowed?  
**You**: Just here is allowed.  
**Clank**: Announcement: just here is allowed.  

#### `s21-complete`

_At dusk the Nubbins come in from the long grass._  
_One by one, they climb onto Clank. He’s warm, like a kettle._  
_One sits on his wheel. One curls up inside his glass tummy._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 8  
> _(Count the Nubbins on Clank: one, two, three, four, five, six, seven, eight!)_  
> **Clank**: Eight friends. Announcement: that is LOTS.  
> Check: `edu:count:nubbins:8`
> _For grown-ups: Counted eight Nubbins snuggled on Clank._

**Clank**: Announcement.  
**Clank**: I am being very still.  
_He is. For a whole hour. You sit beside him._  
**Pixel**: Keep him. He’s yours. He’ll water things, slowly.  
_Next morning he waters the beans. One bean a minute._  

## Q1. Two Shrubberies

- `q01-s1`: Make a shrubbery and give it to the three hoods. · _The Workshop makes one from reeds and wood. Then use Give._
- `q01-s2`: Make a second shrubbery, a bit higher, with a tiny path. · _Make another shrubbery and a path. Put them next to the first one._

#### `q01-intro`

_Splash, splash goes the waterfall._  
_Three Nubbins in brown hoods stand on the path._  
_The middle one looks tall. It is standing on a log!_  
**The Hooded Three**: Boing-a-boing!  
_Your Nubbin jumps. A little bird flies away._  
**You**: Please may we go past?  
**The Tall Hood**: Not yet. First, we need something green. Something leafy. A… bush!  
_A shrubbery is a little bush. Green and leafy._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: sh  
> **The Tall Hood**: Shrubbery starts with a shh sound. Shh!  
> **The Tall Hood**: Which word starts with shh too?  
> - ✅ Shoe → Yes! Shh-oe. Shoe and shrubbery start the same.
> - Bird → Bird starts with a b sound. Shh-oe starts with shh, like shrubbery!
> - Log → Log starts with l. Listen: shh-oe. Shoe starts with shh!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the “sh” sound at the start of words._

_The other two nod. One nods a bit late._  

**Choices**

- **What kind of shrubbery?**
    - **The Tall Hood**: A nice one. Green, please.
    - Green, like leaves.
- **Chant back: Boing-a-boing!**
    - You shout it back, loud and silly.
    - The hoods look at each other. One writes it down.
- **One shrubbery, coming up!**
    - **The Tall Hood**: Good. We will wait here. Swaying.

#### `q01-first`

_You put the shrubbery down. Plop._  
_The hoods look at it. Top, sides, and underneath._  
**The Tall Hood**: It has leaves. We like the leaves.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, notice): what plants need  
> **The Tall Hood**: A shrubbery is alive. It is a plant.  
> **The Tall Hood**: Give it water and sunshine, and it will grow.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that plants need water and light to grow._

_They chant softly. It means thank you._  

#### `q01-second`

_You step towards the path. The tall hood holds up a paw._  
**The Tall Hood**: Wait. There is a second thing.  
**You**: You said one shrubbery!  
**The Tall Hood**: Now we would like two. Put the new one up higher.  
**The Tall Hood**: And a tiny path between them. Just right for a beetle.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): positions: higher and lower  
> **The Tall Hood**: The new shrubbery goes higher. Is higher up or down?  
> - ✅ Up! → Yes! Higher means up. Point up with me!
> - Down → Down is lower. Higher means up. Point up, up, up!
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised the position words higher and lower._

_One hood holds its paws a beetle apart._  
**The Tall Hood**: Also, we have a new name. It goes like this…  
_Clatter, bang, ting-a-ling! Like spoons falling down the stairs._  
**You**: I can’t say that!  
**The Tall Hood**: Nobody can. That is why it is so scary.  

**Choices**

- **OK. Higher, with a path.**
    - **The Tall Hood**: Good. Use little stones. We love little stones.
- **Can the path be pretend?**
    - **The Tall Hood**: No. We tried that. Someone fell off it.

#### `q01-terraced`

_Two shrubberies. One low, one a little higher._  
_A tiny stone path wiggles between them._  
_A beetle walks along it. It looks very pleased._  

> **Lesson** (science, do): insects have six legs  
> _A beetle is an insect. Insects have six legs._  
> _(Count its legs together: one, two, three, four, five, six!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:beetle-legs:6`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted a beetle’s legs and learned insects have six._

**The Tall Hood**: Oh. Oh, that is lovely.  

#### `q01-complete`

_The hoods step aside. Then they step back, to look again._  
**The Tall Hood**: You may pass. Every day, if you like.  
**You**: Will you stay here?  
**The Tall Hood**: We have a shrubbery and a path. Of course we stay.  
**The Tall Hood**: We guarded this path for years.  
**The Tall Hood**: A shrubbery is a much nicer reason to stand here.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): naming feelings  
> _The hoods are smiling at their shrubbery._  
> _How do they feel now?_  
> - ✅ Happy → Yes! Happy and proud. They love their garden.
> - Cross → Look at their smiles. They feel happy, not cross.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the hoods’ feeling from their words and faces._

_One hood gets a tiny rake. Scritch, scratch._  
_At dusk they practise their new name. Clatter, ting… oops!_  

## Q2. The Cardboard Knight

- `q02-s1`: Win a karate game against Sir Dentwhistle on the plank bridge. · _Choose Karate on the bridge. Any of your Nubbins can play._
- `q02-s2`: Win a second karate game. · _If your Nubbin is tired, swap in a fresh one._
- `q02-s3`: Win the last karate game. · _If your Nubbin keeps losing, let it rest first._

#### `q02-intro`

_A plank bridge goes over the stream._  
_In the middle stands a Nubbin in cardboard armour._  
_A cardboard helmet. Two arm guards. Two knee pads._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Count the cardboard pieces together.)_  
> _One helmet. Two arm guards. Two knee pads._  
> _One, two, three, four, five. Five pieces!_  
> Check: `edu:count:armour:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted five pieces of cardboard armour._

_A snail is slowly climbing up its leg._  
**You**: Hello! May we cross, please?  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: Nobody crosses my plank. Not one toe!  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: I am Sir Dentwhistle. I have guarded it for three whole days!  
_It holds up its little fists. Time for a karate game!_  

**Choices**

- **Bow politely, then play.**
    - **Sir Dentwhistle**: Polite! I shall beat you very politely.
- **Can’t we go round?**
    - There are stepping stones just down the stream.
    - **Sir Dentwhistle**: Nobody uses those. They are too low. Down there.

#### `q02-round1`

_Hi-yah! Your Nubbin does its best move._  
_Plonk! The cardboard helmet falls into the grass._  
_Sir Dentwhistle has very flat hair._  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): snails and their shells  
> _Look, the snail is still climbing. Slowly, slowly._  
> _A snail carries its shell on its back._  
> _When it is scared, it hides inside. Safe!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that a snail carries its shell, which keeps it safe._

**Sir Dentwhistle**: Pfft. I didn’t even feel it.  
**You**: It was your helmet!  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: A helmet is only a hat. Again!  

#### `q02-scarf`

_Sir Dentwhistle wraps on a long woolly scarf._  
_Round and round and round. Three times!_  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: My mum knitted this. Nothing gets past the scarf.  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: She meant the cold. But still.  

> **Lesson** (health, ask): dressing for the weather  
> _What is a woolly scarf for?_  
> - ✅ Keeping warm → Yes! Wool keeps your neck cosy when it is cold.
> - Eating → Ha! Not for eating. A scarf keeps you warm in cold weather.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about how a scarf keeps us warm in cold weather._

**You**: Has anyone tried?  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: That is not the point.  
_The snail reaches its shoulder. What a view!_  

#### `q02-round2`

_Hi-yah! Your Nubbin spins._  
_The scarf unwinds and floats off down the stream._  
_Clatter, clatter! Off fall both cardboard arm guards._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): take away 2  
> _Sir Dentwhistle had four pieces left. Two just fell off._  
> _(Hold up four fingers. Fold two down.)_  
> _How many pieces are left?_  
> - ✅ Two → Yes! Two knee pads left. Four take away two is two.
> - Three → Let’s count what’s left: knee pad, knee pad. One, two. Two!
> - None → Not yet! The two knee pads are still on. Two left.
> _For grown-ups: Your child took two away from four to find how many pieces were left._

**Sir Dentwhistle**: Barely bothered. I was too warm anyway.  
_Its little round glasses steam up. It is cross!_  

#### `q02-glasses`

_Sir Dentwhistle wipes its glasses on its tummy._  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: Last round. I still have my glasses and knee pads.  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: If I can’t see you, you can’t see me.  
**You**: That’s not how seeing works!  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): how we see  
> _We see with our eyes._  
> _Glasses help some people’s eyes see clearly._  
> _If you close your eyes, other people can still see you!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that we see with our eyes, and that glasses help some eyes._

**Sir Dentwhistle**: It is on my bridge.  
_Your Nubbin wants to win, then have a snack._  

**Choices**

- **Ask your Nubbin to be gentle.**
    - Your Nubbin nods. It will be kind.
- **Do your very best karate!**
    - Your Nubbin cracks its knuckles. It has no knuckles!

#### `q02-round3`

_Hi-yah! Clonk, clonk! Off fall both knee pads._  
_The glasses fly off too. The snail goes round them._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): zero  
> _Both knee pads fell off. How many cardboard pieces are left?_  
> - ✅ None! Zero! → Yes! Zero. All gone. Just a little Nubbin in a vest.
> - Two → Look: the knee pads are on the grass. None left on. Zero!
> _For grown-ups: Your child found that when every piece is gone, there are zero left._

**Sir Dentwhistle**: I am fine! Never better! Which one of you said that?  
_It is talking to a fence post._  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: Come back! I’m not done being brave!  
_The fence post does not move. Not one bit._  

#### `q02-complete`

_Sir Dentwhistle sits down on the plank. Flump._  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: You may cross. Not because you won. I just want a sit.  
_You pick up the cardboard helmet and hold it out._  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: Keep it. I have lots more. At home. In a box.  
_It does not have lots more. It keeps looking at the helmet._  
**Sir Dentwhistle**: Remember: losing is fine if you don’t mind. I never mind!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, ask): losing well  
> _Sir Dentwhistle lost. What can we do when we lose a game?_  
> - ✅ Take a breath and say “good game” → Yes! That helps everyone feel good.
> - Stamp and shout → It’s OK to feel cross when we lose. Then we take a big breath and say “good game”.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about what to do when we lose a game._

_You cross the plank in two big steps._  
_Now your Nubbins lose karate games with a smile._  

**Choices**

- **Shall we say we both did well?**
    - **Sir Dentwhistle**: Yes! I was just going to say that.
    - It shakes your hand. Then the fence post’s.
- **You lost three times, you know.**
    - Sir Dentwhistle is quiet. The snail is quiet too.
    - **Sir Dentwhistle**: …I know. I was there.
    - **Sir Dentwhistle**: Thank you for telling the truth. That helps.

## Q3. The Bridge of Three Questions

- `q03-s1`: Count all the Nubbins in your Grove, then go back to the bridge. · _Tap each Nubbin once. Look in the pond too!_
- `q03-s2`: Find out how fast a Fly Nubbin can carry a coconut. · _Get a coconut from the palm tree by the sea. Time a Fly Nubbin._

#### `q03-intro`

_By a rope bridge sits a very old Keeper._  
_His hood has moss on it. Maybe he does too!_  
**Old Gorrel**: Stop! Answer three questions to cross my bridge.  
**Old Gorrel**: Get one wrong, and… splash! Into the stream.  
_You look down. The water is shallow, full of happy ducks._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): animal sounds  
> _Listen to the ducks! What sound does a duck make?_  
> - ✅ Quack! → Yes! Quack, quack! (Quack together.)
> - Moo → Moo is a cow! A duck says quack, quack.
> - Baa → Baa is a sheep! A duck says quack, quack.
> _For grown-ups: Your child matched the duck to its sound._

**Old Gorrel**: Question one. How many Nubbins live in your Grove?  
**You**: Um. Let me check.  
**Old Gorrel**: Go and count. I am very good at waiting.  

**Choices**

- **Make a big guess.**
    - **Old Gorrel**: Hmm. Probably wrong. Go and count.
- **Go and count properly.**
    - **Old Gorrel**: Wise. The last guesser is still in the stream. He likes it.

#### `q03-count`

_You come back with the number._  
_Old Gorrel checks it on his sleeve._  
**Old Gorrel**: Right. Bother.  
**Old Gorrel**: Question two. What did your newest Nubbin eat for breakfast?  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, do): clapping syllables  
> _Let’s clap “breakfast”. Break-fast._  
> _(Clap twice together: break… fast!)_  
> _Two claps!_  
> Check: `edu:clap:syllables:breakfast`
> _For grown-ups: Your child clapped the two beats in “breakfast”._

**You**: Half a run fruit. The other half fell in the pond.  
**Old Gorrel**: Right. And very sad.  

#### `q03-third`

**Old Gorrel**: Question three.  
_He leans in. The ducks go quiet._  
**Old Gorrel**: How fast can a Fly Nubbin fly, carrying a coconut?  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): heavy and light  
> _Is a coconut heavy or light?_  
> - ✅ Heavy → Yes! A coconut is big and heavy. Heavy things are hard to carry.
> - Light → A feather is light. A coconut is heavy, with a hard shell.
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared heavy and light things._

**You**: I don’t know!  
**Old Gorrel**: Nor do I. Nobody has ever answered.  
**Old Gorrel**: Go and find out. I shall wait.  
_He sits back, smug as a cushion._  

#### `q03-timed`

_You time two Fly Nubbins._  
_The first one zooms across the pond. Whoosh!_  
_The second flies halfway, sits on the coconut, and has a think._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ask): opposites: fast and slow  
> _One Nubbin zoomed. One sat down. Which one was fast?_  
> - ✅ The zoomy one → Yes! Fast. The sitting one was slow. Fast and slow are opposites.
> - The sitting one → The sitting one stopped! The zoomy one was fast. The sitter was slow.
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the opposite words fast and slow._

_So the answer is: it depends on the Nubbin!_  

#### `q03-complete`

**Old Gorrel**: Well? How fast?  
**You**: Which Fly Nubbin? The zoomy one, or the sitting one?  
_Old Gorrel opens his mouth. Then he shuts it._  
**Old Gorrel**: I don’t know! Nobody ever asked me a question back!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): it is OK not to know  
> **Old Gorrel**: I don’t know the answer. And that is all right!  
> _Nobody knows everything. We can say “I don’t know”._  
> _Then we ask, or we find out together._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that nobody knows everything, and we can ask and find out._

_He steps back… and plops into the stream. Splash!_  
_Quack, quack, quack! say the ducks._  
**Old Gorrel**: I’m fine! It’s lovely in here! The bridge is yours!  
**Old Gorrel**: Being asked a question is rather nice.  
_He floats away, waving. His hood lands on the post._  

## Q4. Clip-Clop

- `q04-s1`: Find out where the coconut shells came from. · _Follow the path to the palm tree by the sea._
- `q04-s2`: Decide what happens to the coconut shells. · _Go back to the lookout wall and talk to the Run Nubbin._

#### `q04-intro`

_Clop. Clop-clop. Clop._  
_A Run Nubbin trots behind you, banging two coconut shells._  
_You stop. It stops. You walk. It clops!_  

> **Lesson** (music, do): copying a rhythm  
> _(Clap the clops together.)_  
> _Clop. Clop-clop. Clop. Your turn!_  
> Check: `edu:clap:rhythm:clop-clopclop-clop`
> _For grown-ups: Your child clapped back a simple clip-clop rhythm._

_Warden Ollery leans over the lookout wall._  
**Warden Ollery**: Coconut shells? Up here? How odd!  
**Warden Ollery**: Coconuts grow on palm trees, by the sea.  
**Warden Ollery**: There is only one palm tree here. Far away, on the beach.  
**Warden Ollery**: So how did they get up the hill?  
_The Run Nubbin clops twice. Clop, clop!_  

**Choices**

- **Ask the Run Nubbin.**
    - It clops. Then it points its whole body at the sea.
- **Does it matter?**
    - **Warden Ollery**: Everything matters up here. There’s not much else to look at.

#### `q04-gullwick`

_Under the palm tree lies a Fly Nubbin called Gullwick._  
_Its wings are very big, and very tired._  
**Gullwick**: Carried it. All the way. Uphill. Puff, puff.  
**Gullwick**: Dropped it on a rock. Crack! Two halves.  
**Gullwick**: Run Nubbin found them. Very happy. I am very tired.  
_Gullwick lies flat in the sand. Flump._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): saying thank you  
> _Gullwick worked very hard. What could we say?_  
> - ✅ Thank you! → Oh. Nice. Very tired. Still nice.
> - Hurry up! → Gullwick is tired. A “thank you” would help it feel better.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about thanking someone who worked hard._


**Choices**

- **Say a big thank you to Gullwick.**
    - Gullwick lifts one wing, just a little.
    - **Gullwick**: Nobody said that before. Nice.
- **Why carry it so far?**
    - **Gullwick**: It looked like it wanted to go up.
    - Maybe that explains everything.

#### `q04-return`

_Back to the wall you go. Clop, clop. Right behind you._  
**You**: A Fly Nubbin carried a coconut up from the beach!  
**Warden Ollery**: All that way? With those little wings?  
**Warden Ollery**: Maybe the shells should go back to the beach.  
_The Run Nubbin hugs the shells tight._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-1, notice): banging makes sound  
> _Clop! When two hard shells bang, they shake a tiny bit._  
> _That shaking makes the sound._  
> _Horses’ feet go clip-clop on a hard road, too._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that hitting two hard things makes them shake, and that makes sound._

_This is the sound it was born to make!_  

**Choices**

- **They’re yours. Keep them!**
    - The Run Nubbin trots in a happy circle. Clop-clop-clop!
- **Keep them, but share turns.**
    - It agrees. As long as its turn is first. And last.

#### `q04-decided`

_The Run Nubbin tucks the shells under its arm._  
_It gives you a long, happy look._  
**Warden Ollery**: That’s settled, then. Clop away, little one!  
_Clop. Clop-clop. Off it goes._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: c  
> _Clop and coconut start with the same sound. C-c-c!_  
> _Which word starts with c too?_  
> - ✅ Cat → Yes! C-c-cat. Clop, coconut, cat!
> - Sun → Sun starts with sss. Listen: c-c-cat. Cat starts like clop!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the hard “c” sound at the start of words._


#### `q04-complete`

_The Run Nubbin is called Trotwell. It clops all round the Grove._  
_Soon every Nubbin wants a go!_  
**Warden Ollery**: I like the noise. It keeps me company.  
_Trotwell teaches you to trot. Lots of knees!_  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): trotting on the spot  
> _(Stand up and trot on the spot!)_  
> _Knees up! Clop, clop, clop, clop, clop!_  
> Check: `edu:trot:knees-up:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child trotted on the spot with high knees._

_Now, when you run, you can sound like a horse!_  
_Far away, Gullwick sleeps on the warm sand._  
_Beside it: a fruit and a note. “Thank you. From the wall.”_  

## Q5. The Rabbit on the Ledge

- `q05-s1`: Ask Sparks to make something to win the rabbit’s friendship. · _Sparks is in the Workshop. Bring one sun fruit._
- `q05-s2`: Take the Hand Melon to the ledge. Count to three, then toss it gently. · _Tap Count on each beat: one, two, three. Then throw._

#### `q05-intro`

_High on the ledge, Nubbins crowd round something small and white._  
_It is a rabbit! Fluffy, and the size of a slipper._  
_It munches clover. Munch, munch._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what animals eat  
> _What does the rabbit like to eat?_  
> - ✅ Clover and grass → Yes! Rabbits eat plants. Grass, clover and leaves.
> - Biscuits → Ha! Rabbits eat plants. Grass, clover, leaves. Munch, munch.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that rabbits eat plants like grass and clover._

**Sir Rollo Brisket**: Stand back! My Nubbins practised karate all week!  
_That is Sir Rollo, with a cape and a cardboard crown._  
_His Nubbin steps up. Whoosh! Boing! Into a soft bush._  
_The next one. Whoosh! Boing! Same bush. It giggles._  
**Sir Rollo Brisket**: Everybody, back away! Quick!  
_Everyone hurries ten steps. The rabbit just eats its clover._  

**Choices**

- **Let your Nubbin try.**
    - Your Nubbin steps up. Whoosh! Boing! Into the bush.
    - It climbs out with a leaf on its head, giggling.
- **Watch the rabbit for a while.**
    - You notice something. The rabbit never starts a fight.
    - It just wants to eat its clover in peace.

#### `q05-sparks`

**Sparks**: A rabbit beat ALL of them? Oh, I love that rabbit!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first letter of a name: S  
> **Sparks**: My name starts with S. Sss, like a snake!  
> **Sparks**: What else starts with sss?  
> - ✅ Sun → Yes! Sss-un. Sun fruit and Sparks!
> - Rabbit → Rabbit starts with rrr. Sss-un starts with sss, like me!
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that Sparks starts with S and found another “sss” word._

**Sparks**: Leave it with me. One sun fruit, a ribbon, a little fizz.  
_Crackle! Flash! A smell of toasty fruit._  
**Sparks**: Ta-da! Nearly ready.  

#### `q05-melon`

**Sparks**: Here it is! The Hand Melon!  
_It is a sun fruit with a ribbon and a paper tag. It hums._  
**You**: It’s not a melon.  
**Sparks**: Melon sounds braver. Read the tag!  
_The tag says: Count to three. Then toss it, gently._  
**Sparks**: Not two. Not four. Three! Near the rabbit, not at it.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 3  
> **Sparks**: Let’s practise. Count with me!  
> _(Hold up one finger for each number.)_  
> _One… two… three! Toss!_  
> Check: `edu:count:beats:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted exactly three beats, one finger for each._

**Sparks**: It pops like a party. Nobody gets hurt.  

**Choices**

- **Practise counting first.**
    - **You**: One. Two. Three.
    - **Sparks**: Perfect! Just like that.
- **What if I count to four?**
    - **Sparks**: Then it just sits there. Four is too many!
- **Let Sir Rollo count.**
    - **Sir Rollo Brisket**: One… two… lots!
    - **Sparks**: THREE, Rollo!
    - Fizzle. Sparks makes another one.

#### `q05-pop`

_One. Two. Three. You toss the Hand Melon, gently._  
_Pop! Out bursts fruit confetti in every colour._  
_Red, yellow, green and blue bits drift down like snow._  

> **Lesson** (art, do): naming colours  
> _(Find the red bits. Point to them!)_  
> _Now find yellow. Now green. Now blue!_  
> Check: `edu:tap:colour:red`
> _For grown-ups: Your child found and named colours in the confetti._

_The rabbit sits up. Its ears stand tall._  

#### `q05-complete`

_The rabbit hops through the confetti, munching._  
_It stops at your feet and looks up at you._  
**Sir Rollo Brisket**: I had a whole plan. It had lots of shouting.  
**Sir Rollo Brisket**: But the rabbit only wanted its clover. We kept bothering it.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): giving animals space  
> _Animals need space and quiet, just like us._  
> _We watch gently, and let them come to us._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that animals need peace and space, and we let them come to us._

**Sparks**: I made you a spare Hand Melon. No fizz. Just pretty.  
_The rabbit follows you home. You call it Snowdrop._  
_It says yes by sitting on your foot._  

## Q6. The Wooden Rabbit

- `q06-s1`: Build a giant wooden rabbit at the Workshop. · _You need lots of wood and some wheels. Try the logs by the woods._
- `q06-s2`: Push the wooden rabbit to the stranger’s wall. · _Push from behind. Go round the stream; it doesn’t float._

#### `q06-intro`

_Far away stands an old wall with a little tower._  
_On top is a man with a huge curly moustache._  
**Sir Barnabus Parapet**: Hello down there! I do not like your hat!  
**You**: I’m not wearing a hat.  
**Sir Barnabus Parapet**: I do not like that either!  
_Splat! A squishy fruit lands on your head._  
**Sir Barnabus Parapet**: And your Nubbin looks like a sock with a secret!  
_Your Nubbin looks sad. You tell it it looks lovely._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): when someone is unkind  
> _The man is being unkind. What can we do?_  
> - ✅ Say “Please stop” and tell a grown-up → Yes. A strong, calm voice. And a grown-up to help.
> - Throw fruit back → That would make more mess and more cross feelings. We can say “Please stop” in a big, calm voice.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about what to do when someone is unkind: a calm “stop” and a grown-up._


**Choices**

- **Shout up: Please stop!**
    - **You**: Please stop! That’s not kind!
    - **Sir Barnabus Parapet**: Not kind? Hmm. Nobody ever told me.
- **Wipe off the fruit, calmly.**
    - You wipe it off, very calmly.
    - Splat. Another one.

#### `q06-built`

_The wooden rabbit is twice as tall as you!_  
_It has big flat ears and a worried face._  
_It has four round wheels._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, ask): circles  
> _What shape are the wheels?_  
> - ✅ Circles → Yes! Wheels are circles. Round things roll.
> - Squares → Squares have corners, so they won’t roll. Wheels are circles!
> - Triangles → Triangles have three pointy corners. Wheels are round circles!
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the wheels as circles and heard why round things roll._

_There is a little door in its tummy. Nobody knows why._  

#### `q06-plan`

_The wooden rabbit waits outside the Workshop._  
**You**: We push it to his wall and leave it at the gate.  

> **Lesson** (science, ask): pushing moves things  
> _We stand behind the rabbit. To make it go forwards, we…_  
> - ✅ Push! → Yes! Push from behind. Heave… roll! Heave… roll!
> - Pull → Pulling from behind brings it back to us! We push from behind.
> _For grown-ups: Your child worked out that pushing from behind moves the cart forwards._

_Your Nubbins nod. They know it is a plan._  
_They don’t know what the plan is._  
_Neither do you!_  
**You**: He’ll take it in… and then… something happens.  
_It has wheels. That is a sort of plan._  

#### `q06-gate`

_You leave the rabbit at the gate. You hide behind a bush._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): position: behind  
> _Where are you hiding?_  
> - ✅ Behind the bush → Yes! Behind the bush, where he can’t see us.
> - On top of the bush → On top, he would see us! We are behind the bush.
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the position word “behind”._

_Creak! The man drags the rabbit inside._  
_Behind the bush, everyone looks at everyone._  
**You**: Wait… was somebody meant to be inside it?  
_Your Nubbins look at their feet._  
_One looks at a different bush._  

**Choices**

- **Start laughing.**
    - You laugh. Your Nubbins laugh.
    - Behind the wall, the man laughs too.
- **Knock and ask for it back.**
    - **Sir Barnabus Parapet**: What rabbit? I am just a man with a wall.

#### `q06-complete`

_Next morning, the rabbit is back! A note hangs on its ear._  
_“Too big for my yard. Nobody inside. Nine out of ten for ears.”_  
_Then, very small: “Nobody ever visits my wall. Please come again.”_  
_Maybe he was lonely all along._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): understanding others’ feelings  
> _Nobody visits his wall. How do you think he felt?_  
> - ✅ Lonely → Yes. Sometimes lonely people act grumpy. A friendly visit can help.
> - Very busy → He says nobody visits. I think he felt lonely.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how the man on the wall might have felt._

_The little tummy door swings open._  
_You and your Nubbin squeeze in and peek out. Perfect for photos!_  

## Q7. The Little Castle

- `q07-s1`: Find 12 stones for the castle towers. · _Look in the riverbed and under the cliffs. Tap a sparkly rock._
- `q07-s2`: Build the castle towers with Pixel. · _Put stones on the glowing spots. Six on each tower._

#### `q07-intro`

_On the little hill, Pixel kneels by a castle._  
_It is as tall as your knee._  
_It has a lolly-stick drawbridge and two short towers._  
**Pixel**: Look, everyone! The Grand Palace of the Hill!  
_Byte walks past with a cup of tea._  
**Byte**: Hmm. It’s smaller than a sheep.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): big and small  
> _Which is bigger: a real castle, or Pixel’s castle?_  
> - ✅ A real castle → Yes! Real castles are huge. Pixel’s is small.
> - Pixel’s castle → Pixel’s castle only comes up to your knee. Real castles are much bigger!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared the size of a model castle and a real one._

**Pixel**: SHH!  
**Pixel**: It needs stone towers. Will you help?  
**Byte**: I am drinking my tea.  

**Choices**

- **Of course I’ll help!**
    - **Pixel**: Thank you! Byte, write that down. Somebody believes!
- **It is quite small.**
    - **Pixel**: It is SMALL ON PURPOSE!
    - **Byte**: It is just small.

#### `q07-stone`

_You bring Pixel an armful of stones._  
_Pixel holds each one up, like treasure._  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-PS1-1, ask): hard and soft materials  
> _Is stone hard or soft?_  
> - ✅ Hard → Yes! Stone is hard and strong. Good for castles.
> - Soft → A pillow is soft. Stone is hard. Knock, knock!
> _For grown-ups: Your child sorted stone as hard and strong, good for building._

**Pixel**: This one is a tower. This one is a wall.  
**Pixel**: And this one is just a nice stone. Keep it!  

#### `q07-towers`

**Pixel**: Now the towers. Don’t breathe on them!  
_You build together. Your Nubbin hands up stones, one by one._  
**Pixel**: Six stones for this tower. Six for that one.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 6  
> _(Count the stones on one tower together.)_  
> _One, two, three, four, five, six!_  
> **Pixel**: Six! And six more for the other one.  
> Check: `edu:count:stones:6`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted six stones onto one tower._

**Byte**: You moved that one back where it was.  
**Pixel**: I moved it MORE where it was.  
_Up, up go the towers. Past your knee!_  

#### `q07-finished`

_The last stone goes on. Two tall towers!_  
_A flag made from a sweet wrapper flaps on top._  
**Pixel**: Grove! The Grand Palace is FINISHED!  
_The hills call back: “…ished!”_  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): echoes  
> _That is an echo._  
> _Pixel’s voice bounced off the hills and came back._  
> _(Try it: say “hello” and listen!)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that an echo is a sound bouncing back off hills or walls._

_Pixel thinks that is clapping._  

#### `q07-complete`

_That evening, three Nubbins come up the hill._  
_One puts a leaf on its head, like a crown._  
_One stands on the drawbridge and points. Ta-da!_  
_One lies down and plays a dragon. Or a cloud._  
**Pixel**: They’re putting on a play. In my castle!  
**Byte**: Still quite sm…  
_Byte stops. He watches the little crown take a bow._  
**Byte**: It’s a very good castle.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): kind words  
> _Byte changed his words. Which words are kinder?_  
> - ✅ “It’s a very good castle.” → Thank you, Byte! Kind words make friends feel proud.
> - “It’s quite small.” → That might make Pixel sad. “It’s a very good castle” is kinder.
> _For grown-ups: Your child picked the kinder of two things to say about a friend’s work._


## Q8. The Water Bridge

- `q08-s1`: Build a water bridge from the falls to your crops. · _Make water-bridge pieces at the Workshop. Lay them from the falls to the crops._
- `q08-s2`: Pick a crop and take it to the meeting. · _Ripe crops glow. Pick one and give it to Reggie._

#### `q08-intro`

_In the Keepers’ Hall, there is a meeting._  
_Three Keepers. Four chairs. Lots of paper._  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: Order! First thing: all this building!  
**Frannie Quill**: Fences. Paths. Something with a drawbridge.  
**Old Mags**: My cousin tripped on a fence post. A new one!  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: What good has any of it done us?  
**Frannie Quill**: Reggie, you are sitting on the Keeper’s bench.  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: If you must build, build something useful!  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: The crops are thirsty. My back hurts from carrying buckets.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what plants need  
> _The crops are thirsty. What do plants need to grow?_  
> - ✅ Water and sunlight → Yes! Plants need water and sunlight to grow.
> - Socks → Ha! No socks. Plants need water and sunlight.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named water and sunlight as things plants need._


**Choices**

- **I’ll build a water bridge!**
    - **Reggie Thistlewood**: A what?
    - **You**: A bridge that carries water.
    - **Reggie Thistlewood**: Hmph. More building.
- **Can I read your meeting notes?**
    - The notes say: “Met. Grumbled. Biscuits.”

#### `q08-water`

_Click! The last piece goes in._  
_Water slides down from the falls. Whoosh!_  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): water flows downhill  
> _Water flows downhill._  
> _The falls are high. The crops are low._  
> _So down it goes! Whoosh!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that water always flows from high places down to low ones._

_Splish, splash, into the crop beds._  
_The crops droop… then perk up. Ahh!_  
_Your Nubbins jump in the spray._  

#### `q08-invite`

_A note arrives, folded very small._  
_“Meeting! Bring a crop to look at. From Reggie.”_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): first letters of names  
> _Reggie starts with the letter R. Rrr!_  
> _(Draw an R in the air with your finger.)_  
> Check: `edu:tap:letter:r`
> _For grown-ups: Your child found the first letter of Reggie’s name._

_Under it: “Bring biscuits too. Frannie.”_  
_Another note: “Ignore that. MY meeting is the real one. Mags.”_  
_By lunch there are three meetings!_  
_The crop beds by the water are plump and shiny._  

#### `q08-delivered`

_Reggie takes the crop. He turns it over._  
_He sniffs it. He holds it up to the light._  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: Hmm. It’s very good.  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: I don’t like how good it is.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): saying “you were right”  
> _It can be hard to say “that’s good” when you were grumpy._  
> _Reggie said it anyway. Well done, Reggie!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that admitting someone else was right can be hard, and is a good thing to do._


#### `q08-complete`

**Reggie Thistlewood**: Fine. The water bridge. What has it done for us?  
**Frannie Quill**: The Nubbins love the spray.  
**Old Mags**: Frogs moved in. I like frogs.  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-LS3-1, notice): frogs and tadpoles  
> **Old Mags**: Baby frogs are called tadpoles. They swim with tails.  
> **Old Mags**: Then they grow legs, and hop out!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that baby frogs are tadpoles that grow legs._

**Frannie Quill**: It hums at night. Very calming.  
**Old Mags**: My cousin can’t trip on it. It’s up high!  
**Frannie Quill**: And Reggie fills his teapot from it every day.  
**Reggie Thistlewood**: …All right. Hands up to thank the Keeper.  
_Up go the hands. Reggie’s goes up last, slowly._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 3  
> _(Count the hands together.)_  
> _Frannie, Mags, Reggie. One, two, three!_  
> Check: `edu:count:hands:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted the hands raised in the meeting._

_The crops will never be thirsty again._  

## Q9. The Gourd and the Shoe

- `q09-s1`: Go back for your shoe. · _It’s by the ditch on the long path. The crowd will follow you._
- `q09-s2`: Help the gourd group and the shoe group stop arguing. · _Wear the gourd and the shoe where both groups can see._

#### `q09-intro`

_On your evening walk, your bag strap snaps._  
_A little gourd rolls into the ditch. Bump, bump._  
_You reach for it. Your shoe comes off in the mud. Schlurp!_  
_You are tired. You leave them both for later._  
_You look back. Nubbins are gathering round them._  
_Now they follow you. Tiptoe, tiptoe._  
**Barty**: It is a sign!  
**You**: It’s a gourd. And a shoe.  
**Barty**: TWO signs!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): rhyming words  
> _Shoe… two! Those words rhyme. They end the same._  
> _Which word rhymes with shoe too?_  
> - ✅ Moo → Yes! Shoe, two, moo. They all rhyme!
> - Gourd → Gourd doesn’t end like shoe. Listen: sh-oo, m-oo. Moo rhymes!
> _For grown-ups: Your child found that “shoe” and “two” rhyme._


**Choices**

- **Please stop following me.**
    - They stop. Then they all take one tiny step.
- **Walk faster.**
    - They walk faster too.
    - One holds the gourd up high, like a lantern.

#### `q09-split`

_At the ditch, the crowd has split in two._  
_One group bows to the shoe on a rock._  
_The other group holds up the gourd and hums._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> _Five Nubbins by the shoe. Three by the gourd._  
> _Which group has more?_  
> - ✅ The shoe group → Yes! Five is more than three.
> - The gourd group → Let’s count. Shoe: five. Gourd: three. Five is more!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared two groups and found which had more._

**A Gourd Nubbin**: The gourd is round, like the sun. Round is best.  
**A Shoe Nubbin**: The shoe has laces! Follow the laces!  
_A tiny third group sits round a pebble._  

#### `q09-mother`

_A Keeper in a headscarf marches up. It’s Mother Brindle!_  
**Mother Brindle**: What’s all this? Barty! Is that you at the front?  
**Barty**: Mum! The signs!  
**A Gourd Nubbin**: Barty saw them first. Barty is very special.  
**Mother Brindle**: He’s just my cheeky Barty. And it’s past his bedtime!  

> **Lesson** (health, ask): why we sleep  
> _Why do we need to go to bed?_  
> - ✅ Sleep helps our bodies rest and grow → That’s right. Sleep is good for everyone.
> - To hide from socks → Ha! No. Sleep helps our bodies rest and grow.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about why sleep matters for growing bodies._

**Barty**: I am a bit cheeky.  
**Mother Brindle**: Keeper, please help. Nobody listens to me.  

**Choices**

- **Tell them both are signs.**
    - Both groups cheer. Then they frown. Then they cheer again.
- **Tell them you just dropped them.**
    - Nobody believes you. Mother Brindle sighs.
- **Tell them to think for themselves.**
    - **You**: You don’t have to follow me. You can think for yourselves!
    - Everybody nods at once. Then they wait for you to say what to think.
    - **Barty**: What should we think first?
    - Mother Brindle puts her face in her hands.

#### `q09-hats`

_You put the gourd on your head._  
_Then you balance the shoe on top of the gourd._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): positions: on top, under  
> _Where is the shoe?_  
> - ✅ On top of the gourd → Yes! On top. Wobble, wobble!
> - Under the gourd → Under would be squashed! The shoe is on top of the gourd.
> _For grown-ups: Your child described where the shoe is using position words._

_Both groups gasp. Both groups are happy!_  
**Barty**: Follow the Keeper!  

#### `q09-complete`
