## LJ1. Konnichiwa, Hana!

- `lj1-step1`: Say hello to Hana at the gate. Say “konnichiwa”! · _Tap Hana and choose Greet. A little bow is lovely too._
- `lj1-step2`: Teach three Nubbins to bow when you say “konnichiwa”. · _Tap a Nubbin and say “konnichiwa”. Say it a few times. Nubbins learn by copying._
- `lj1-step3`: Say “oyasumi” (good night) to everyone. · _In the evening, tap each friend and choose Say, then “oyasumi”._

#### `lj1-s1`

_Bang! The gate swings open._  
_In runs a girl with a sketchbook. Nova follows, slowly._  
**Nova**: This is Hana. She raced me here!  
**Hana**: I won. By a lot.  
**Hana**: こんにちは (konnichiwa, “hello”)!  
_(Say it: kon-nee-chee-WAH. Your child says it back.)_  
_Hana bows. A quick little dip, then up again._  
**Hana**: はじめまして (hajimemashite, “nice to meet you”)!  
_(Say it: hah-jee-meh-MASH-teh. Say it slowly, together.)_  
**Hana**: We only say that the very first time we meet.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, do): greeting someone new with a bow  
> **Hana**: At home, we bow to say hello. And thank you. And sorry.  
> **Hana**: A deeper bow shows extra respect.  
> _(Stand up together. Bow and say: konnichiwa!)_  
> Check: `edu:bow:konnichiwa`
> _For grown-ups: Greeted Hana with konnichiwa (“hello”) and a small bow, and heard that in Japan people bow to greet, thank and say sorry._


**Choices**

- **Bow and say “konnichiwa”.**
    - You bow. Hana bows back, a bit lower.
    - **Hana**: Lower means I win. Probably.
- **Wave and say “konnichiwa”.**
    - **Hana**: A wave is friendly too!
    - **Hana**: Next time, add a tiny bow. Like this.
- **Say “hajimemashite” with a bow.**
    - **Hana**: Hajimemashite! The first-time word! Perfect!
    - **Hana**: Nova took three days to learn that.
    - **Nova**: Two days.

#### `lj1-s1-done`

_Hana is drawing you already. Scribble, scribble!_  
**Hana**: Done! Lovely nose. Big ears. My fault, not yours.  
_Three Nubbins peek out from behind the water butt._  
_One tries to bow. It tips over. Roly-poly!_  
**Hana**: Do they want to learn? はい (hai, “yes”)!  
_(Say it: HAI. Nod your head.)_  
**Hana**: Is that a frog? Am I scared? いいえ (iie, “no”)!  
_(Say it: EE-eh. Shake your head.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): yes and no in Japanese  
> **Hana**: Silly questions! Answer hai or iie.  
> **Hana**: Is a Nubbin a teapot?  
> _(Your child answers: iie! Shake your head.)_  
> **Hana**: Do you like plums?  
> _(Your child answers hai or iie. Both are fine!)_  
> Check: `edu:say:ja:hai-iie`
> _For grown-ups: Answered silly questions with hai (“yes”) and iie (“no”), nodding and shaking their head._


#### `lj1-s2`

_Morning! Dew on the grass. Sparkle, sparkle._  
**Hana**: おはよう (ohayō, “good morning”)!  
_(Say it: oh-hah-YOH. Say it back to Hana.)_  
**Fern**: Good morning. Have a plum.  
**Hana**: ありがとう (arigatō, “thank you”)!  
_(Say it: ah-ree-gah-TOH. Say thank you to Fern.)_  
_Hana runs too fast. Bump! Over goes a round Nubbin._  
**Hana**: ごめんなさい (gomen nasai, “I’m sorry”)!  
_(Say it: go-men nah-SAI.)_  
**Fern**: That’s Dumpling. He copies everything.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): saying sorry after a bump  
> _Hana bumped Dumpling by mistake. What can she do?_  
> - ✅ Say sorry and help him up. → Yes! Gomen nasai, and a hand up. Better! Hana bows low. Dumpling copies, and lands on his face.
> - Run away. → Running away won’t help Dumpling. Say sorry, then help him up.
> - Pretend it didn’t happen. → Dumpling still feels bumped! Saying sorry helps him feel better.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about what to do after bumping someone by mistake: say sorry (gomen nasai) and help._


**Choices**

- **The shy one under the bench.**
    - You whisper: konnichiwa.
    - The shy one dips its head. Just a tiny bit.
    - **Hana**: Small bows count!
- **The bouncy one on the wall.**
    - Konnichiwa! The bouncy one bows. Boing! Off the wall!
    - **Hana**: Very keen. I like it.
- **The sleepy one in the sun.**
    - Konnichiwa! The sleepy one bows… slowly… and stays down.
    - **Hana**: Is it bowing? Or asleep?
    - **Fern**: Bit of both.

#### `lj1-s2-done`

_Three Nubbins in a row._  
_You say konnichiwa. Bow, bow, bow!_  
**Hana**: Arigatō, everyone! So good!  
_Then one more bow. Dumpling copies, late!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): 3 and 1 more make 4  
> _Three Nubbins bowed. Then Dumpling bowed too. How many bowed?_  
> _(Count on your fingers together.)_  
> - 3 → Three Nubbins, and Dumpling makes one more. One, two, three, four!
> - ✅ 4 → Four! Three Nubbins and one Dumpling.
> - 5 → Let’s count: one, two, three Nubbins. Dumpling makes four.
> _For grown-ups: Counted three bowing Nubbins and one more, and found that 3 and 1 more make 4._

**Hana**: Other way round, Dumpling!  
_He turns round. He bows again. Bump!_  
_Dumpling looks very pleased with himself._  

#### `lj1-s3`

_Evening. The sky goes orange, then dark blue._  
_Luna comes up the path with a jar of moths._  
**Hana**: こんばんは (konbanwa, “good evening”), Luna!  
_(Say it: kom-bahn-WAH. Wave to Luna!)_  
**Luna**: Konbanwa! Nova taught me.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): times of day: morning and evening  
> _The sun is going down. Is it morning or evening?_  
> - ✅ Evening → Evening! The sun goes down in the evening. So we say konbanwa.
> - Morning → In the morning the sun comes up. Now it’s going down. That’s evening!
> _For grown-ups: Noticed that the sun goes down in the evening, when Hana says konbanwa (“good evening”)._

**Hana**: Bedtime word: おやすみ (oyasumi, “good night”).  
_(Say it: oh-yah-SOO-mee. Whisper it, soft and sleepy.)_  
**Hana**: Everybody gets an oyasumi. That’s the rule.  
_Dumpling is asleep already. In the herb bed._  

**Choices**

- **Start with the Nubbins.**
    - Oyasumi, oyasumi, oyasumi! Three sleepy bows.
    - And one snore. That’s Dumpling.
- **Start with Nova.**
    - You say oyasumi to Nova, with a little bow.
    - **Nova**: A bow! I feel very grand.
- **Start with Hana.**
    - **Hana**: Me first? Oyasumi! I win!
    - **Nova**: It’s not a race.
    - **Hana**: Everything is a race.

#### `lj1-s3-done`

_Oyasumi to everyone. Even the herbs._  
**Fern**: I’m off home. またね (mata ne, “see you”)!  
_(Say it: mah-tah NEH. Wave goodbye to Fern.)_  
_Dumpling bows to the moon._  
_The moon does not bow back. But it shines._  
_Hana falls asleep on the bench, pencil in hand._  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): a calm bedtime routine  
> _(Take a big, slow breath together. In… and out.)_  
> _(Now whisper oyasumi to your favourite toy.)_  
> Check: `edu:whisper:ja:oyasumi`
> _For grown-ups: Practised a calm bedtime routine: a slow breath, then a whispered oyasumi (“good night”)._


#### `lj1-complete`

_Morning. A paper lantern hangs on the gate._  
**Nova**: A welcome sign. For everyone who comes.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): tracing the kana shape こ (ko)  
> **Hana**: The sign starts with this shape: こ (ko).  
> **Hana**: It says “ko”, like the start of konnichiwa.  
> _(Trace it with a finger: two little lines. The top one first.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:ja:ko`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Japanese shape こ (ko), the first shape of konnichiwa on the welcome sign._

**Hana**: I made up a bowing song! Bow at every line.  
**Hana**: おはよう (ohayō), up comes the sun!  
**Hana**: こんにちは (konnichiwa), hello, everyone!  
**Hana**: ありがとう (arigatō), thanks for the fun!  
**Hana**: こんばんは (konbanwa), the day is done!  
**Hana**: おやすみ (oyasumi), down goes the light. Bow, bow, and say good night!  

> **Lesson** (music, RF.K.2, do): singing a song with rhymes and actions  
> _(Sing it again together. Bow at the end of every line!)_  
> **Hana**: Sun, everyone, fun, done! Light, night! They rhyme!  
> Check: `edu:sing:ja:bowing-song`
> _For grown-ups: Sang Hana’s Bowing Song with five Japanese greetings, bowing on the beat and hearing the rhymes._

_Dumpling bows one line late. Every time! Then he lies down._  
**Hana**: Hai! You can bow now. Properly.  

## LJ2. The Hiragana Pond

- `lj2-step1`: Find the painted stones and hop across. Say each sound! · _Start nearest the bank. Say ah, ee, oo, eh, oh as you hop._
- `lj2-step2`: Trace a shape in the sand by the pond. · _Tap the sand. Follow the numbered lines with your finger. No rush._
- `lj2-step3`: Help write Hana’s name in her sketchbook. Then draw a flower. · _Open the sketchbook. A grown-up can help you trace the two shapes._

#### `lj2-s1`

_Hana has painted shapes on the pond stones._  
**Marina**: She asked you first. Mostly.  
**Hana**: Each shape makes a sound. Five sounds today!  
**Hana**: Ah. Ee. Oo. Eh. Oh.  
_(Say them after Hana: ah, ee, oo, eh, oh.)_  
**Hana**: Hop on the stones. Say each sound as you go.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): saying the five Japanese vowel sounds  
> _(You say a sound. Your child says it back and hops.)_  
> **Hana**: Ah! Ee! Oo! Eh! Oh!  
> Check: `edu:say:ja:vowel-sounds`
> _For grown-ups: Said the five Japanese vowel sounds (ah, ee, oo, eh, oh) back to Hana, hopping on each one._

_On the last stone sits a frog. Fat and wet._  
**Hana**: That is a normal frog. I am being normal.  

#### `lj2-s1-done`

_Hop! Hop! Hop! Hop! Hop! Five stones._  
_On the last stone, the frog moves. A tiny bit._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): position words: on and under  
> _Where is the frog?_  
> - ✅ On the stone → On the stone. Still. On. The. Stone.
> - Under the stone → Look again. The frog is on top of the stone.
> - In a tree → Not in a tree! The frog is on the stone.
> _For grown-ups: Used position words to say where the frog was: on the stone, not under it._

**Hana**: Now jump up onto the bank! うえ (ue, “up”)!  
_(Say it: OO-eh. Jump up when you say it!)_  
_Up you go! Hana claps._  
**Hana**: Nobody mention the frog.  
**Marina**: Nobody mentioned the frog.  

#### `lj2-s2`

_Dusty has raked the sand flat. Smooth!_  
**Dusty**: I found two spoons under here. Not mine.  
**Hana**: We draw shapes in the sand. Next to the いけ (ike, “pond”).  
_(Say it: EE-keh. Point to the pond.)_  
**Dusty**: I’ve been next to an ike all day!  
_Hana draws one bendy line in the sand. Like a beak._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): tracing the kana shape く (ku)  
> **Hana**: This shape is く (ku). It says “ku”.  
> **Hana**: Just one line. Like a bird’s beak.  
> _(Trace it with one finger: from the top, in to the point, then down.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:ja:ku`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Japanese shape く (ku), which is just one stroke, in the sand._

_The frog hops into the sand. Right in the way._  
**Hana**: It’s fine. Frogs are fine. It’s fine.  

**Choices**

- **Gently carry the frog back to the pond.**
    - You scoop it up. Gently. Back to the water.
    - **Hana**: I was fine. But thank you.
- **Draw round the frog.**
    - You draw the beak shape round the frog.
    - It looks like the frog is getting a hug.
    - **Hana**: That’s a really good drawing!
- **Ask Hana if she’s scared.**
    - **Hana**: Me? No. Not at all.
    - She is standing on one leg. Far, far away.

#### `lj2-s2-done`

_The beak shape sits in the sand. Neat!_  
_Dusty steps back to look. Oops! Squish._  
**Dusty**: ごめんなさい (gomen nasai)! I’ll rake it smooth.  
_(Say it: go-men nah-SAI.)_  
**Hana**: Now let’s draw a かお (kao, “face”)!  
_(Say it: KAH-oh. Point to your face.)_  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): naming parts of the face  
> **Hana**: Point to your eyes! Your nose! Your mouth!  
> _(Point together. Then make a silly kao!)_  
> Check: `edu:point:face:eyes-nose-mouth`
> _For grown-ups: Pointed to their eyes, nose and mouth, and learned kao (“face”)._

_Two eyes. A nose. A smile. It looks like Dusty._  
**Dusty**: That’s me, isn’t it?  

#### `lj2-s3`

_Hana opens her sketchbook. The page is empty!_  
_That never happens._  
**Hana**: My name is はな (hana, “flower”).  
_(Say it: HAH-nah. Now say it to Hana.)_  
**Hana**: It means flower. Flowers were named after me.  

> **Lesson** (art, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): drawing a flower  
> **Hana**: Hana means flower. Draw one next to my name!  
> _(Draw a flower together. Count the petals!)_  
> Check: `edu:draw:flower`
> _For grown-ups: Drew a flower for Hana, whose name means “flower” in Japanese, and counted its petals._

**Hana**: Write my name with me. Big!  
_(You can help with the tracing.)_  
**Hana**: Across, or down? Japanese can go both ways.  

**Choices**

- **Write it across.**
    - Side by side, across the page.
    - **Hana**: Like a name on a door!
- **Write it down.**
    - One on top. One underneath.
    - **Hana**: Very fancy! Like a tall tower.

#### `lj2-s3-done`

_Hana’s name, big on the page. A bit wobbly._  
**Hana**: That’s how my family writes my name.  
**Hana**: I’m keeping this page for ever.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): noticing how someone feels  
> _Hana’s voice is soft. She is smiling. How does she feel?_  
> - ✅ Happy and proud → Yes! Happy and proud. My name, the way my family writes it.
> - Cross → Not cross. She is smiling. She feels happy and proud.
> - Scared → Not scared. Not yet! She feels happy and proud.
> _For grown-ups: Read Hana’s face and voice and named her feeling: happy and proud._

_The frog hops onto the page. Plop!_  
_Hana makes a tiny squeak. Not a scream._  
**Hana**: That was not a scream.  

#### `lj2-complete`

_The pond has lots of painted stones now._  
**Hana**: Quick game! I say it, the Nubbin does it!  
**Hana**: Ue! Ike! Kao!  
_Up! The pond! A silly face!_  
**Hana**: あか (aka, “red”)! あお (ao, “blue”)!  
_(Say them: ah-KAH, AH-oh. Point to red, then blue.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): acting out Japanese words  
> _(You say a word. Your child does the action!)_  
> _(Ue: jump up. Ike: point to water. Kao: silly face.)_  
> _(Aka: find red. Ao: find blue. Hana: be a flower!)_  
> Check: `edu:act:ja:pond-words`
> _For grown-ups: Played a listen-and-do game with six Japanese words: ue, ike, kao, aka, ao and hana._

_The Nubbin goes red in the cheeks. Then it sits down._  
**Marina**: I’d keep the stones. They’re lovely.  
**Hana**: Stepping stones that say hello in Japanese!  

## LJ3. Counting Koi

- `lj3-step1`: Count the koi into the pond, from ichi to jū. · _Tap each fish as it swims in. Say the number with Hana._
- `lj3-step2`: Race Hana round the pond. Count the laps in Japanese. · _Start the race at the lap board. Say each lap number as you pass the post._
- `lj3-step3`: Count ten stars with Hana. Then find the moon. · _Go up the hill with Luna after dark. The moon is low, behind the plum trees._

#### `lj3-s1`

_Marina has two big buckets. Splash, splash!_  
**Marina**: New fish for the pond!  
**Hana**: こい (koi, “koi fish”)! They’re koi!  
_(Say it: KOY. It’s the same word in English!)_  
**Hana**: Let’s count them in. Hold up your fingers.  
**Hana**: いち (ichi, “one”), に (ni, “two”), さん (san, “three”)!  
_(Say it: EE-chee, NEE, SAHN. One finger at a time.)_  
**Hana**: よん (yon, “four”), ご (go, “five”)! A whole hand!  
_(Say it: YOHN, GO. Wiggle all five fingers!)_  
_Plop! Plop! The koi do not wait._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5 in Japanese  
> **Hana**: Count this bucket! Ichi, ni, san, yon, go!  
> _(Count on your fingers together. Five koi!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:ja:koi:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five koi in Japanese: ichi, ni, san, yon, go._


#### `lj3-s1-done`

**Marina**: Five more in this bucket.  
**Hana**: ろく (roku, “six”), なな (nana, “seven”), はち (hachi, “eight”)!  
_(Say it: ROH-koo, NAH-nah, HAH-chee. Copy Hana!)_  
**Hana**: きゅう (kyū, “nine”), じゅう (jū, “ten”)! Ten koi!  
_(Say it: KYOO, JOO. Show ten fingers!)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): tracing the kana shape い (i)  
> **Hana**: Ichi starts with this shape: い (i).  
> **Hana**: It says “ee”. Two little lines, like two koi.  
> _(Trace it: the long line on the left first, then the short one.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:ja:i`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Japanese shape い (i), the first shape of ichi (“one”)._

**Marina**: A little dramatic. But right.  
_Dumpling counts the koi with his head. Nod, nod, nod._  

#### `lj3-s2`

_Zoom! Sparks arrives. Pixel brings a lap board._  
**Sparks**: WHO HERE THINKS SHE’S FAST?  
**Hana**: Me. I am fast.  
**Pixel**: It flips for every lap. One line, two lines, three lines!  
**Hana**: Count the laps in Japanese. Out loud!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): counting actions: one, two, three  
> **Hana**: Run on the spot! One lap: ichi!  
> **Hana**: Two laps: ni! Three laps: san!  
> _(Run on the spot together. Count each lap out loud.)_  
> Check: `edu:count:ja:laps:3`
> _For grown-ups: Ran on the spot and counted three laps in Japanese: ichi, ni, san._

**Hana**: Loser feeds the さかな (sakana, “fish”).  
_(Say it: sah-KAH-nah. Make a fish face!)_  
**Byte**: I will write down the excuses.  

**Choices**

- **A fair start.**
    - **Byte**: Fair start. Nobody moves. Nobody. Hana.
    - **Hana**: I wasn’t moving. I was leaning.
- **Give Hana a head start.**
    - **Hana**: I don’t need a head start!
    - **Hana**: …But I’ll take it.
- **Let Sparks race too.**
    - **Sparks**: THREE-WAY RACE! YES!
    - **Hana**: Fine. I’ll beat you both.

#### `lj3-s2-done`

_Clack! The last tile flips._  
_Everyone flops on the grass. Puff, puff, puff._  
**Byte**: Laps: counted. Hana’s excuse: coming soon.  
**Hana**: That was a practice one.  
**Byte**: There it is.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): losing kindly  
> _Hana didn’t win. What can she say?_  
> - ✅ “Good race!” → GOOD RACE, HANA! Again tomorrow? …Good race. Tomorrow I win.
> - “I’m the best!” → Hmm. That might make the others sad. “Good race!” is kinder.
> - “That was a practice one!” → There it is again. Feeling cross is OK. Then “good race!” helps.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about what to say after losing a race, and practised “good race!”._

**Hana**: Now we all feed the sakana. Together!  
**Sparks**: Mata ne! Rematch tomorrow!  
_(Say it: mah-tah NEH. Wave to Sparks!)_  

#### `lj3-s3`

_Night. Luna spreads a blanket on the hill._  
**Luna**: It’s darker up here. So we see more stars.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): we see more stars where it is dark  
> **Luna**: Why do we see more stars up here?  
> - ✅ It’s darker here. → Yes. Away from bright lights, the sky is darker. More stars show.
> - The stars came to see us. → A lovely idea! Really, it’s darker here. So we see more.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that we can see more stars where the sky is darker, away from bright lights._

**Hana**: Star is ほし (hoshi, “star”).  
_(Say it: HOH-shee. Point up at the sky.)_  
**Hana**: Ichi… ni… san… Oh! That one’s a plane.  
**Hana**: Moon is つき (tsuki, “moon”). Where is the tsuki?  
_(Say it: TSOO-kee. Look for the moon.)_  
_Hana points at a star. Dumpling points too, with his whole body!_  

**Choices**

- **Start with the brightest star.**
    - **Luna**: The bright one. Easy to find again.
- **Count the ones Dumpling points at.**
    - Dumpling points. You count. Dumpling points. You count.
    - Very slow. Very right.

#### `lj3-s3-done`

_Ten hoshi. One by one._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 10 in Japanese  
> **Hana**: Ichi, ni, san, yon, go!  
> **Hana**: Roku, nana, hachi, kyū, jū!  
> _(Count ten stars on your fingers together.)_  
> Check: `edu:count:ja:hoshi:10`
> _For grown-ups: Counted ten stars in Japanese, from ichi to jū, one finger for each._

_And there, low behind the trees: a thin silver tsuki._  
**Hana**: Found it! Hold still. I’m drawing it.  
_Her drawing has eleven stars and two moons._  
**Hana**: That’s on purpose. It’s art.  

#### `lj3-complete`

_Morning. Pixel puts the lap board by the pond._  
**Hana**: A chant for feeding the koi! Clap on every number.  
**Hana**: Ichi, ni, san, koi in the pond!  
**Hana**: Yon, go, round and beyond!  
**Hana**: Roku, nana, under the reeds!  
**Hana**: Hachi, kyū, jū, and everyone feeds!  

> **Lesson** (music, RF.K.2, do): clapping a counting chant with rhymes  
> _(Say the chant again. Clap on every number!)_  
> **Hana**: Pond, beyond! Reeds, feeds! They rhyme!  
> Check: `edu:clap:ja:koi-chant`
> _For grown-ups: Clapped along to Hana’s Koi Chant, counting from ichi to jū and hearing the rhymes._

_Ten koi pop up at once. Gulp, gulp, gulp!_  
**Marina**: The spotty one is yours. A koi for your pond.  
**Hana**: ありがとう (arigatō), Marina!  
_(Say it: ah-ree-gah-TOH. Say thank you to Marina too!)_  

## LJ4. Colours and Lunchboxes

- `lj4-step1`: Pack a lunch box with Hana. Sort the food by colour. · _Drag each food into its own little room. Dumpling does not go in the box!_
- `lj4-step2`: Say “itadakimasu” before the picnic and “gochisōsama” after. · _Hands together and a little bow before eating. Say the second word when the box is empty._
- `lj4-step3`: Draw three fruits for Hana’s sketchbook. · _Open the sketchbook. Pick three fruits and colour them in._

#### `lj4-s1`

_Hana has an empty box. It has four little rooms._  
**Hana**: This is an おべんとう (obentō, “lunch box”).  
_(Say it: oh-ben-TOH. Pretend to open a lid.)_  
**Hana**: First, おにぎり (onigiri, “rice ball”). Rice is しろ (shiro, “white”).  
_(Say it: oh-nee-GEE-ree, shee-ROH.)_  
**Hana**: Now the green room. Green is みどり (midori, “green”).  
_(Say it: mee-DOH-ree. Find something green!)_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.B.3, do): sorting things by colour  
> **Hana**: Onigiri here. Green things there. Every food in its room.  
> _(Point to where each food goes. Sort by colour!)_  
> Check: `edu:sort:ja:bento:colour`
> _For grown-ups: Sorted lunch-box food into groups by colour: shiro (white) and midori (green)._

_Dumpling climbs in. He sits in the green room._  
**Fern**: Dumpling is not lunch.  
**Hana**: So what goes in the midori room?  

**Choices**

- **The beans.**
    - You put in a bean. Dumpling eats it.
    - You put in another. He eats that too.
- **Cucumber from Fern’s garden.**
    - **Fern**: My cucumber! I grew that. Mostly on purpose.
- **A big lettuce leaf.**
    - **Hana**: Good! Green, and also a wall.

#### `lj4-s1-done`

_In goes the egg. It is きいろ (kiiro, “yellow”)._  
_(Say it: kee-EE-roh. Find something yellow!)_  
**Hana**: Red tomatoes: あか (aka)! Black seaweed: くろ (kuro, “black”)!  
_(Say it: ah-KAH, koo-ROH.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): tracing the kana shape あ (a)  
> **Hana**: Aka starts with this shape: あ (a).  
> **Hana**: It says “ah”. It has a loop, like a curly noodle.  
> _(Trace it: the line across, the line down, then the loop.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:ja:a`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Japanese shape あ (a), the first shape of aka (“red”)._

_The box looks like a little painted window._  
_You lift the lid to check. Dumpling is in the box!_  
**Fern**: How? I was looking right at him!  

#### `lj4-s2`

_Picnic time by the pond. Out comes a blue cloth._  
**Hana**: Blue is あお (ao). Hardly any food is blue!  
_(Say it: AH-oh.)_  
**Hana**: Before we eat, we say いただきます (itadakimasu, “said before eating”).  
_(Say it: ee-tah-dah-kee-MAS. Hands together, little bow.)_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, do): waiting, and saying thank you for food  
> **Hana**: We wait until everyone is ready. Then we start together.  
> _(Hands together. A little bow. Say itadakimasu together!)_  
> Check: `edu:say:ja:itadakimasu`
> _For grown-ups: Waited for everyone, then said itadakimasu with hands together and a little bow before eating._

**Sparks**: So I can’t eat ANYTHING yet?  
**Hana**: Nothing. You have a bean in your mouth.  
**Sparks**: …It got in by itself.  
_Dumpling copies the little bow. Then he eats the food!_  
**Hana**: That’s not how it works, Dumpling!  

#### `lj4-s2-done`

_“Itadakimasu!” Onigiri for everyone. Dumpling is already chewing._  
**Hana**: Fruit! りんご (ringo, “apple”) and みかん (mikan, “mandarin orange”).  
_(Say it: REEN-go, mee-KAHN.)_  
**Hana**: A mikan’s colour is オレンジ (orenji, “orange”)!  
_(Say it: oh-REN-jee. It sounds like “orange”!)_  
**Hana**: All gone? Now we say ごちそうさま (gochisōsama, “said after eating”).  
_(Say it: go-chee-SOH-sah-mah. Pat your full tummy.)_  
**Sparks**: I’m too full to run. Don’t tell anyone.  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, notice): noticing when your tummy is full  
> **Hana**: Your tummy tells you when it’s full. Then: gochisōsama!  
> _(Ask: how does your tummy feel after lunch? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Talked about how our tummies tell us when we are full, after saying gochisōsama (“said after eating”)._

**Hana**: I’m telling everyone.  

#### `lj4-s3`

_Hana opens her sketchbook. A fresh page!_  
**Hana**: Let’s draw fruit. It’s not a race.  
**Nova**: It’s a race.  
**Hana**: いちご (ichigo, “strawberry”)! もも (momo, “peach”)!  
_(Say it: ee-CHEE-go, MOH-moh.)_  
**Hana**: Peaches are ピンク (pinku, “pink”). Fern’s plums are むらさき (murasaki, “purple”).  
_(Say it: PEEN-koo, moo-rah-SAH-kee. Say murasaki three times fast!)_  

> **Lesson** (art, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): drawing and colouring three fruits  
> **Hana**: Pick three fruits. Colour them in!  
> _(Draw together. An ichigo? A momo? A plum? Name each colour!)_  
> Check: `edu:draw:ja:fruit:3`
> _For grown-ups: Drew and coloured three fruits, naming their colours in Japanese._

_Dumpling sits in the fruit bowl. He wants to be drawn._  
**Hana**: What will you draw first?  

**Choices**

- **An ichigo, very red.**
    - **Hana**: Lots of little dots on the outside. Those are the seeds!
- **A momo, pink and round.**
    - **Hana**: Peaches are hard to draw. They look like a bottom.
- **One of Fern’s purple plums.**
    - **Fern**: You made it look lovely. Thank you.

#### `lj4-s3-done`

_Three fruits on your page. Three on Hana’s._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): adding 3 and 3  
> _You drew three fruits. Hana drew three. How many fruits?_  
> _(Count them together on your fingers.)_  
> - 5 → Let’s count: one, two, three. Then four, five, six. Six fruits!
> - ✅ 6 → Six! Three and three make six.
> - 7 → Let’s count: one, two, three. Then four, five, six. Six fruits!
> _For grown-ups: Put two groups of three fruits together and counted six._

**Hana**: Yours look happier. They go at the front.  
_In the corner is a small round smudge._  
_Dumpling sat on the pencils!_  
**Hana**: He drew himself. Fine. He’s in.

_Hana puts coloured cards on the grass._  
**Hana**: Colour game! I say a colour. Dumpling sits on it.  
**Hana**: Aka! Kiiro! Midori!  
_Hop, hop, hop!_  
**Hana**: Shiro! Kuro! Murasaki! Pinku! Orenji!  
_He sits on purple so hard it creases._  
**Hana**: And ao!

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): nine colour words in Japanese  
> _(You say a colour word. Your child finds that colour in the room!)_  
> **Hana**: Aka, ao, kiiro, midori, shiro, kuro, pinku, orenji, murasaki!  
> Check: `edu:quiz:ja:colours`

**Hana**: The obentō is yours! Take it to a sad Nubbin.

## LJ5. Obāchan’s Letters

- `lj5-step1`: Read one of Obāchan’s letters with Hana. · _Tap the letter on the kitchen table. A grown-up can read it with you._
- `lj5-step2`: Draw a family picture with Hana. · _Draw anyone who is family to you. People and pets all count._
- `lj5-step3`: Tell Hana how each Nubbin feels. · _Look at each Nubbin’s face. Is it happy, sad, sleepy or scared?_

#### `lj5-s1`

_Rain on the roof. Pitter, patter._  
_Hana opens a tin of letters._  
**Hana**: Hazel used to write letters to my grandma.  
**Hana**: I call my grandma おばあちゃん (obāchan, “grandma”).  
_(Say it: oh-BAH-chahn. Long and soft in the middle.)_  
**Hana**: Obāchan wrote: our ねこ (neko, “cat”) sleeps on my letters!  
_(Say it: NEH-koh. Meow!)_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): animal names and the sounds they make  
> **Hana**: The いぬ (inu, “dog”) next door says woof!  
> _(Say it: ee-NOO. Woof, woof!)_  
> **Hana**: The とり (tori, “bird”) says tweet!  
> _(Say it: TOH-ree. Flap your arms!)_  
> Check: `edu:say:ja:animal-sounds`
> _For grown-ups: Learned neko (“cat”), inu (“dog”) and tori (“bird”), and made each animal’s sound._

**Hana**: She signed it: from your ともだち (tomodachi, “friend”).  
_(Say it: toh-moh-DAH-chee. Who is your friend?)_  
**Hana**: Your friend. I like that.  

#### `lj5-s1-done`

_You read the rest together. Hana does the voices._  
_The neko gets a very grand voice._  
**Hana**: A letter is like a little visit.  
**Hana**: I miss my obāchan. She’s fine. I just miss her.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): missing someone, and what helps  
> **Hana**: When I miss her, I draw her a picture.  
> _(Ask: who do you miss sometimes? What helps you feel better?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Talked about missing someone, and things that help, like drawing them a picture._

_Hana pins the letter above her bed._  

#### `lj5-s2`

_The rain stops. Hana spreads big paper on the floor._  
**Hana**: Let’s draw our かぞく (kazoku, “family”).  
_(Say it: kah-ZOH-koo.)_  
**Hana**: My おかあさん (okāsan, “mum”). My おとうさん (otōsan, “dad”).  
_(Say it: oh-KAH-sahn, oh-TOH-sahn.)_  
**Hana**: And my おじいちゃん (ojīchan, “grandpa”).  
_(Say it: oh-JEE-chahn.)_  
**Hana**: He died when I was little. That means his body stopped working.  
**Hana**: It won’t start again. But I still draw him in every picture.  
**Hana**: He’s still in my family.  

> **Lesson** (art, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, do): drawing your own family  
> **Hana**: Now yours. Anyone who is family to you.  
> **Hana**: Families are all different. People, pets, friends. They all count.  
> _(Draw your family together. Say who each one is.)_  
> Check: `edu:draw:ja:kazoku`
> _For grown-ups: Drew their own family, and heard that families are all different and everyone in them counts._


**Choices**

- **Start with the grown-ups who look after me.**
    - **Hana**: Good! Write their names. Names are labels too.
- **Start with my pets.**
    - **Hana**: Pets first! My rabbit would agree.
- **Start with my friends.**
    - **Hana**: Friends can be family too. Nova is mine this summer.
    - **Nova**: I’m honoured.

#### `lj5-s2-done`

_Two families on one big paper._  
**Hana**: And our うさぎ (usagi, “rabbit”). She’s called Biscuit. She bites.  
_(Say it: oo-SAH-gee. Hop like a rabbit!)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): tracing the kana shape う (u)  
> **Hana**: Usagi starts with this shape: う (u).  
> **Hana**: It says “oo”. A little line on top, like an ear.  
> _(Trace it: the little line first, then the big curve.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:ja:u`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Japanese shape う (u), the first shape of usagi (“rabbit”)._

**Nova**: What’s that bird in the tree? It looks cross.  
**Hana**: A ふくろう (fukurō, “owl”). Owls always look cross.  
_(Say it: foo-koo-ROH. Hoo, hoo!)_  
**Hana**: It was a very kind owl.  

#### `lj5-s3`

_Clover sits in the grass with the Nubbins._  
**Hana**: うれしい (ureshii, “happy”)! That one is rolling about.  
_(Say it: oo-REH-shee. Make a happy face!)_  
**Hana**: かなしい (kanashii, “sad”). That one lost its ball.  
_(Say it: kah-NAH-shee. Make a sad face.)_  
_Plop! A frog lands on Hana’s shoe._  
**Hana**: かえる (kaeru, “frog”)! It’s a kaeru!  
_(Say it: kah-EH-roo. Ribbit!)_  
**Hana**: I’m not こわい (kowai, “scared”). Not at all.  
_(Say it: koh-WAI.) Hana is standing on a chair._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): it is OK to feel scared  
> **Clover**: It’s all right to be scared. Even of something small.  
> **Hana**: …Maybe I’m a little bit kowai.  
> _(Ask: what helps when you feel scared?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that it is all right to feel scared, and talked about what helps._


**Choices**

- **“It’s OK to be scared.”**
    - **Hana**: Thank you. It is OK. I’m staying up here, though.
- **Gently carry the frog to the pond.**
    - You cup the frog in your hands. Back to the pond.
    - **Hana**: Thank you. Really.
- **“Let’s look at it together.”**
    - You stand by the chair. The kaeru blinks. Hana blinks.
    - **Hana**: It’s got quite a nice face.

#### `lj5-s3-done`

_You look at each Nubbin’s face._  
**Hana**: The ball came back! Now that one is ureshii!  
_Dumpling does a huge yawn. Aaaah._  
**Hana**: Dumpling is ねむい (nemui, “sleepy”). Always.  
_(Say it: neh-MOO-ee. Do a big yawn!)_  
**Clover**: And you, Hana?  
**Hana**: たのしい (tanoshii, “fun”)! Today was fun. Even the frog. Mostly.  
_(Say it: tah-NOH-shee. Do a happy wiggle!)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): feelings words in Japanese  
> **Hana**: Show me ureshii! Kanashii! Nemui!  
> _(Make each face together: happy, sad, sleepy!)_  
> Check: `edu:show:ja:feelings`
> _For grown-ups: Made faces for ureshii (“happy”), kanashii (“sad”) and nemui (“sleepy”)._


#### `lj5-complete`

_Evening. Hana writes a letter home._  
**Hana**: Help me check my words! Dumpling makes faces.  
_Dumpling smiles. Then droops. Then yawns. Then hides._  
**Hana**: Ureshii! Kanashii! Nemui! Kowai!  
**Hana**: Kazoku: okāsan, otōsan, obāchan, ojīchan, tomodachi!  
**Hana**: Animals: neko, inu, tori, usagi, fukurō!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> **Hana**: How many animals in my letter? Count them!  
> _(Count on your fingers: neko, inu, tori, usagi, fukurō. Five!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:ja:animals:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five animals in Hana’s letter, saying each one’s Japanese name._

**Hana**: And one kaeru. He’s in the story now.  
**Hana**: Obāchan’s letters can stay here. In a little box.  
**Hana**: And here’s my happiest face. For when it’s tanoshii!  

## LJ6. Floating Lanterns

- `lj6-step1`: Sit with Hana by the stream. · _Hana is sitting by the stream. Tap her and choose Talk._
- `lj6-step2`: Help Hana make a lantern for her grandpa. · _Fold a lantern at Fern’s table. You can make one too. A grown-up can help write the name._
- `lj6-step3`: Float Hana’s lantern with her at dusk. Later, join the party! · _Go to the stream in the evening. Luna lights the lanterns. The party is back at the cottage._

#### `lj6-s1`

_Late summer. Hana sits by the stream. She is not drawing._  
_That means something is up._  
**Hana**: At home, there’s a special time in summer.  
**Hana**: Families remember the people in their family who have died.  
**Hana**: My family floats a paper lantern down the river for my grandpa.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, notice): families remember people in different ways  
> _(Tell your child: families all over the world remember people who have died.)_  
> _(Some tell stories. Some look at photos. Some float lanterns.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that Hana’s family floats a paper lantern in summer to remember her grandpa, and that families everywhere remember people in their own ways._

**Hana**: River is かわ (kawa, “river”). This stream is a little kawa.  
_(Say it: KAH-wah. Point to the water.)_  
**Hana**: The lantern has a ひかり (hikari, “light”) inside.  
_(Say it: hee-KAH-ree.)_  
**Hana**: This year I’m here. Could I float his lantern on your stream?  

**Choices**

- **“Yes! We’ll all come.”**
    - **Hana**: Really? Then I need paper. And sticks. And you, to help.
- **“Tell me about your grandpa.”**
    - **Hana**: He went fishing every week. He never caught anything.
    - **Hana**: He said that wasn’t the point. He taught me to run.
- **Sit next to her for a while.**
    - You sit together. The water goes by.
    - **Hana**: Thank you. That helped.

#### `lj6-s1-done`

_Hana is drawing again! Lanterns and tiny lights._  
**Hana**: And this is my grandma’s やま (yama, “mountain”).  
_(Say it: YAH-mah. Make a pointy mountain with your arms!)_  
**Hana**: In winter it has ゆき (yuki, “snow”) on top.  
_(Say it: YOO-kee. Shiver! Brrr!)_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): snow melts into water when it warms  
> **Hana**: When the spring sun warms the yuki on the yama, what happens?  
> - ✅ It melts into water. → Yes! Warm snow melts. It turns into water.
> - It turns into a snowman. → Only if you build one! Warm snow melts into water.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that snow (yuki) melts into water when the sun warms it._

**Hana**: Everyone is coming tomorrow. Even Ember!  
**Nova**: Ember said “fine”, and asked what time. That’s a yes.  

#### `lj6-s2`

_Fern’s kitchen is full of paper, glue and sticks._  
**Fern**: The sticks are from the old plum き (ki, “tree”).  
_(Say it: KEE. Stand tall like a tree!)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): tracing the kana shape き (ki)  
> **Hana**: This shape is き (ki). It says “ki”.  
> **Hana**: All by itself, ki means tree!  
> _(Trace it: two lines across, one line down, then the curve.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:ja:ki`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Japanese shape き (ki), which on its own is the word for “tree”._

_Pixel fills a bucket with みず (mizu, “water”)._  
_(Say it: MEE-zoo. Splash!)_  
_The first lantern goes in. Glug. It sinks._  
**Pixel**: Paper gets soggy. Wood floats. Next one gets a wooden base.  
**Hana**: I write my grandpa’s name on mine. Slowly.  
**Hana**: You can make one too. For someone you remember, or love.  
_Hana writes very carefully. Nobody talks. Shh._  

**Choices**

- **Write the name of someone I remember.**
    - **Hana**: Write it big. So it shows from far away.
    - (A grown-up can help write the name.)
- **Write the name of someone I love.**
    - **Hana**: Yes. Grandpa would like that. Lanterns are for love too.
- **Ask Hana to help write the name.**
    - **Hana**: Say it slowly. I’ll write it, and you go over it.
    - (A grown-up can help too.)

#### `lj6-s2-done`

_Lanterns dry on the windowsill. A long row._  
**Hana**: Mine is the best one.  
**Fern**: It’s not a race, Hana.  
**Hana**: …You’re right. This one isn’t.  
_Pixel puts the test one in the mizu. It floats!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): what floats and what sinks  
> _The paper one sank. Which one floats?_  
> - ✅ The one with a wooden base. → Yes! Wood floats. The paper stays dry on top.
> - A paper one with no base. → That one went glug! The wooden base helps it float.
> _For grown-ups: Saw that a lantern with a wooden base floats, while soggy paper sinks._

_Pixel nods. Good._  

#### `lj6-s3`

_Dusk. Everyone walks to the stream with Hana._  
**Hana**: No あめ (ame, “rain”) tonight. Good!  
_(Say it: AH-meh. Pitter-patter with your fingers.)_  
**Luna**: I’ll light them. One at a time. No hurry.  
**Hana**: This one is for my grandpa.  
_Hana’s little hikari floats away. Slowly, slowly._  
**Hana**: He died. His body stopped working. It won’t start again.  
**Hana**: But I still love him. The lantern helps me remember him.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, notice): remembering someone who has died  
> **Hana**: I feel a bit sad. And a bit happy. Both at once.  
> _(Say: it’s OK to feel sad and happy together.)_  
> _(Ask: who would you like to remember, or send love to?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard plainly what dying means, and that remembering, and feeling sad and happy at once, are all OK._

_A frog sits on the bank by Hana. It watches the lights._  
**Hana**: …You can watch too, frog.  

#### `lj6-s3-done`

_The lanterns float on the kawa. No ame. Just stars._  
**Hana**: ありがとう (arigatō). For doing this with me.  
_(Say it: ah-ree-gah-TOH. Say it back to Hana.)_  
_Later, at the cottage, Sparks shouts: PARTY!_  
**Hana**: Hello and good night words! Ohayō! Konnichiwa! Konbanwa! Oyasumi!  
_(Say them: oh-hah-YOH, kon-nee-chee-WAH, kom-bahn-WAH, oh-yah-SOO-mee. Bow each time!)_  
**Hana**: Hajimemashite! Hai! Iie! Gomen nasai!  
_(Say them: hah-jee-meh-MASH-teh, HAI, EE-eh, go-men nah-SAI.)_  
**Hana**: Now count the Nubbins in the front row!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 10 in Japanese  
> **Hana**: Ichi, ni, san, yon, go!  
> _(Say them: EE-chee, NEE, SAHN, YOHN, GO.)_  
> **Hana**: Roku, nana, hachi, kyū, jū!  
> _(Say them: ROH-koo, NAH-nah, HAH-chee, KYOO, JOO. A finger each!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:ja:nubbins:10`
> _For grown-ups: Counted ten Nubbins at Hana’s party in Japanese, from ichi to jū._


#### `lj6-complete`

_The party goes on, up at the cottage._  
**Hana**: Food! Ringo, mikan, ichigo, momo, onigiri, obentō! Itadakimasu!  
_(Say them: REEN-go, mee-KAHN, ee-CHEE-go, MOH-moh, oh-nee-GEE-ree, oh-ben-TOH, ee-tah-dah-kee-MAS.)_  
_Dumpling eats a ringo before Hana says the word!_  
**Hana**: Family! Okāsan, otōsan, obāchan, ojīchan, tomodachi, kazoku!  
_(Say them: oh-KAH-sahn, oh-TOH-sahn, oh-BAH-chahn, oh-JEE-chahn, toh-moh-DAH-chee, kah-ZOH-koo.)_  
**Hana**: The world! Ki, hana, mizu, kawa, yama, ame, yuki, hoshi, tsuki, hikari!  
_(Say them: KEE, HAH-nah, MEE-zoo, KAH-wah, YAH-mah, AH-meh, YOO-kee, HOH-shee, TSOO-kee, hee-KAH-ree.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): reviewing every word from the questline  
> **Hana**: Colours! Aka, ao, kiiro, midori, shiro, kuro, pinku, orenji, murasaki!  
> _(Say them: ah-KAH, AH-oh, kee-EE-roh, mee-DOH-ree, shee-ROH, koo-ROH, PEEN-koo, oh-REN-jee, moo-rah-SAH-kee.)_  
> **Hana**: Animals! Neko, inu, tori, usagi, fukurō, kaeru, koi, sakana!  
> _(Say them: NEH-koh, ee-NOO, TOH-ree, oo-SAH-gee, foo-koo-ROH, kah-EH-roo, KOY, sah-KAH-nah.)_  
> **Hana**: Feelings! Ureshii, tanoshii, kanashii, nemui, kowai!  
> _(Say them: oo-REH-shee, tah-NOH-shee, kah-NAH-shee, neh-MOO-ee, koh-WAI.)_  
> **Hana**: Pond words! Ue! Ike! Kao! And after lunch: gochisōsama!  
> _(Say them: OO-eh, EE-keh, KAH-oh, go-chee-SOH-sah-mah. Jump up, point, pull a face!)_  
> Check: `edu:review:ja:party`
> _For grown-ups: Played Hana’s review game: colours, animals, feelings and pond words in Japanese, said back and acted out._

**Nova**: A Hiragana Keeper! That’s you!  
**Hana**: I go home soon. So: またね (mata ne)!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): saying goodbye to a friend  
> _(Say it: mah-tah NEH. It means “see you”. Wave!)_  
> **Hana**: See you means I’ll see you again.  
> _(Ask: how do you say goodbye to a friend?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Said mata ne (“see you”) to Hana and talked about saying goodbye to a friend._


## S24. The Adventure Book

- `s24-step1`: Sit with Nova by Gran Hazel’s flower. · _Walk up the hill. Sit down next to Nova._
- `s24-step2`: Look at the front of Nova’s big book. · _Stay sitting by Nova. Look at the first page._
- `s24-step3`: Fetch Gran Hazel’s lantern. · _It hangs on a hook by the cottage door._
- `s24-step4`: Put Nova’s bag on the cart. Who is giggling? · _Lift the cover at the back of the cart._
- `s24-step5`: Climb up beside the waterfall. · _Follow your Nubbin. Step where it steps._
- `s24-step6`: Meet the big bird gently. · _Hold out a bun, or sit and wait. Both are kind._
- `s24-step7`: Follow the bird’s footprints. · _Big footprints with three toes. Your Nubbin finds the next one._
- `s24-step8`: Wait at the camp until evening. · _Sit by the tent while the sky gets dark._
- `s24-step9`: Light Gran Hazel’s lantern for Nova. · _Sit with Nova first. Then give her the lantern._
- `s24-step10`: Stay with Nova while she is sad. · _Sit still. There is no hurry._
- `s24-step11`: Help Nova make a pile of stones. · _Big flat stones at the bottom. Take your time._
- `s24-step12`: Follow Tilly’s whistle. · _Walk towards the whistle. Hold the lantern low._
- `s24-step13`: Help untangle the net. · _One loop at a time. Your Nubbin holds the end._
- `s24-step14`: Put the lantern on the high rock at sunrise. · _Face the Grove. Then wait for the sun._
- `s24-step15`: Write on the last page of the book. · _Pick what to write. Use Gran Hazel’s tiny pencil._

#### `s24-tea`

_It is spring. The flowers on the hill are open._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): flowers open in spring  
> _The flowers have opened. What time of year is it?_  
> - ✅ Spring → Yes! Spring. The days get warmer, and the flowers open.
> - Winter → Winter is cold, with hardly any flowers. Warm days and new flowers mean spring!
> _For grown-ups: Your child linked opening flowers to spring._

_This one is Gran Hazel’s flower. It’s a bit untidy, like her._  
_Gran Hazel died. Her flower grows here, so we remember her._  
_Here comes Nova, with a great big bag. She always walks on by._  
**Nova**: Hello. Today I thought I’d stop, and sit with her.  
_Nova sits down by the flower. Her big bag stays on her back._  
_Your Nubbin runs to Nova. Then back to you. Then to Nova again!_  
**Nova**: Is there tea? I always say no to tea.  
**Nova**: Today I’ll say yes. Yes, please!  

#### `s24-tea-done`

_You carry the teapot up the hill. Careful, careful._  
_Nova drinks one cup. Then another cup._  
_She holds a third cup, just to keep her hands warm._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 3  
> _(Count Nova’s cups of tea together: one, two, three.)_  
> **Nova**: Three cups. That’s a record for me.  
> Check: `edu:count:cups:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted three cups._

**Nova**: Gran Hazel always asked me in for tea.  
**Nova**: I always said, “No, I can’t stop.” But I could have.  
_Nova is sad. That’s all right. You sit close._  
_Then she opens the top of her big bag._  

#### `s24-pages`

_Nova takes out a big fat book. It is tied with a shoelace._  
**Nova**: This is my atlas. That means a book of maps.  
**Nova**: I never show anyone the front page. But I’ll show you.  
_The first page is a list, in pencil. The writing is big and cross._  
_At the top it says: PLACES I’LL GO._  
**Nova**: Gran Hazel wrote that. She was nineteen. Young, like a big sister.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.His.2.K-2, ask): old people were young once  
> _Gran Hazel was old. Was she ever young, like you?_  
> - ✅ Yes, once! → Yes! Every old person was a baby, then a child, then young. Gran Hazel was nineteen when she wrote this list!
> - No, never → Every grown-up was a child once. Even Gran Hazel! She was nineteen when she wrote this list.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that old people were once young, with plans of their own._

_A sea with no other side. A city with trams. Snow in summer._  
**Nova**: I was twelve. Gran Hazel gave me the book. She said, “You go.”  
**Nova**: So I went! I put a tick by every place.  

#### `s24-pages-done`

_One line, right at the bottom, has no tick._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): a tick on a list means “done”  
> _A tick means “done”. No tick means “not done yet”._  
> _(Draw a tick in the air with your finger.)_  
> Check: `edu:draw:shape:tick`
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned how a tick marks something done on a list._

_It says: The Sunrise Shelf. See the whole valley. Take someone._  
**Nova**: It’s a high rock on the far hill. One day’s walk.  
**Nova**: I went all over the world. But I never went there.  
**Nova**: It says, take someone. I never had anyone to take.  
**Nova**: Maybe I do now. Will you come?  

#### `s24-lantern`

**Nova**: Can we take Gran Hazel’s lantern? And light it up high, at sunrise?  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): the sun comes up in the morning  
> _Sunrise is when the sun comes up. When is that?_  
> - ✅ In the morning → Yes! The sun comes up every morning, and the day begins.
> - At bedtime → At bedtime the sun goes down. It comes up in the morning!
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that the sun comes up in the morning._

**Nova**: It’s for remembering her. Like saying hello to her.  
_Gran Hazel’s lantern hangs by the cottage door._  
_Nobody has lit it since she died. You keep it clean and shiny._  
_Your Nubbin sits under it and looks up._  
**Nova**: It’s yours now. Is it all right to borrow it?  

#### `s24-lantern-done`

_You lift the lantern off its hook. It feels light._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): heavy and light  
> _Which is heavier: the lantern, or Nova’s big bag?_  
> - ✅ Nova’s big bag → Yes! The bag is much heavier. The lantern is light.
> - The lantern → The lantern is easy to lift. Nova’s big bag is much heavier!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared two things by weight: heavy and light._

_Gran Hazel nearly always carried it._  
**Nova**: Thank you. Now we need a cart!  
_Your Nubbin hops along beside you. Off we go!_  

#### `s24-stowaway`

_The cart goes up the hill road. Clip-clop, creak._  
_Nova puts her big bag on. The cart goes, “Groan!”_  
_Under the cover, something says, “Ow!” Then, “Nobody heard that.”_  
_Here comes Sparks, running and waving both arms._  
**Sparks**: Is she in there? Sorry! Sorry! It’s my cousin, Tilly.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: t  
> _Tilly starts with a “t” sound. T-t-Tilly!_  
> _Which one starts with “t” too?_  
> - ✅ Tea → Yes! T-t-tea. Tilly and tea both start with “t”.
> - Bun → B-b-bun starts with “b”. T-t-tea starts with “t”, like Tilly!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the first sound in a word._

_You lift the cover. A girl in big wellies looks up at you._  
_She has a sash with lots of home-made badges._  
**Tilly**: I’m a stowaway. You can’t send a stowaway home. That’s the rule.  
**Nova**: A stowaway is someone who hides, to come on a trip.  

#### `s24-badges`

**Tilly**: I collect badges. I make them myself.  
**Tilly**: Knots. Brave at the Dentist. Goose Handling. And an old tin one.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 4  
> _(Count Tilly’s badges on your fingers: one, two, three, four.)_  
> **Tilly**: Four! One more makes five.  
> Check: `edu:count:badges:4`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted four badges and heard that one more makes five._

**Tilly**: I need one more: Helping Someone Sad.  
**Tilly**: Somebody here is sad. I can tell.  
_Tilly looks at Nova. Nova looks hard at the cart wheel._  
_In the top of Tilly’s welly is a folded note._  

**Choices**

- **Say nothing about the note.**
    - You say nothing. Tilly stands up a bit taller.
    - **Tilly**: You can come on my adventure. You’re allowed.
- **Ask her if she’s all right.**
    - **Tilly**: I’m FINE. The note is from Gran Hazel.
    - **Tilly**: It isn’t for opening. It’s for keeping.
    - She pats her welly, once. Some things are private, and that’s OK.

#### `s24-pack`

_The cart stops by the waterfall. Whoosh! Splash!_  
_Nova lifts her bag. One, two, three tries! Clank!_  
**You**: What’s in your bag?  
**Nova**: Things Gran Hazel gave me. A tin cup. A long scarf.  
**Nova**: Postcards. And a shell. I can’t leave any of them.  
_You offer to help carry it. Nova says no._  
**Nova**: I’ll manage. I always manage.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): letting friends help  
> _Nova’s bag is too heavy. But she says no to help._  
> _Is it OK to let a friend help?_  
> - ✅ Yes, friends can help → Yes. Letting a friend help is fine. It makes heavy things lighter.
> - No, do it alone → Some things are too heavy for one. Friends can help, and that’s OK.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about letting friends help with something hard._

_Your Nubbin runs ahead up the stones. Then runs back. Come on!_  

#### `s24-falls-top`

_The path goes up beside the waterfall. Roar! Splash!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): water flows downhill  
> _Which way does the waterfall water go: up or down?_  
> - ✅ Down → Yes! Water always runs downhill. Whoosh!
> - Up → Watch it fall: down, down, down. Water always runs downhill.
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that water flows downhill._

_The stones are wet and wobbly._  
_Your Nubbin finds the dry stones. Run up, wait, run back!_  
_At the top, Tilly climbs a big rock and throws out her arms._  
**Tilly**: The whole world’s up here! The WHOLE WORLD!  
**Nova**: …It is, isn’t it.  
_Nova is very puffed. But she is smiling, just a little._  

#### `s24-bird`

_Up high, you walk into a cloud. It is misty and cool._  
_You put Fern’s five buns on a rock. Then there are fewer!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): take away 1 from 5  
> _Fern packed five buns. Someone took one! How many are left?_  
> - ✅ Four → Yes! Five take away one is four. (Count four fingers.)
> - Six → Six would be one more. Somebody took one away! Hold up five fingers. Fold one down. Four!
> _For grown-ups: Your child took one away from five with fingers._

_A huge bird stands in the mist, with a bun in its beak._  
_It is taller than Nova. It has stripes and long, long legs._  
**Tilly**: Oh! He’s beautiful! He’s called Sir Reginald.  
_Gulp! The bun goes all the way down._  
_The bird steps forward, then back. It is shy._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-3, notice): shy animals need slow, quiet people  
> _Shy animals get scared by fast moves and loud noises._  
> _Slow and quiet helps them feel safe._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how to be gentle around a shy wild animal._

**Nova**: Don’t grab. Be gentle and slow.  

**Choices**

- **Hold out a bun on your flat hand.**
    - You hold out a bun on your flat hand. Very, very still.
    - The bird takes it, so gently. Peck!
    - Then it follows you a few steps. Is there another one?
- **Sit down and wait for it to come to you.**
    - You sit down on the path. Your Nubbin sits too. Very still.
    - At last the bird comes close. It looks at your Nubbin.
    - It tidies the fur on your Nubbin’s head with its beak!

#### `s24-bird-done`

_Then a call comes from higher up: creak-creak!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): birds call to each other  
> _(Make the bird’s call together: creak-creak!)_  
> _Birds call to tell each other, “Here I am!”_  
> Check: `edu:copy:sound:creak-creak`
> _For grown-ups: Your child copied a bird call and heard that birds call to each other._

_The bird turns its head. Off it runs, into the cloud._  
**Tilly**: He’s got somewhere to be. You can tell.  
**Nova**: That bird… I’ve seen it in old pictures!  

#### `s24-tracks`

_The bird left footprints in the mud. Big ones!_  
_Each footprint has three toes._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, do): more and fewer: 3 and 5  
> _(Count the toes on the footprint: one, two, three.)_  
> _(Now count the toes on one of your feet. Five!)_  
> _Five is more than three._  
> Check: `edu:count:toes:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared three and five: five is more._

_Your Nubbin runs to each footprint and sits in it. Here! Here!_  
**Tilly**: We’re tracking him! That’s another badge!  
_Up in the cloud there is a tent. Flags flap on a line._  
_An old, old lady holds a telescope. Her hat has a feather._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: Stand still! STILL! You’ll scare it away!  

#### `s24-camp`

**Augusta Fairweather**: My name is Augusta Fairweather. I am an explorer.  
_An explorer goes to new places, to find out about them._  
**Nova**: THE Fairweather? From the old picture papers?  
**Nova**: Gran Hazel had your picture on her wall when she was a girl!  
_Fairweather sees the old tin badge on Tilly’s sash._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: That’s one of my badges! Where did you get it?  
**Tilly**: From Gran Hazel. It was hers when she was little.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.His.2.K-2, notice): things from long ago get passed down  
> _Long, long ago, little Gran Hazel wore that badge._  
> _Now Tilly wears it. Old things can be passed down, and carry stories._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how an old thing can be passed down from long ago._

**Nova**: Gran Hazel wrote her list because of you.  

#### `s24-cage`

**Augusta Fairweather**: Long ago I saw that bird. Nobody believed me.  
**Augusta Fairweather**: They called me a fibber. So I came up here, to prove it.  
_Behind the tent is a big cage. It is for the bird._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: One night in a cage, in the village. Then everyone will believe me.  
**Tilly**: You can’t put Sir Reginald in a CAGE!  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-1, ask): wild animals need their own homes  
> _Where is the best home for a wild bird?_  
> - ✅ Outside, where it lives → Yes. Wild birds need space, their own food, and their families.
> - In a cage → A cage is too small for a wild bird. It needs to be free, near its family.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that wild animals need to live free in their own homes._

**Augusta Fairweather**: The bird drinks at the spring when it gets dark. I’ll be ready.  

**Choices**

- **Say the bird doesn’t have to prove anything.**
    - **Augusta Fairweather**: Nor did I. But they wanted proof anyway.
- **Say nothing. Sit down near the cage.**
    - You sit down. Fairweather looks very, very tired.

#### `s24-net`

_Evening comes. The sky goes dark blue._  
_The bird comes to the spring to drink._  
_Whoosh! Fairweather throws her net over it!_  
_The bird runs off up the hill, pulling the net and Fairweather too._  
_Tilly runs after them, fast!_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): staying close to your grown-up  
> _Tilly ran off in the dark. On a walk, what should children do?_  
> - ✅ Stay close to their grown-up → Yes. Staying close keeps everyone safe, and nobody gets lost.
> - Run off on their own → Running off can mean getting lost. Stay close to your grown-up.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about staying close to a grown-up on walks._

_Nova’s big bag slides on the wet rock. She grabs it with both arms._  
_When she looks up, Tilly has gone into the dark._  
_Don’t worry. Tilly is safe. You’ll find her soon._  

#### `s24-rock`

_Nova sits on a rock, hugging her bag. She doesn’t move._  
_Your Nubbin runs up the hill into the dark. It comes back, calm._  
_Then, from up the hill: a whistle! Wheee-oo!_  
**Tilly**: I’M FINE! I’M WITH SIR REGINALD! DON’T COME, YOU’LL SCARE HIM!  
**Nova**: She’s all right. Oh, she’s all right.  
**Nova**: I grabbed my bag, not Tilly. I feel awful.  
_It’s too dark to climb now. You can still hear Tilly whistling._  
_You sit down next to Nova. You don’t talk. You just wait._  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): being with someone who feels awful  
> _Nova feels awful. What can a friend do?_  
> - ✅ Sit with her, and wait → Yes. Sitting close says, “I’m here.” You don’t need words.
> - Tell her to cheer up → Cheering up takes time. Sitting close, and waiting, helps more.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that sitting quietly with someone can comfort them._


#### `s24-light`

_You light Gran Hazel’s lantern. It glows, small and gold._  
_Now you can see the rock, and the bag, and Nova’s face._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-2, ask): we see things when there is light  
> _Why can we see Nova’s face now?_  
> - ✅ The lantern makes light → Yes! We need light to see. In the dark, we can’t see much.
> - Nova got bigger → Nova is the same size! The lantern makes light, so now we can see.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that we need light to see things._

_You put the lantern in Nova’s hands._  
_She puts the bag down, so she can hold the lantern properly._  
**Nova**: I’ll read Gran Hazel’s list. It always helps.  
_She opens the book from the back, by mistake._  

#### `s24-back-pages`

_The back pages have writing too. Gran Hazel’s writing!_  
**Nova**: I never looked at the back. Not once.  
**Nova**: “Nova came. Tea? No, thank you.”  
**Nova**: “Nova came. She brought a shell. Tea? No, thank you.”  
_Gran Hazel wrote down every visit. Every single one._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, notice): a diary keeps memories  
> _A book where you write about your days is called a diary._  
> _Gran Hazel’s diary kept every one of Nova’s visits safe._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard what a diary is, and how writing keeps memories._

**Nova**: “Sunny. Moss sat on my foot all day. Best day this year.”  
_The very last page has one more line, just for Nova._  
**Gran Hazel**: “Thank you for all of it. Now go and find somewhere new!”  
_Nova cries and cries. That’s all right. Crying lets sad feelings out._  
_Your Nubbin leans on her. You stay close._  

#### `s24-crying`

_Your Nubbin climbs into Nova’s lap and leans on her._  
_You keep still. Nobody hurries._  
_Up the hill, Tilly whistles now and then. Wheee-oo!_  
_Nova cries for a long time. Then a little longer._  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): naming feelings: sad and missing someone  
> _Nova is crying. How does she feel?_  
> - ✅ Sad. She misses Gran Hazel. → Yes. Missing someone can make us cry. A hug and a friend close by help.
> - Cross → Not cross. She is sad. She misses Gran Hazel. A friend helps.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named sadness and missing someone, and what helps._

**Nova**: She wrote it down. Every time. She did love me.  
_Nova wipes her face. She feels a tiny bit better._  

#### `s24-cairn`

_Nova opens her big bag. She takes things out, one at a time._  
_A tin cup. A long scarf. A shell. Lots of postcards._  
**Nova**: Gran Hazel didn’t want me carrying her things everywhere.  
**Nova**: I’ll leave them here, in a pile of stones. A cairn.  
_You build it together. Big flat stones at the bottom._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-2-ETS1-1, ask): building a steady pile: big flat stones at the bottom  
> _Which stones go at the bottom: big flat ones, or small round ones?_  
> - ✅ Big flat ones → Yes! Big flat stones make a strong base. Round ones would roll.
> - Small round ones → Round ones roll away! Big flat stones at the bottom keep the pile steady.
> _For grown-ups: Your child worked out how to build a steady pile._

**Nova**: Gran Hazel would say I was being dramatic.  
**You**: She would!  
_Nova laughs. Then she holds the last two things: the cup and the scarf._  

**Choices**

- **“Keep the tin cup.”**
    - **Nova**: For tea. I’ll say yes to tea now.
- **“Keep the scarf.”**
    - **Nova**: It’s too long! It was always too long.
    - She winds it round her neck. There’s still lots left over.
- **“Leave them both. Keep the book.”**
    - **Nova**: Just the book. It has her words in it.

#### `s24-cairn-done`

_Now the bag is empty. Nova lifts it with one finger!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, do): heavy and light  
> _(Can you lift a sock with one finger? Now try a big book!)_  
> _Light things are easy to lift. Heavy things are hard._  
> Check: `edu:lift:finger:light`
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared lifting something light and something heavy._

**Nova**: Oh! That’s much better!  
_Up the hill, Tilly whistles again. She sounds fed up._  
**Nova**: Come on. Let’s go and find Tilly.  

#### `s24-whistle`

_You walk up the hill. You hold the lantern low, to light the path._  
_Your Nubbin runs ahead, and back, and ahead again._  
_Nova looks back at the pile of stones. It’s still there._  
**Nova**: It’s just stones. I know it’s just stones.  
_Wheee-oo! The whistle is louder now._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): sounds get louder as you get closer  
> _The whistle sounds louder. Are we closer to Tilly, or further away?_  
> - ✅ Closer → Yes! Sounds get louder as we get closer.
> - Further away → Far-away sounds are quiet. Louder means closer!
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that sounds get louder as you get closer._

**Tilly**: I CAN SEE YOUR LANTERN! YOU’RE GOING THE LONG WAY!  

#### `s24-nest-fed`

_Under a rocky roof there is a big, big nest._  
_In the nest are four fluffy, stripy chicks!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 4  
> _(Count the chicks together: one, two, three, four.)_  
> **Tilly**: Four! I counted them loads of times.  
> Check: `edu:count:chicks:4`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted four chicks._

_Next to the nest sits Tilly. She is safe, and very cross._  
_Her badge sash is round the smallest chick, to keep it warm._  
**Tilly**: You took AGES! Sir Reginald is a mummy. She can still be Sir.  
_The cage is open and empty. Far off, a bird calls: creak-creak!_  
_You remember the bun on your hand. You make a trail of buns._  
_Peck, peck, peck! The mother bird eats her way home._  
_She stands over her chicks and looks at you. She remembers!_  
**Nova**: There. She came straight home.  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-LS1-2, notice): parent birds keep their chicks warm and fed  
> _Mother birds sit over their chicks to keep them warm._  
> _Chicks need their mums. That’s why wild birds must stay free._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how bird parents look after their chicks._


#### `s24-edge`

_Over by the rocks, someone says, “Could anyone help, please?”_  
_It is Fairweather! Her boot is stuck in her own net._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: Don’t hurry. Hurrying is how you get stuck in nets.  
_You and Nova kneel down beside her._  
_You put the lantern down, so you can see the knots._  
**Nova**: One loop at a time. Slowly.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, do): calming down with slow breaths  
> _(Take a slow breath with Nova. In… and out.)_  
> _Slow hands untangle knots. Fast hands make more!_  
> Check: `edu:breathe:slow:untangle`
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised slow breathing to stay calm and careful._


#### `s24-proof`

_Nobody hurries. Your Nubbin sits on the net. The last loop comes free!_  
_Fairweather is very tired. She sits against the rock._  
_So you all sit, and wait. Then the mother bird walks past her. Close!_  
_Fairweather lifts her hand. Then she puts it back down. She doesn’t touch._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-3, ask): look at wild animals, but don’t touch  
> _The wild bird walks close. Should Fairweather grab her?_  
> - ✅ No, just look → Yes. With wild animals, we look, and keep our hands to ourselves.
> - Yes, grab her → Grabbing would scare her. With wild animals, we just look.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned to look at wild animals without touching._

**Augusta Fairweather**: That’ll do. I saw her. That’s enough for me.  
_She gives you an old stripy feather from her hat._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: Go and see your sunrise. I’ll mind the chicks.  

#### `s24-shelf`

_The Sunrise Shelf is a flat, high rock. Below it is the whole valley._  
_It is still dark. Everything is blue and sleepy._  
**Tilly**: Is this it? It’s just dark!  
**Nova**: Wait. That’s the trick. You wait.  
_Nova gives you Gran Hazel’s lantern._  
**Nova**: You do it. She’d want it to be you.  
_Look! The sky is going pale on one side._  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Geo.2.K-2, ask): the sun comes up in the east  
> **Nova**: The sun comes up on the same side every morning.  
> **Nova**: That side has a name. Do you know it?  
> - ✅ East → Yes! East. The sun comes up in the east, every single day.
> - Up → Good guess! That side is called east. The sun comes up in the east.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that the sun comes up in the east._


#### `s24-lights`

_The sun peeps over the hills. The light runs down into the valley._  
_First the tall rock called the Tooth. Then the roofs. Then the waterfall._  
_Then the tide pools, flash! Then the Grove: the pond, the cottage, the hill._  
**Tilly**: Oh!  
**Nova**: There. The whole valley, all at once.  
_Down in the Grove, four little lights shine, one on each post._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 4, even when things look small  
> _(Point and count the tiny lights: one, two, three, four.)_  
> **Nova**: Four. All lit. Good.  
> Check: `edu:count:posts:4`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted four lights, even far away._

_You hold Gran Hazel’s lantern up high. Nova helps you hold it._  

#### `s24-tick`

_Nova sits on the flat rock with the book and Gran Hazel’s tiny pencil._  
_She makes a tick by the last line. A very small tick._  
**Nova**: Take someone. Done! I took two, and a stowaway!  
_She pours tea into Gran Hazel’s tin cup. She drinks half._  
**Nova**: Tea. Yes, please. You have the rest.  
_Nova ties the pencil on a string. She hangs it round Tilly’s neck._  
**Nova**: Your new badge: Helping Someone Sad. You earned it.  
**Tilly**: It’s my best badge ever.  
_Nova opens the very last page. It is empty. She gives you the book._  
**Nova**: Your turn.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, SL.K.1, do): talking about places you would like to go  
> _(What would go on YOUR list of places? Take turns to say one.)_  
> **Nova**: Good one. Write it down, so nobody stops you.  
> Check: `edu:tell:place:my-list`
> _For grown-ups: Your child took turns to talk about a place they would like to visit._


**Choices**

- **Write: “The Sunrise Shelf, again. Take someone.”**
    - **Nova**: Good. Somebody should keep coming up here.
- **Write: “Wherever Nova goes next. Tea after.”**
    - **Nova**: Then come and find me. I’ll put the kettle on!
- **Write: “Here. Every morning.”**
    - **Nova**: That’s the hardest one. Gran Hazel did it for forty years.

#### `s24-written`

_You write your line with the tiny pencil._  
_Tilly makes you a badge too, from a bit of cereal box._  
_It says: HELPING SOMEONE SAD._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): the letter H  
> _“Helping” starts with H. H-h-helping!_  
> _(Draw an H in the air: down, down, across.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:letter:h`
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard the first sound of a word and drew the letter H._

**Tilly**: You were all right. For a grown-up.  
_Under your line there’s lots of room. Room for lots more places!_  

#### `s24-home`

_You walk back down the hill, slowly._  
_Fairweather comes too. She holds Nova’s arm._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: Fifty years up there. I hope somebody kept my room!  
_That night you light Gran Hazel’s lantern and open the book._  
_Look! Little notes in pencil, on every page. Gran Hazel wrote them!_  
_She read all of Nova’s stories, and wrote what she thought._  
_Nova stays three days. She says yes to tea every time._  
_Then off she goes. Her bag is so light now!_  
_At the top of the hill she waves. Goodbye, Nova! See you soon!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, ask): retelling a story in order: first and last  
> _What happened first, on this big adventure?_  
> - ✅ Tea on the hill → Yes! First tea, then the cart, the bird, the lantern… and last, the sunrise.
> - The sunrise → The sunrise came last! First was tea on the hill, with Nova.
> _For grown-ups: Your child retold what happened first and last in the story._


## S25. Real

- `s25-step1`: Open the soft parcel in the loft. · _It is on the trunk, under the little window. Pull the string._
- `s25-step2`: Help Pixel mend the loft lamp. · _Hold the lamp still. Pass Pixel the small cog._
- `s25-step3`: Sit on the window seat until Moss has finished. · _Sit still and wait. Moss is slow._
- `s25-step4`: Give your Nubbin something soft to hold. · _Put the knitted toy in its basket._
- `s25-step5`: Find the toy before bedtime. · _Take a lantern. Look by the pond._
- `s25-step6`: Take the toy to Fern’s for tea. · _Your Nubbin will carry him in._
- `s25-step7`: Let the wild Nubbins look at the toy. · _Stand by Moss and keep very still._
- `s25-step8`: Find the right page in Gran Hazel’s book. · _Look for the page with the old horse._
- `s25-step9`: Hold the toy while Clover mends him. · _Hold him still. Clover is quick._
- `s25-step10`: Sit with your Nubbin all night. · _Stay by the basket. Give it water when it wakes._
- `s25-step11`: Carry the rag bundle to the shed. · _Put it by the shed door._
- `s25-step12`: Wait until morning, then go and look. · _Stay at the window seat until it is light._
- `s25-step13`: Tell Moss about the warm water. · _Sit by his ledge. He will listen._

#### `s25-loft`

_Pitter-patter. Warm rain on the roof._  
_The lamp in the loft is broken. Pixel will mend it after lunch._  
_You climb the ladder. Up, up, up!_  
_The loft smells of apples and dust._  
_Your Nubbin climbs up too. Atchoo! Dusty!_  
_Under the little window is a big box, called a trunk._  
_On the trunk is a soft parcel, tied with string._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): position words: on and under  
> _Where is the parcel? (Wait for an answer.)_  
> - ✅ On the trunk → Yes! The parcel is ON the trunk, UNDER the window.
> - Under the bed → Let’s look again. The parcel is ON the trunk.
> - In the pond → Splash! Not in the pond. It’s ON the trunk, nice and dry.
> _For grown-ups: Practised the position words “on” and “under”._

_Gran Hazel wrote on it, a long time ago: “For when they come back.”_  

#### `s25-name`

_You pull the string. The bow falls open._  
_Inside is a knitted Nubbin, soft and lumpy._  
_One button eye is tight. One hangs on a long thread._  
_There is a thin book too, with a rabbit on it._  
_You remember! Gran Hazel made this toy for you._  
_You were three. You took it everywhere._  
_What did you call it? Can you remember?_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, do): first sounds: b  
> _Button and Bobble start with the same sound. B, b, b!_  
> _(Say “b, b, Button” and “b, b, Bobble” together.)_  
> Check: `edu:say:first-sound:b`
> _For grown-ups: Heard and said the first sound “b” in Button and Bobble._


**Choices**

- **“Button.” For its eye.**
    - Button! Your Nubbin looks up, then goes back to a hatbox.
- **“Bobble.” For the bobble on top.**
    - Bobble! Your Nubbin looks up, then goes back to a hatbox.
- **“Mister.” Just Mister.**
    - Mister! A very polite name. Your Nubbin looks up, then back at a hatbox.

#### `s25-lamp`

_After lunch, Pixel climbs up with her toolbox._  
**Pixel**: Hello! The lamp is tired. I brought some helpers.  
_Sparks and Fern climb up to watch._  
_Out come Pixel’s clockwork things._  
_A little moth. A tiny fan. A turning cog._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 3  
> _How many clockwork things? (Point and count together.)_  
> _Moth, one. Fan, two. Cog, three. Three!_  
> Check: `edu:count:clockwork:3`
> _For grown-ups: Counted three things, pointing at each one._

**Sparks**: Ooh! Does any of it go bang?  
**Pixel**: Not on purpose.  
_Whirr! The moth flies round and lands on Fern’s head._  
_Your Nubbin chases the shiny flashes. Squeak! Squeak!_  
_The knitted toy sits on the trunk. Nobody looks at it._  

#### `s25-sill`

_Click! The lamp comes on. Tick, tick, tick._  
_Everyone goes down the ladder, talking about the moth._  
_You put the toy and the book on the window seat._  
_One week goes by. Then two weeks._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 7 (the days in a week)  
> _One week is seven days. Let’s count them!_  
> _(Count to seven together on your fingers.)_  
> _One, two, three, four, five, six, seven!_  
> Check: `edu:count:days:7`
> _For grown-ups: Counted to seven, the number of days in a week._

_The toy sits on the windowsill, in the sun._  
_Nobody plays with it._  
_Far away on the hill, a little grey one is looking this way._  

#### `s25-real`

_One morning, Moss comes all the way down the hill._  
_He sits on the ledge outside the window. The toy sits inside._  
_It is a long walk for Moss. He is very old and slow._  
**Moss**: Don’t say anything. I’m not here.  
_You sit on the window seat and wait. Quietly._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, do): waiting quietly  
> _Waiting quietly is hard. Let’s try it._  
> _(Sit very still together and count to five in a whisper.)_  
> Check: `edu:sit:still:5`
> _For grown-ups: Practised sitting still and waiting quietly._

**Moss**: Hazel knitted that toy in one week.  
**Moss**: She read you that book on this seat. I listened too.  
_Then Moss talks to the toy, not to you._  
**Moss**: Real isn’t in your stitches. It grows on you, slowly.  
**Moss**: Somebody has to love you for ages. Properly. That’s how it starts.  

**Choices**

- **Ask Moss: does it hurt?**
    - **You**: Does it hurt?
    - Moss answers the toy, not you.
- **Ask Moss: does it happen quickly?**
    - **You**: Does it happen quickly?
    - Moss answers the toy, not you.
- **Say nothing. Just sit with them.**
    - You say nothing. Moss nods, very slowly, and goes on.

#### `s25-hurt`

**Moss**: Sometimes it hurts a little. But a Real one isn’t so scared of that.  
**Moss**: It doesn’t happen quickly. It’s slow, like a tree growing tall.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): some things grow slowly  
> _Moss says being Real is slow. What else grows slowly?_  
> - ✅ A tree → Yes! A tree grows a tiny bit every year. Slowly, slowly.
> - A splash → A splash is very quick! A tree grows slowly.
> - A sneeze → Atchoo! A sneeze is quick. A tree grows slowly.
> _For grown-ups: Compared fast and slow changes: a tree grows slowly._

**Moss**: It’s harder for someone with sharp edges.  
**Moss**: Or someone kept on a high shelf, where nobody can hug them.  
_Then Moss climbs down, one slow foot at a time._  
_He walks back to his hill. It will take him all day._  
**Moss**: Your toy has a good face. Don’t keep him on a shelf.  

#### `s25-storm`

_Later that week, Sable walks past the window._  
_She stops. She looks at the toy for a long time._  
_Moss said sharp edges make it hard. Sable has sharp edges._  
_She lies down near the toy and goes to sleep. She doesn’t take it._  
_Then, one night, a big storm comes. Boom! Crash!_  
_Your Nubbin can’t settle. Its ears are flat._  
_It is scared of the loud bangs._  
_Flash! The lightning lights up the toy on the windowsill._  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): what helps when you feel scared  
> _Your Nubbin is scared of the storm. What could help it?_  
> - ✅ Something soft to hold → Yes! Something soft and cosy to hug can help us feel safe.
> - Being told to stop it → Being told off doesn’t help a scared feeling. Something soft to hug helps more.
> - Being left all alone → Being alone can feel scarier. A hug, or something soft to hold, helps more.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about what helps when someone feels scared: comfort, and something soft to hold._


#### `s25-sleeps`

_You put the toy in your Nubbin’s basket._  
_Your Nubbin climbs in. It curls all the way round the toy._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, ask): name a shape: circle  
> _Your Nubbin curls up round the toy. What shape does it make?_  
> - ✅ A circle → Yes! Curled up round and round, like a circle.
> - A square → A square has four corners. Your Nubbin has no corners! It’s a circle.
> - A triangle → A triangle has three points. A curled-up Nubbin is round, like a circle.
> _For grown-ups: Named a circle in a curled-up shape._

_Boom! Thunder! Your Nubbin doesn’t even wake up._  
_In the morning, it is still holding the toy._  
_From now on, the toy belongs to your Nubbin._  

#### `s25-everywhere`

_Now the toy goes everywhere._  
_First, it goes to the pond. Splash!_  
_Next, it goes to a picnic. It gets jam on it._  
_Last, it has a bath. Bubbles!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, do): order events: first, next, last  
> _Where did the toy go first? Next? Last?_  
> _(Put the pictures in order: pond, picnic, bath.)_  
> Check: `edu:order:toy-trip`
> _For grown-ups: Put three events in order using first, next and last._

_Then, one evening, the toy is missing!_  
_Your Nubbin stands up in its basket. It won’t lie down._  
_The toy is out in the Grove. It’s getting dark._  

#### `s25-lantern`

_You take a lantern. Its light shines in the dark._  
_There! The toy is in the wet grass by the pond._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-2, ask): we need light to see  
> _It is dark outside. What helped you find the toy?_  
> - ✅ The lantern’s light → Yes! We need light to see. The lantern lit up the grass.
> - Closing my eyes → With eyes shut, we can’t see at all! The lantern’s light helped.
